Architecture review · digitalbrain at a275f82 · 10 November 2026

The kernel does not own its own protocol

A read-only audit of digitalbrain (50,250 source lines, 20 projects) and of how os uses it. It names the main source of complexity and gives 14 refactoring proposals, each with the code as it stands and the code it should become.

240 commits in 13 days94 fix commits37,734 test linesnothing was built or run

The core package describes how to host an actor. It does not describe what a brain does.

The rule that makes DigitalBrain what it is has four parts: write a neuron's state, its signals and the reader's cursor as one journal entry; tell subscribers; let each subscriber catch up from its cursor; decide who may call. That rule has no home in the core. It sits inside each runtime's adapter, so it is written twice: once in DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans and once in the TestOS simulator. The two copies disagree in at least 16 places, and the simulator is what os uses to approve AI-written units before they deploy.

The second cause is a habit. Each new diagnosis added a mechanism beside the old ones and none was taken out. The largest case is the two-phase gate: 1,074 source lines that every durable write passes through, no production user in either repo, and 23 of the 94 fix commits.

The finding

One problem at three depths

1. The investment is inverted

In DDD terms an actor runtime is a generic subdomain. Orleans already provides it. The journal protocol is the core domain: nobody else has it.

The core package spends its surface on the generic part. It restates the grain model in neutral words: Neuron, an INeuronContext with 12 properties and 5 methods, eight scheduling attributes that mirror the Orleans ones, and a string-plus-JSON call (RuntimeInvocation). The core part, the commit and delivery protocol, is left to whoever implements INeuronState.Save. One line of interface hides about 320 lines of protocol on Orleans and about 190 in the simulator.

2. Addition without deletion

The history shows eleven dated diagnoses, each followed by a new mechanism, and no model removed. Fix commits are 39% of all subjects and refactor commits 2%. Delivery alone was designed three times in twelve days; each design left parts of the previous one in place, so comments, docs and tests describe machinery that is gone or was never wired.

3. Boundaries follow folders

About 80 of 178 core files share one flat namespace. The sandbox boundary is two hand-kept lists that sort roughly 170 public types into allowed and refused. The App base class lives in the framework and its only host lives in os. The generic operations runtime names storage plan types. Identity rules exist three times.

What the finding is not

  • Primitive obsession. The a275f82 pass fixed that at the leaves. None of the 16 runtime disagreements and none of the 23 gate fixes involve an id type.
  • Reinventing Orleans, as the largest cost. Your earlier brief put this first. The call tunnel is a real restatement of Orleans: 12 steps, 6 JSON passes and 2 to 3 method binds for one in-silo call. It is also 1,142 lines (2.3% of the source) and 6 of 94 fix commits. It needs one owner (proposal R06), and a measurement before anyone replaces it. Orleans is misused in a narrower place: persistent streams, a PubSubStore and a queue are used to wake grains in the same process (R05).
  • Four rival programming models. On the silo there is one base class. Flow, RevisionedNeuron and the unit host all derive from Neuron<TState> and reuse its save, turns and delivery. Production code has 150 neuron classes, 30 apps, 10 units and 6 flows. The one outlier is App, which rebuilt state, turns and identity outside the actor system (R10).

The simulator is a product feature and stays. os calls UnitScenario from production code before a generated unit deploys. That is why the disagreement between the two runtimes matters more than its size in lines: a unit can pass the gate on rules that production does not follow. The owner's own note from the last live start says the same thing from the other side: three defects that the unit and integration tiers did not see.

Today and target

Move the cut below the protocol

Nothing an author writes changes. The line between core and runtime moves down, so a runtime supplies storage, a wake-up call, a clock and a transport, and no longer supplies behaviour.

Today

CoreA neutral actor surface: Neuron, INeuronContext, scheduling attributes, call envelope. Protocol hidden behind Save(next, signals).
Runtime.OrleansCommit saga: gates, append, announce, commit. Delivery engine, cursors, stream doorbell. Rules: exempt signals, [On] lookup, interleaving, retention. Access filter that only os registers.HostNeuronState.cs:32-57 · SynapseEngine.cs · GateRunner.cs
Testing (TestOS)The commit saga again in a different order and with no version check. Delivery again. The same rules again. No access check.TestNeuronContext.cs:96-110 · TestSynapses.cs · TestGates.cs

Target

Core · AuthoringThe published language: signals, ids, state records, unit functions, rules, contracts. The only namespace sandboxed code may see.
Core · Kernel, written onceCommit over a journal aggregate. Subscriptions with one position. One delivery loop. One call catalog. One access decision.namespace DigitalBrain.Kernel · R02 to R07
Orleans adaptersJournal file engine, one-way nudge, timers and reminders, call transport, placement.
Simulation adaptersMemory journal, direct nudge, virtual clock. Runs the same kernel code as production.

The decision

Three target architectures were argued and scored

Each option had an advocate who measured what it deletes and wrote its migration. A judge re-checked their numbers against the repo. Scores are 0 to 10; a higher safety score means a safer migration.

OptionComplexity removedSafetyFits real needsEffect on osVerdict
A · keep neutral fix the cut5898Right cut, lowest risk, all four real requirements kept, os untouched in steps 1-8. But as written it moves the gate saga, the stream path and the ambient cursor into the core behind five new ports instead of removing them, so src stays line-neutral (-825 est.). Used as the base of the recommended hybrid.
B · orleans native7253Largest deletion (-5,570 est.) and the only full answer to thesis part (a), but 4,942 of its 7,638 deleted lines are the simulator, a real requirement it must rebuild on a silo with Quiesce and Advance that do not exist and a start cost nobody measured. Four releases, two os passes, 125 neuron files, 143 contracts, 97 test files and a persisted GrainId change, for a tunnel that draws 6 of 94 fix commits. Rejected as the target; four small parts grafted.
C · brain as aggregate7477Best statement of what the kernel is (one Commit, subscriptions in the journal, one position, no stream) and it deletes real mechanisms. Its two option-specific steps (BrainGrain owns the brain; pure unit folds inside the brain turn) put every tenant write and untrusted code behind one grain turn for a multi-silo hole no shipped topology can hit. Steps 0-3 and 7 are grafted; steps 4 and 6 are not; step 5 is replaced by deleting gates.
Recommended hybrid: A's cut + C's journal kernel + gate deletion8698About the same net deletion as B with two cascades instead of four, no author API change, the simulator kept and made to run the same commit and delivery code as production. Risk concentrates in one move (subscriptions and cursor conversion), which must be rehearsed on a copy of real brain storage.

The panel's line estimates in the verdict column predate the detailed designs. The reviewed figures are under Order of work.

Requirements that are real and are kept

  • Units compiled at run time in a sandbox. Orleans cannot add grain classes to a running silo, so a generic late-bound host stays.
  • State and signals durable together, with one order per brain and a trace. Orleans has no atomic write across grain state and a stream.
  • A fast deterministic simulator, because os uses it as a deploy gate.
  • Tenant isolation.

What was rejected and why

  • Typed Orleans grains for every neuron. The largest deletion on paper, but 4,942 of its 7,638 deleted lines are the simulator, which would have to be rebuilt on a silo. It touches 125 neuron files, 143 contracts and 97 test files in os for a tunnel that draws 6 of 94 fixes.
  • The brain as a single grain. It puts every tenant write and untrusted unit code behind one turn, to close a multi-silo gap no shipped topology can reach.

Order of work

Two release cascades for the kernel, then the contexts one at a time

Deletion comes before extraction, so nothing dead is moved into the core. The moves below carry the figures from the reviewed proposals, which are lower than the panel's first estimate.

Expect fewer copies more than fewer lines. The architecture panel estimated about 4,900 fewer source lines for the kernel wave. The reviewed proposals add up to about 2,500 fewer source lines (about 3,700 if the out-of-process client and edge are dropped) and about 2,000 fewer test lines. Three moves are close to line-neutral or grow: one commit path (R04, about +285), subscriptions and delivery (R05, about −65) and the call catalog (R06, −61). Their gain is that each rule exists once and the simulator runs the code production runs.

By the proposals' own estimates the kernel wave is about 11 working weeks for one developer with an AI pair, and the second wave 9 to 10.

  1. Pin and measure. Add facts for commit order, cursor atomicity and retention. Add a differential fact that runs shipped units on both runtimes and compares journals. Check in the HostCostFacts numbers. Count gate synapses installed in production brains.digitalbrain · tests only · about +400 test lines
  2. Delete what has no production reference. R01digitalbrain · −965 src measured (−2,184 with client and edge) · about −2,100 tests · os −254
  3. Put the pure rules in the core once. No new port. Production behaviour wins where copies disagree. R02digitalbrain · about −50 src · about +430 tests · 2 to 3 days
  4. Delete the two-phase gate if the census is zero. R03digitalbrain · about −1,085 src · about −550 tests · 3 to 4 days
  5. One commit path over a journal aggregate. R04digitalbrain · about +285 src · about +665 tests · 6 to 8 days
  6. Subscriptions and delivery once, without streams. The riskiest move. Rehearse the cursor upgrade on a copy of real brain storage. R05digitalbrain · about −65 src · about +680 tests · about 2 weeks
  7. One call catalog and one access decision. R06, R07digitalbrain · about −410 src · os about −220 · two releases · about 3 weeks together
  8. A pure unit machine. R08digitalbrain · about −90 src · about +380 tests · 3 days, plus 3 to 4 for the rename
  9. Re-cut conformance and split out Simulation. Release 1 ships moves 2 to 9. R14digitalbrain · about −130 src · about −1,800 tests · 1 to 2 weeks
  10. One os bump. Re-pin, delete the dead kernel copies, reference Simulation from the Apps module, drop the signals queue from the Azure install, run the live Aspire start. Then delete DigitalBrain.Client.os · about −300 lines · then digitalbrain −1,005 measured
  11. Volatile consumers through one follow call. Session and listener proxy neurons (758 lines) become about 250. Release 2. This move has no detailed proposal in this report.digitalbrain then os · about 12 os files
  12. Decision point, no code. With cost numbers in hand, decide whether build-time neurons deserve a typed lane. Decide where App lives (R10).

The second wave has no fixed order. R09 (namespaces and glossary), R10 (App), R11 (Work), R12 (Identity and Connections) and R13 (unit deployment) each stand alone once the kernel wave has landed.

Proposals

The code, before and after

Before-code is copied from the repo at a275f82 with its path and line range. After-code is the target. A second agent re-opened every file, corrected the quotes and numbers, and checked the target code against the types that remain. Its notes are under each proposal. All fourteen reviewers rated their proposal medium: the quotes and counts were checked against the files, and the target code was read but never compiled.

R01 · Kernel hygiene · wave 1

Delete every type with no production reference

Delete 965 src lines and 2,387 test lines in digitalbrain that no production code in either repo reaches (2,184 and 3,014 if the edge and client package go too), 254 dead lines in os, and add one architecture fact that stops lone orphans from coming back; the unmapped storage gateway is NOT deleted, it is a 4-day-old regression the owner must decide.

Net
digitalbrain src -965 (measured; -2,184 with D7 edge+client; -2,737 only if the storage gateway feature is also dropped) / digitalbrain tests -2,387 measured +292 est. (-3,014 with D7) / os src -232 measured -22 est. / os tests about -110 est. (-112 more measured with D7) / PublicAPI.Unshipped.txt -339 lines by name pattern: core 141, Runtime.Orleans 54, Runtime.Http 46, Capabilities.Http 12, Compute 86 (+13 edge lines and the 302-line Client file with D7)
Effort
M (3-4 days): the deletions are mechanical, but there are 11 test files and 8 test regions to remove, about 340 PublicAPI lines to edit by hand, a 200-line fact baseline to annotate against os, and two os PRs of which one is a production deploy.
Depends on
nothing
Review confidence
medium

What is wrong today

Measured at digitalbrain a275f82 and os 7a28188b3: 965 lines of digitalbrain src (2.4% of 40,903) have no production reference in either repo, and 2,387 test lines (6.3% of 37,734) exist only to exercise them. The largest fossil is a second delivery engine: IBrainSignalLog + BrainSignalLog + ReactionFaultBoundary + ISignalSubscriber + NeuronState (350 lines) is registered by every host that calls AddBrainStorage (Storage/BrainStorageHosting.cs:73-79), resolved by nothing, and carries 2,145 test lines in 8 files (6.1 : 1). Dead types sit on the untrusted-unit allow-list (AllowedApis.cs:85,87,90: DeadLetterEntry, IRevisioned, NeuronState), tests such as LegacyPureTypeFacts.cs:8-27 assert only that a type name exists, and StreamIdFacts.cs:27 pins a persisted stream name against an obsolete constant nothing reads. os keeps 254 dead lines of its own, including a call sink (KernelInvocationSink instance half, 54 lines) that nothing constructs. Two groups look like fossils and are not plain deletions: the runtime edge + DigitalBrain.Client (1,219 lines; os removed its MapRuntimeEdge call in bc6cdb313 on 2026-11-08 but its Aspire E2E fixture still opens a client connection), and Runtime.Http/Storage (553 lines, added by dd02153 on 2026-11-06 while os 6b6a99a57 deleted its own copy the same day and never mapped the new one, although os Files.cs:467 still emits /brains/{brain}/storage/{connection}/items/{id}/content URLs). The earlier census was also wrong in 3 places: RevisionedNeuron.cs cannot be deleted whole (RevisionConflictException in it is used by 8 os production files), BehaviorView is live (os InProcessAppRuntime.cs:33), and the os TaskBoxing copy is live (NeuronSurface.cs:110).

Design principle

A published model must contain only concepts the domain uses: every public type is a promise to consumers and a candidate 'right way' for the next author (human or AI), so an unused second engine or base class is a competing model, not harmless weight. The target keeps one mechanism per concept and adds a fitness function: a public type must be named by code in a second production file or be listed with its reason in ConsumerSurface.txt, and a registered service interface must have a consumer.

Defects this removes

  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Storage/BrainStorageHosting.cs:73-79: a factory for a second signal log is registered with the brain store and resolved by nothing (dead DI registration).
  • src/DigitalBrain/Capabilities/CapabilityCaller.cs:119: [Obsolete("... removed in 0.13.")] sits on CapabilityCallers.AssistantApp, the id the framework itself uses at 0.18.0 to classify an Agent caller (CapabilityCaller.cs:23,71,139, each under a pragma) and that 3 os production files use (InoApp.cs:172, SecurityPostureNeuron.cs:56, ContractActions.cs:35); the attribute is wrong and is removed.
  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Synapses/Host/AllowedApis.cs:85,87,90: the allow-list for untrusted unit code names three dead types (DeadLetterEntry, IRevisioned, NeuronState); removed with the types.
  • tests/DigitalBrain.Architecture.Tests.Unit/StreamIdFacts.cs:27: the persisted stream binding name is pinned against the obsolete constant IntoChatOSNames.SignalStreams (IntoChatOSNames.cs:35), which no code reads; the runtime uses RuntimeOptions.SignalStreams (RuntimeOptions.cs:57, LocalProfile.cs:142), so changing the real default would not fail the fact. D4 points the fact at the real default.
  • tests/DigitalBrain.Architecture.Tests.Unit/ContractManifest.json:52 pins the wire alias signal.subscriber of an interface no grain implements; removed in D1.
  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Synapses/RetentionPolicy.cs:5: the doc says the floor never passes a live cursor, but SynapseRetentionHosting.cs:16 wires NullSynapseCursorLookup and the interface is internal, so no host can supply cursors. R01 corrects the doc only; a subscriber lagging beyond the history window can still lose signals until cursors live in the journal.
  • os AgentNeuron.cs:79,86: the work-continuation gate is resolved with GetService<IWorkContinuation>() and registered in neither repo, so AcquireContinuation and ReleaseContinuation always return early; the dead branch is removed.
  • os BrainHosting.cs:63: INeuronResolver is registered and consumed by nothing; removed with GrainNeuronResolver.cs.
  • os IntoChat.OS.Kernel.Tests.E2E.Aspire/OsAppHostFixture.cs:86 (fixed only with D7): the collection fixture opens a DigitalBrainConnection, whose OpenAsync posts runtime/v1/report before returning (SessionConnection.cs:63); os removed its MapRuntimeEdge call in bc6cdb313, so the fixture of all 3 Aspire E2E classes (run unfiltered by os ci.yml:204) cannot initialise. Read, not run.
  • FOUND, NOT FIXED HERE: os Files.cs:467 builds /brains/{brain}/storage/{connection}/items/{id}/content for preview and download (:181,313,386); os deleted its StorageContentEndpoints in 6b6a99a57 (2026-11-06) and never mapped the framework copy added the same day (digitalbrain dd02153); os maps only /storage/local/roots*. Read, not run.

A second delivery engine, registered with the brain store, resolved by nothing

350 src lines and 2,145 test lines (8 files) go; rg over both repos finds no consumer of Func<string, IBrainSignalLog>. It is an abandoned attempt at the decided target (one position, a notify, catch-up from a cursor) built as another engine beside SynapseEngine and TestSynapses and forcing subscribers to inherit NeuronState; the kernel replaces it. The same commit must drop the pinned alias "signal.subscriber" (tests/DigitalBrain.Architecture.Tests.Unit/ContractManifest.json:52) or RuntimeTypeFacts fails.

A dead base class that shares a file with a live exception

RevisionedNeuron<T> and IRevisioned<T> (lines 3-16 and 38-69, 46 lines) have 0 production subclasses in either repo and 1 test subclass (PlainNeuronFacts.cs:77); os throws the exception by hand in 4 neurons. The census said delete the file: that would break 8 os production files that throw or catch RevisionConflictException (SceneNeuron, WorkspaceNeuron, WorkspaceEndpoints, ViewportNeuron, ChatBinding, ModuleRouteGuard, BrainCall, CompiledAppsHost).

os: a call sink that nothing constructs

Every os hit on KernelInvocationSink is CallerKey (5 test lines), ContractOf (PlainInstanceRegistration.cs:85) or HostTypeOfLoaded (KernelGrains.cs:104, KernelTestHooks.cs:21): no new KernelInvocationSink(, no .For(, no .Register(, no DI registration. So os carries a full copy of 'how to call a neuron' (host lookup, caller stamping, interleave choice, stream access) that never runs. The two static dictionaries and their StaticCacheFacts rows (lines 15-16) are untouched; everything kept is the existing code, verbatim.

A test that keeps fossils alive becomes a fact that removes them

5 of the 13 pinned names (NeuronContractAttribute, FieldAttribute, StoredRecord, WriteResult, IStateCodec) have no production user; the test asserts only that they exist. Simulated on today's tree with the same regexes: fact 1 flags 218 of 800 public type declarations, including 26 of 42 fossil names checked (IAppHost, RevisionedNeuron, InvocationEncoder, CallerContextStamper, IntoChatOSNames, KitCoverage, RuntimeEdge, DigitalBrainConnection ...); fact 2 flags 9 of 41 registered interfaces, including IBrainSignalLog, which fact 1 cannot see. Limit: types that only name each other (NeuronState, StorageContentGateway, SessionConnection) pass. The listing is read from the repository like ContractManifest.json (nothing is copied to the output).

Risks

  • Sources stored in production journals (units and compiled apps) could name NeuronState, DeadLetterEntry, IRevisioned or call GetCapability<T>() and fail to rebuild. Not checkable from the repos; the shipped units, apps and prompts in os have 0 hits. Before the os bump, scan installed sources for the four names.
  • os AppState field removal relies on Orleans skipping removed members (orleans-facts.md:570-571) and System.Text.Json skipping unknown properties (the only UnmappedMemberHandling.Disallow in either repo is Gmail.Tools.cs:11). Read, not run: O-pre adds the read test before the fields go.
  • Storage gateway: R01 leaves 553 unmapped lines in place on purpose. If nobody takes decision 2, the os Files app keeps emitting content URLs that no route serves (read, not run).
  • D7 deletes a published package 5 days after os docs/2026-11-05-system-map.md recorded the decision to promote it; os then deleted the sandbox lane (bc6cdb313, 2026-11-08). If the owner still wants an out-of-process client, skip D7; the 1,219 + 627 lines then stay without a production dispatcher and os must map the edge again or drop the fixture connection.
  • os FrameworkBoundaryFacts.FrameworkPackagesAreExactlyTheDx03List reads the sibling digitalbrain checkout, so D7 on digitalbrain master turns it red on developer machines before any package bump.
  • NoOrphanFacts is regex-based and its listing must match exactly: about 27% of public types (218 of 800 today) are on it, so every new grain class, extension holder or host-only type costs one reviewed line. It does not see reflection by name and does not catch dead types that reference each other.
  • Removing [Obsolete] from AssistantApp leaves unnecessary #pragma warning disable CS0618 lines in os (3 production files, some tests); harmless unless IDE0079 is enforced there (not checked).
  • Overlap with other moves: GateChainKey and CatalogGateIndex belong to R03, RequestLedger/App.Key/App.Present/App.Check/BehaviorView to R10, OnBehalfOf and IObservedCaller to R07. R01 does not take them.

Your decision

1) Is an out-of-process client still a product commitment? DigitalBrain.Client (1,005 lines) and the runtime edge (214 lines, 627 test lines) were promoted into digitalbrain for the sandbox lane, which os deleted on 2026-11-08 (bc6cdb313); no production code in either repo dispatches a runtime/v1 call and the os Aspire fixture that still connects is broken by it. Default: delete both (D7). If kept, os must map the edge again. 2) Is file content over HTTP (preview, download, upload tickets) still a feature? The gateway moved to digitalbrain on 2026-11-06 and os never mapped it, while os Files.cs:467 still links to it. Default: keep the 553 lines and wire them (map StorageContentEndpoints next to JournalTailEndpoints in ModuleHttpSurface.cs:48 and register StorageContentGateway in os); that is a fix outside this move. If the feature is dropped, delete the gateway, its 412 test lines and the os URL builder together.

Doubts that remain

  • Nothing was built or run (read-only). The two 'read, not run' defects (os Aspire fixture posting to an unmapped runtime/v1/report; os Files content URLs with no mapped route) rest on grep and git pickaxe; a route mapped through a mechanism I did not find would falsify them.
  • The NoOrphanFacts numbers (218, 193, 9 services) come from a Python simulation of the same regexes; .NET regex semantics should match for these patterns but the exact baseline may differ by a few names. Whether the test project's analyzers accept an interpolated Regex.IsMatch was inferred from StreamIdFacts.cs:20 doing the same.
  • A ~200-line exact-match allow-list may be judged too heavy; fact 2 alone (8 lines) already catches the IBrainSignalLog class of defect. I kept both facts because the 56 'named by neither repo' lines are a useful review list.
  • IBrainOwnershipCounters (BrainStorageHosting.cs:65, Azure CloudProfile.cs:213) looks like a second registered-but-unresolved service: os declares and consumes its own interface of the same name. I did not trace whether os bridges them, so it is not in the proposal.
  • CS0414/IDE0052 behaviour for a write-only private field was not tested; moving GetCapability into D8 sidesteps the question.
  • os KernelInvocationSink.Contracts (kept unchanged) caches Type objects by name in a static dictionary and scans every non-dynamic assembly; if a collectible unit assembly ever declares a contract interface it would be pinned. Pre-existing, listed as justified in os StaticCacheFacts.cs:15, not changed by R01.
  • The release number (designer said 0.19.0) and whether os O-pre can ride 'any regular deploy' depend on the owner's release cascade; os currently pins 0.18.0-units.7.
  • PublicAPI line counts are by name pattern (core 141, Runtime.Orleans 54, Runtime.Http 46, Http 12, Compute 86); RS0017 at build time is the real check.
  • The wiring sketched for decision 2 (map StorageContentEndpoints in ModuleHttpSurface.cs:48 and register the gateway in os) was not designed in detail: StorageContentGateway needs a storage resolver, UploadPlanBook, ContentUploadLog and data protection, and no registration helper exists in the repo.

R02 · Kernel · wave 1

Write five pure kernel rules once in the core

Five rules that need no I/O and no port ([On] lookup, call scheduling, contract binding, retention floor, schedule diff) move from one copy per runtime into four internal core types that both runtimes call.

Net
src about -51 for steps 1-3 (244 measured out, 193 est. in) / tests about +430 est., 0 deleted / os 0. Not in that figure: step 4 about +8 (only if R03 slips), step 5 about +44, step 6 about -34. The value is not lines: two drifts and two leaks close and five rules cannot drift again.
Effort
M (2-3 days): four small core files, about 30 new facts and 14 retargeted call sites in 11 files, with no port, no persisted data and no os edit. The part that can slip is the pair of unload facts in step 1, which need a real deploy, swap and GC on both runtimes.
Depends on
nothing
Review confidence
medium

What is wrong today

Five rules exist once in DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans and once in DigitalBrain.Testing: five grep patterns (IsDefined(typeof(OnAttribute), GetCustomAttribute<InterleavesWhenAttribute>, ContractName(face) == key.Contract, Math.Min(lowestLive, held.SetEquals(wanted)) are each in exactly 2 files of src (measured). Three pairs are verbatim (contract lookup, retention formula, the 5-line schedule diff apart from ConfigureAwait). Two have drifted: the simulator's [On] filter accepts any arity (TestSynapses.cs:388; production ReactionDispatcher.cs:41-43 accepts 1 or 2 parameters), and its [InterleavesWhen] lookup cannot find a base class's private static predicate and lets a throwing predicate fail the call (TurnScheduler.cs:38,45 against NeutralCallMapping.cs:32-33,47-53). Both copies carry a comment saying they are the same rule (ReactionDispatcher.cs:9, TurnScheduler.cs:25), and ReactionDispatcher.cs, NeutralCallMapping.cs and TurnScheduler.cs each have exactly one commit (76357d8, the import), so the drift arrived with the code and nothing since compared the copies. Reading the twins also shows two leaks outside the 16 known drifts: production caches reaction lookups for collectible signal types, hits and misses, while the simulator got the guard in 6890f0f (ReactionDispatcher.cs:13,35 against TestSynapses.cs:379-380); and both runtimes key static caches by the closed StateNeuron<TGenerated> (NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:28,322; NeutralCallMapping.cs:20,30; TestNeuronHost.cs:16,185; TurnScheduler.cs:30,35), which roots a unit's load context after any IViewReader or IStateReader call, and UnitScenario.cs:134,168 makes exactly those calls on every scenario os runs in production (BuilderTools.cs:81, ScenarioTranscripts.cs:35). The leaks are verified by reading only: none of the 5 test files that assert Leaked reads a view or state first. Two more twins are left out on purpose: the 16-type un-gated list (GateRunner.cs:194-195 = TestGates.cs:52-53, changed in the same 4 commits) belongs to the gate R03 deletes, and the cause stamp (CurrentCause.cs:43-50, TestCause.cs:22-27) costs about +44 lines to unify.

Design principle

A domain rule has one home in the domain layer; an adapter translates, it does not decide. Today five decisions (what reacts, what interleaves, what binds, what may be trimmed, what a schedule change is) are taken inside each adapter, so the simulator that os uses as its deploy gate can decide differently from production. The target makes them stateless domain services in DigitalBrain.Kernel that both adapters call.

Defects this removes

  • D5, [On] filter drift: src/DigitalBrain.Testing/TestOS/TestSynapses.cs:388 accepts any arity; production src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Synapses/ReactionDispatcher.cs:41-43 accepts 1 or 2 parameters. The simulator selects a method production ignores and then throws in Invoke (TestSynapses.cs:364-366). Latent: no handler in either repo has that shape today.
  • D6, interleave predicate drift: src/DigitalBrain.Testing/TestOS/TurnScheduler.cs:38 lets a throwing predicate fail the call and :45 (FlattenHierarchy) cannot find a base's private static; production src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Hosting/NeutralCallMapping.cs:32-33 swallows and :47-53 walks the bases. The silo probe tests/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans.Tests.Integration/Hosting/HostProbes.cs:191-194 cannot be registered on TestOS.
  • New, production leak (by reading): src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Synapses/ReactionDispatcher.cs:13,35 stores (class, signal type) keys of collectible signal types in a static dictionary, including misses; the guard exists only in src/DigitalBrain.Testing/TestOS/TestSynapses.cs:379-380 (commit 6890f0f).
  • New, leak on both runtimes (by reading): static caches keyed by the closed StateNeuron<TGenerated> at src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Hosting/NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:28,322 and NeutralCallMapping.cs:20,30 (silo), src/DigitalBrain.Testing/TestOS/TestNeuronHost.cs:16,185 and TurnScheduler.cs:30,35 (TestOS) root a unit's load context after any IViewReader or IStateReader call; src/DigitalBrain.Testing/Synapses/UnitScenario.cs:134,168 makes those calls on every scenario run, which os does in production (BuilderTools.cs:81, ScenarioTranscripts.cs:35).
  • Dead code removed: the OneWay marker computed at src/DigitalBrain.Testing/TestOS/TurnScheduler.cs:30,33,63 is discarded by all three callers (TestNeuronHost.cs:49,56,64).
  • Not fixed, stated so nobody assumes it: D7 (production retention never sees a live cursor, src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Synapses/SynapseRetentionHosting.cs:28-32) needs a real cursor lookup and stays open.

[On] reaction lookup: two filters, and the cache guard on one side only

Two disagreements, two different winners. Filter: production wins (the simulator picks [On] void H(Sig s, int x) and then throws inside Invoke). Cache: the simulator wins; production is the bug, because GetOrAdd stores a (class, collectible signal type) key even on a miss. Find and Invoke stay separate because not starting a turn for an unreacted signal is a host decision. No [On] method in either repo has the affected shape today (105 occurrences in digitalbrain, 49 in os).

Call scheduling: the interleave rule that drifted, written once

Production wins on both disagreements: a throwing predicate means no interleave, and the predicate is found on any base with any visibility. The silo probe HostProbes.cs:191-194 (private static predicate on an abstract base) cannot be registered on TestOS today; each test suite uses the shape its own copy accepts. Resolve is NeutralCallMapping.cs:44-55 unchanged. The OneWay marker of the simulator copy is not carried over: all three callers discard it (TestNeuronHost.cs:49,56,64).

Contract binding: a verbatim twin with the same leak on both sides

Neither copy wins on caching. Both key a static dictionary by the neuron class, and a unit's class is StateNeuron<TRecord> closed over a record from a collectible load context. The repo already avoids exactly this pin fifty lines above (NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:94-99 skips ActivatorUtilities for StateNeuron<TRecord> for this reason). CallPolicy applies the repo's own IsCollectible idiom once, so the fix cannot land on one runtime only again.

Schedule diff (and the retention floor): verbatim twins kept in step by hand

Nothing to decide here: the only difference in 20 lines is ConfigureAwait. The retention floor is the same case in two lines (RetentionPolicy.cs:20-21 and TestJournal.cs:150-151 are the same formula). Warning: the formula is equal but its input is not. Risks

  • Both leak findings are verified by reading only. Step 1 turns them into two facts before any code moves. Green before the change means the finding is wrong and the guards are dropped; still red after the change means another pin exists on the read path that reading did not find (I listed all 29 static dictionaries in src and found none keyed by the neuron class there).
  • Per-call reflection for collectible classes has no measurement behind it (HostCostFacts.cs:27 is Explicit and no result is checked in). If a unit state or view read gets measurably slower, swap the three dictionaries in CallPolicy for one ConditionalWeakTable keyed by class; the rule does not move.
  • Swallowing a throwing [InterleavesWhen] predicate on TestOS hides an author bug the simulator used to show. Production already hides it. One log line in CallPolicy.MayInterleave would surface it on both hosts; not included because the core type has no logger.
  • The unload facts depend on GC timing. The existing Leaked assertions (12 in SynapseHostFacts.cs, 1 in SynapseHostSiloFacts.cs) use the same mechanism, so the risk is the existing one.
  • Core internals are used by three packages for four more types; a mixed-version install fails at load, not at compile. This is already true for other internals and os pins all DigitalBrain packages to one version.
  • Judged by LOC alone the move is small (about -51). Its value is that D5, D6 and two unlisted leaks close and five rules cannot drift again.

R03 · Kernel · wave 1

Delete the two-phase gate; caps become policy reservations

Remove the Gate/Committed/Aborted saga (tickets, 30 s provisional lease, per-brain gate index) and the Check type from both runtimes in one release, let the existing unit boundary refuse a stored build that still references Check (listed as RebuildRequired), and express the one real use (a spending cap) as an IPolicies.Allow/Release reservation made by the neuron that acts.

Net
src about -1,085 (measured: 733 in 8 deleted files + 259 in 6 shared files; est. 110 scattered; +17 est.) / PublicAPI.Unshipped -109 entries, 2 changed, +1 (measured) / tests about -550 est. (965 + 99 lines measured as gate-touching; est. 250 of those rewritten in place, +250 est. new facts and fixtures) / os -6 lines, 3 lines edited (measured), +30 est. one-off census not merged
Effort
M (3-4 days, top of the band): the src change is almost pure deletion, but 37 test files must be swept and about a dozen unit-host fact files re-expressed with a reaction on two runtimes, and it needs one NuGet release plus one os bump that is a production deploy.
Depends on
nothing
Review confidence
medium

What is wrong today

A gate synapse lets one neuron veto another neuron's publish. Because a journal entry carries one neuron's record, the framework runs a saga around every durable Save/Publish that carries a signal: ask each gate (next state held in memory under a ticket), append, announce, then tell each gate Committed or Aborted (src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Hosting/HostNeuronState.cs:44-56, :91-100). Nothing shipped uses it: 0 [Synapse] functions returning Check in os src (grep now), git log -S"Check.Allow(" over os src returns 0 commits, digitalbrain src/templates/examples declare none; only 50 test fixtures do. The saga is written twice, GateRunner.cs (264 lines) and TestGates.cs (154 lines), and the copies differ (announce-then-commit vs commit-then-deliver; different error mapping). Measured size: 733 lines in 8 gate-only files, 259 gate-only lines in 6 shared files, about 110 scattered lines in 19 more files, 109 PublicAPI.Unshipped entries, and 37 test files that name a gate identifier. It carries latent defects verified by reading: a reaction saved between Gate and Committed is overwritten (StateNeuron.cs:89 vs :190-195), Committed with an unknown ticket saves a publisher-supplied state (StateNeuron.cs:179-184), and the gate index fails open on a load/invalidate race (InstalledGates.cs:56-57 with InstalledGateIndex.cs:46).

Design principle

An invariant is enforced inside one aggregate, in one transaction. The gate enforces a cross-aggregate invariant with a hand-rolled two-phase commit between two journal entries, so it is neither atomic nor owned, and it is duplicated per runtime adapter. The target puts the limit where it is a true invariant: the Policies aggregate reserves in one entry of its own before the actor acts (fail-safe), and a state neuron has exactly one state, changed only by its own reactions.

Defects this removes

  • Lost update: a reaction saved between Gate and Committed runs on State, then FlushHead saves the provisional state computed from the older base over it - src/DigitalBrain/Synapses/StateNeuron.cs:89 vs :190-195
  • Blind save: Committed with a ticket neither queued nor in the 256-entry flushed memory saves the publisher-supplied state (after the 30 s lease pruned it, or after reactivation) - StateNeuron.cs:179-184, ProvisionalGateQueue.cs:5
  • Fail-open race: an invalidation during a load of a brain's installs returns without refreshing, and the same publish then finds no gates and proceeds - src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Synapses/InstalledGates.cs:56-57, InstalledGateIndex.cs:46, GateRunner.cs:111
  • Order drift between runtimes: Orleans announces then commits the gates (HostNeuronState.cs:55-56), TestOS commits the gates then delivers (TestNeuronContext.cs:107-109, :68-70)
  • Under-count after a crash between the publisher's append and Committed: nothing replays it, so a cap can be exceeded; documented as an accepted limit, not fixed - SynapseAttribute.cs:12-19, GateRunner.cs:154-157
  • Error drift: Orleans maps only a TimeoutException of the installs load to RuleViolationException (GateRunner.cs:62-67), TestOS wraps every failure and uses another message (TestGates.cs:124-127)
  • Rejected is journaled by a refusing gate (Save(State, [new Rejected(...)]), StateNeuron.cs:162) while its contract says it is never journaled (Rules/Rejected.cs:4-5)
  • Gates are not enforced on IBrainJournalWriter.Append, a production write path (Storage/BrainJournalWriter.cs:105); moot once gates are gone
  • db.gate-chain is written to and restored from RequestContext around every gated publish and read by no decision - GateRunner.cs:198-215, HostNeuronContext.cs:30-31
  • Coupling: a signal-carrying publish of any neuron waits up to GateTimeout (15 s) on the brain's installs neuron and fails closed when the installs are not loaded (first publish per silo process and after every install change), and every such publish scans and sorts the catalog - GateRunner.cs:53-69, InstalledGates.cs:48-55, InstalledGateIndex.cs:46-47, SynapseCatalog.cs:51-53

The publish saga leaves the write path, in both runtimes

A durable write becomes append-then-deliver, the same two steps in both runtimes. Today Orleans announces before it commits the gates (HostNeuronState.cs:55-56) and TestOS commits the gates before it delivers (TestNeuronContext.cs:107-109). GateRunner.cs (264), TestGates.cs (154), InstalledGates.cs (65) and GateVerdict.cs (21) have no caller left; a publish no longer reads the brain's installs or restores installed aliases.

StateNeuron loses its second, unsaved state

Today a state neuron has two states: State (journaled) and a queue of provisional next states keyed by ticket (ProvisionalGateQueue.cs, 100 lines). React runs on State (line 89); FlushHead saves the provisional one over it (190-195): the lost update. The blind save for an unknown ticket is lines 179-184. After: 130 lines leave StateNeuron.cs (435 -> about 305), ProvisionalGateQueue.cs is deleted, and the using DigitalBrain.Rules / Verdict alias go with them.

No function is a gate; a stored gate build is refused by the boundary that already re-checks stored builds

No tombstone type and no special refusal branch. The restorer already re-runs the unit boundary on stored bytes without loading them (SynapseRestorer.cs:50-55, :73-91) and SynapseHost.List shows the record as DeployStatus.RebuildRequired with the reason (SynapseHost.cs:270-277); one refused name is the whole migration path. A new source naming Check fails to compile (CS0246). Explicit enum values keep Reaction = 1 and View = 2 as PublicAPI.Unshipped has them. Neuron.Check(...), Rule, Rejected and RuleViolationException are a different mechanism and stay.

The spending cap: a gate in a unit versus a reservation in the Policies aggregate

Gate: ask, append, announce, then save the gate's total as a second entry; a crash in between leaves the charge journaled and never counted (documented at SynapseAttribute.cs:12-19). Policy: PoliciesNeuron.Allow reserves and saves in one entry of one neuron before the charge (PoliciesNeuron.cs:80-99); a crash leaves a reservation without a charge, so the cap is reached early, never passed. A unit cannot call IPolicies (it is on the refused list of AllowedApis), so the reservation belongs to the acting neuron or module, as os already does for 7 guarded actions (Gmail 1, GitHub 4, MCP 1, Salesforce 1) and proves end to end in os WeeklyCapJourneyFacts.cs:28.

  • Persisted data: none changes. GateVerdict crosses a grain call only, GateChanged is an ephemeral signal, provisional entries are in memory, rule.rejected entries a refusing gate journaled stay readable (Rejected stays). The persisted NeedsHash of a gate-free unit is byte-identical because an empty Gates list adds no line to the hash input (SynapseNeeds.cs:59); pinned by a fact
  • Grain contract: INeuronHost loses the methods aliased gate/committed/aborted; in a rolling deploy an old silo could still call them only if a gate exists
  • docs: docs/neurons-and-synapses.md:17, :19 (2 lines); templates and examples: 0

Risks

  • A production unit declares a gate and the census missed it: after the os bump its build is not restored, the source is listed RebuildRequired and its state neuron stops reacting until the source is redeployed without the gate. A functional loss for that unit, not a crash.
  • The test sweep is the real cost and the real risk: 37 test files name a gate identifier, and about a dozen of them are unit-host facts (deploy, update, restore after restart, bindings) that use a gate refusal as their observable. Deleting instead of re-expressing them would silently drop coverage of the unit host.
  • Gate-path side effects disappear: today the first signal-carrying publish of a brain per silo process reads the installs neuron and eagerly restores every installed alias (GateRunner.cs:59-69, :109-124). Both runtimes also restore on an activation miss and Orleans subscriptions are durable (PubSubStore), so this should not be load-bearing; it was not run. The conformance fact AnInstalledUnitReactsAfterARestartWithoutAGatePath proves it; if it is red on one runtime the fix is in that runtime's delivery, est. 10 lines.
  • Capability loss, stated plainly: a unit can no longer veto a signal of a neuron that does not cooperate; the actor must call IPolicies, units cannot call IPolicies at all, and policies only cover actions a module declares (ActionDescriptor).
  • Refusal text changes from the gate's own string to the rendered policy sentence (PolicyGuard.Require); a test or client matching the old text must follow.
  • A client outside the os repo may read kind == "gate" or isBlocking from the os endpoints; the fields stay with constant values. No dart/ts file under os/src reads them (grep).

Proof it worked

1) Census number recorded (expected 0 stored unit builds referencing DigitalBrain.Rules.Check). 2) In digitalbrain, grep -rE "GateRunner|TestGates|ProvisionalGateQueue|InstalledGates|IGateIndex|GateVerdict|GateChanged|GateTimeout|SynapseKind\.Gate|Rules\.Check" src --include=*.cs returns only the refused-name string in AllowedApis.cs; the 8 files (733 lines) are gone; StateNeuron.cs is about 305 lines (435 today). 3) Green facts: AllowedApisFacts.TheRemovedCheckTypeIsRefused, SynapseRestorerFacts.AStoredBuildNamingCheckIsListedAsRebuildRequired, SlotVocabularyFacts.AGateFreeNeedsHashIsPinned (same literal before and after step 4). 4) Conformance facts green on both runtimes: APublishDoesNotCallTheInstallsNeuron, AnInstalledUnitReactsAfterARestartWithoutAGatePath. 5) The cap holds without a gate: WeeklyCapFacts.ThePromiseHoldsAcrossAWeekOfCharges (TestOS) and WeeklyCapSiloFacts (silo), plus AFailedChargeReleasesItsReservation and AReservationWithoutAChargeStillCounts, all green already at step 2 against unchanged src; os WeeklyCapJourneyFacts.TheWeeklyCapPromiseHoldsFromSentenceToRefusedCharge stays green unchanged. 6) The unit-host facts that used a gate as observable still exist under reaction-based names (count of [Fact] in SynapseHostSiloFacts, SynapseHostFacts, SourceRestoreFacts, SourceBindingsFacts unchanged). 7) os compiles against the new version with exactly the 3-line + 3-member change. 8) After the production deploy no source is listed RebuildRequired for Check.

Your decision

(1) Is the non-cooperative veto (a unit refusing another neuron's signal) a product requirement? If no, delete as proposed. If yes, keep Check but still delete the saga: evaluate the pure gate function inside the journal append and write the gate's next state as a side record of the publisher's entry (est. +220 src lines, a journal entry format change, async gates and gates with [Slot] refused). (2) If the census finds a few gate units: rewrite them by hand as display reaction + policy rule before the bump, or accept that they show RebuildRequired after it?

R04 · Kernel · wave 1

Give the write protocol one home in the core

Every Save, Store, Clear and Publish of a neuron becomes one Commit value appended through a named IBrainJournal port by one KernelState in the core; BrainLog and the simulator's journal become adapters over one pure Journal aggregate.

Net
src about +285 est. (636 measured out, about 920 est. in) / tests about +665 est. (35 measured out, about 700 est. in, 300 of it the journal conformance kit) / os -8 measured in HandleDirectoryNeuron.cs plus 9 one-line edits. This is not a line-count win. The saga is a wash, the journal rules exist once instead of twice, and the growth is the named port, its two adapters, the nudge port and the Store member.
Effort
L (6-8 days): steps 1-2 are mechanical but step 2 changes the fold of the durable store and needs a replay rehearsal (about 3 days with the 15-fact kit); the Orleans switch touches SynapseEngine and 23 test call sites (1.5-2 days); the simulator switch surfaces an unknown number of facts in two repos (1-2 days); the os bump is a production deploy with its own gate (0.5-1 day).
Depends on
R03 (delete the two-phase gate): soft. Shown with gates gone; without it KernelState takes an IGatekeeper port and four more lines, and GateRunner/TestGates stay twice.
Fossil deletion of IBrainSignalLog / Log/BrainSignalLog.cs (fossil census row 3): soft. If it lands first, the AppendEntryWithPosition wrapper has no src caller left and 21 of the 23 test call sites of step 3 are gone with the fossil's test files.
R02 (pure rules once): soft. The retention formula stays where it is until then; the aggregate only offers HeldAt and Trim.
Judgment move 1 (pin facts and the differential UnitScenario transcript): recommended before steps 3 and 5, not required.
Review confidence
medium

What is wrong today

The write protocol (append state + signals + cursor as one entry, then tell subscribers) has no home in the core. It is written in an Orleans adapter (Hosting/HostNeuronState.cs, 136 lines: Save :32-57, Publish :81-101) and again in the simulator (TestOS/TestNeuronContext.cs :52-78 and :92-117, 53 lines), with a different order after the append (:55-56 vs :107-109) and a stateless refusal on the Orleans side only (:35). The journal rules are also twice: BrainLog.cs holds 147 lines of in-memory rules (version check, numbering, trim, replay fold; the eight ranges are listed under deletes) while TestJournal.cs:111-120 overwrites the record with no expected version, and os deploys AI-authored units after they pass on that copy (os BuilderTools.cs:81 -> UnitScenario.cs:52 TestOS.Local()). The Orleans side reaches the journal by downcast (HostNeuronState.cs:37 and :89) through journal-shaped overloads on an IGrainStorage (BrainGrainStorage.cs:26-43); the reaction cursor rides an AsyncLocal (ReactionCursor.cs, 68 lines) that HostNeuronState reads (:48, :93) and hands to the storage call, and the simulator has a second AsyncLocal (TestSynapses.cs:49). Side effects of the disguise: the stream reference carries a brain position read after the write gate was released (BrainGrainStorage.cs:67) where a per-source subscriber compares a publisher sequence (SynapseEngine.cs:252), which costs journal reads, not signals; an ephemeral signal sends a reference numbered by a process-static counter (HostNeuronState.cs:67, :111-112) that no subscriber can use. And 13 production sites save with no signal against the documented rule "There is no save without a signal" (NeuronOfTState.cs:6): 12 literal Save(next, []) calls plus OperationNeuron.cs:83-86 (a computed empty list), one of them by reflection on a compiler-generated backing field (os HandleDirectoryNeuron.cs:9-16).

Design principle

A domain protocol belongs to the domain layer and is written once; infrastructure implements a narrow driven port (ports and adapters), and an aggregate's invariants (version check, numbering, atomic entry) live in the aggregate, not in each store. Today the invariant "one entry = state + signals + cursor, checked against its versions" is enforced by one adapter and skipped by the other; the target puts it in a pure Journal aggregate and one commit function, and names the port IBrainJournal instead of disguising it as IGrainStorage.

Defects this removes

  • src/DigitalBrain.Testing/TestOS/TestJournal.cs:111-120: the simulator's save overwrites the record with no expected version, so a save over a record the activation never loaded passes on the copy the os deploy gate runs (os BuilderTools.cs:81) and conflicts in production (BrainLog.cs:203-207).
  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Storage/BrainGrainStorage.cs:65-67 with Hosting/HostNeuronState.cs:121-123 and Synapses/SynapseEngine.cs:252: the stream reference carries a brain position, read after the write gate was released, where a per-source subscriber compares a publisher sequence. The duplicate check stops working in a brain with more than one publisher (extra journal reads; no signal is lost). BrainLog.cs:158 already returns the right number and is ignored.
  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Hosting/HostNeuronState.cs:67 and :103-114: an ephemeral signal sends a stream reference numbered by a process-static counter for a signal that is not in the journal; src/DigitalBrain.Testing/TestOS/TestNeuronContext.cs:73-78 does not (a runtime drift, pure cost).
  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Hosting/HostNeuronState.cs:35 and :129-135 vs src/DigitalBrain.Testing/TestOS/TestNeuronContext.cs:96-110: a [StatelessNeuron] may save on the simulator and not on Orleans.
  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Synapses/SynapseEngine.cs:317-328 with Hosting/HostNeuronState.cs:17: a version conflict thrown inside a reaction is swallowed by the catch-all, nothing drops the activation and its ETag stays stale, so every later save of that activation fails until it idles out. KernelState asks the host to drop the activation on every stale version.
  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Storage/BrainJournalWriter.cs:125-127: IBrainJournalWriter.Append reports a position read outside the write gate.
  • os HandleDirectoryNeuron.cs:9-16: a neuron sets Neuron<TState>'s compiler-generated backing field by reflection to save without a signal; src/DigitalBrain/Neurons/NeuronOfTState.cs:6 documents a rule that 13 production sites bypass.
  • Only if R03 has not already removed the gates: src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Hosting/HostNeuronState.cs:55-56 vs src/DigitalBrain.Testing/TestOS/TestNeuronContext.cs:107-109, the order of announce and gate commit differs per runtime; the variant with an IGatekeeper port fixes one order.

The save saga is written twice, in a different order, with two ambients; it becomes one commit function and one scope

Same steps on both sides today, but the order after the append differs, only Orleans refuses a stateless save, only Orleans passes a version, and each side finds the cursor through its own AsyncLocal (ReactionCursor.For at :48; Delivery.Committed at :107 reads TestSynapses.cs:49). After: one order, one version field, one scope read by one function. Orleans opens the scope with a move that encodes the synapses record; the simulator opens it with a null move and carried: () => Advance(entry, cursor), which replaces SynapseRegistry.Committed at the same moment in the flow. Shown with gates already deleted (R03); without R03 the function takes an IGatekeeper port and four more lines.

Version check in one journal, blind overwrite in the other; one pure aggregate decides

The top block is the production rule; the bottom block is what the deploy gate runs: _states[id] = json. After, both adapters call the same Decide, so the simulator refuses what production refuses (a save over a record the activation never loaded, a seed under a live activation). BrainLog keeps only disk, seal, lease and checkpoint. BrainEntry and BrainSnapshot stay the on-disk shapes, so every existing byte reads as before; the one difference in new bytes is that a signal-only entry is written with StateName "" where today it carries the class state name, a field Apply ignores when Change is None (BrainLog.cs:537-542).

The journal hides behind IGrainStorage and is reached by downcast; it becomes a named port with a thin adapter

Last line of the before block: log.LastPosition is read after the append returned, outside the write gate, and the caller sends it as a per-publisher sequence; BrainLog.AppendEntryWithPosition (BrainLog.cs:158) already returns the right number and is ignored here. After, the result carries both numbers from the entry itself. The record key comes from the activation's own grain id, not from a registry lookup at write time, so os PlainInstanceRegistration.cs:54-56 keeps reading the same key. BrainGrainStorage shrinks to its three IGrainStorage members and stays registered for application grains that are not neurons (os KernelGrains.cs:21,74,110). The expected version sits on StateWrite and on CursorMove because one entry writes two records with two versions.

Silent saves and ephemeral publish get honest members

Top: an os neuron sets a compiler-generated field by reflection because the only way to save without a signal is to bypass Neuron<TState>; 13 production sites save with no signal (12 literal [] calls: 2 digitalbrain, 10 os; plus OperationNeuron.cs:86). Bottom: an ephemeral signal sends a stream reference numbered by a process-wide static counter for a signal that is not in the journal; the only consumer of references is SynapseEngine, which can count it as a duplicate or read the journal and find nothing. The simulator does not send it (TestNeuronContext.cs:73-78). After: Store says what it does, and Offered reaches host listeners only, on both runtimes.

Deleted lines
