Steps, each shippable

  1. 1. digitalbrain, additive, core only: add Kernel/Commit.cs, Journal.cs, MemoryJournal.cs, IBrainJournal.cs (with ISignalNudge), ReactionScope.cs, KernelState.cs; add INeuronState.Store (default implementation) and protected Neuron<TState>.Store with the StoreState split in NeuronOfTState.cs:28 and StateNeuron.cs:344 (+2 lines PublicAPI.Unshipped.txt). Add JournalFacts, KernelStateFacts, ReactionScopeFacts in tests/DigitalBrain.Tests.Unit/Kernel. No host uses the kernel and Neuron<TState>.Save still refuses an empty list, so the Store branch is reachable only through the new member; suite green by construction.
  2. 2. digitalbrain: BrainLog folds through Journal<JournaledSignalPayload>. New BrainLog.Append and Record; AppendEntry (both overloads), AppendEntryWithPosition (caller Log/BrainSignalLog.cs:57) and AppendEncoded stay as wrappers that translate StaleVersionException to InconsistentStateException; Read, Named (caller SynapseObservation.cs:50), the three signal reads, Floors, Checkpoint and Recover are re-plumbed; the checkpoint unwraps record bytes with MemoryMarshal.TryGetArray instead of copying them. Add BrainLogJournal and UnregisteredJournal (no host uses them yet). Add the JournalConformance kit with MemoryJournalFacts and BrainLogJournalFacts in tests/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans.Tests.Integration/Storage (one new InternalsVisibleTo in DigitalBrain.csproj for that test project). Disk format untouched. Safety net unchanged: the 31 facts in that Storage folder (7 GoldenBytesFacts) and every silo fact. Before merging, open a copy of a real brain directory with the new build (replay, checkpoint, reopen).
  3. 3. digitalbrain, the Orleans switch: NeuronHostGrainBase.OnActivateAsync builds the journal once (record key = this.GetGrainId()) and hands it to SynapseEngine and HostNeuronContext; HostNeuronContext constructs KernelState with StreamNudge, CurrentCause.Stamp and ticks.Deactivate; SynapseEngine uses ReactionScope and the port for its synapses record (Load, Persist; Persist also asks for deactivation on a stale version); NeuronHostGrainBase.Invoke (:142) catches StaleVersionException and rethrows InconsistentStateException; BrainJournalWriter.cs:125-127 takes the position from the returned entry. Delete HostNeuronState.cs, ReactionCursor.cs, ReactionCursorFacts.cs; shrink BrainGrainStorage to its 3 IGrainStorage members; retarget the 23 test call sites in 6 integration files to one BrainStore.Commit helper (2 call sites in JournalJsonFacts if the IBrainSignalLog fossil and its test files are already deleted). Net: the DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans.Tests.Integration suite and HostConformanceFacts on the local Orleans target.
  4. 4. digitalbrain, simulator part 1, behaviour-neutral: TestJournal becomes a facade over MemoryJournal. Its internal Load, Save, Append and Clear survive this step as wrappers that expect the current version, so nothing can conflict yet; Seed, Publishers(), Checkpoint(publisher, options, lowestLive) and Position keep their signatures (callers TestOS.cs:334, :347, :349; TestSynapses.cs:113, :201). DigitalBrain.Testing.Tests.Unit runs unchanged.
  5. 5. digitalbrain, the simulator switch: TestNeuronContext constructs the same KernelState (TestNudge over Delivery.Appended and Listeners.Offered, TestCause.Stamp, TestNeuronHost.Drop); TestSynapses opens ReactionScope at :352 and :362 with a null move and carried: () => Advance(entry, cursor); delete JournalState, the Publish bodies, Reacting, Committed, the Reaction record and the step-4 wrappers. The simulator now checks versions and refuses a stateless save. Facts that relied on either fail here and are fixed here; known candidates: tests/DigitalBrain.Testing.Tests.Unit/TestOS/TurnNeurons.cs:183 and ReactionDeliveryFacts.cs:72. The cursor record in the simulator's entries waits for R06.
  6. 6. digitalbrain: the zero-signal sites call Store: Identity.Local/Connections/ConnectionIndexNeuron.cs:46, Operations/OperationNeuron.cs:121, and OperationNeuron.cs:86 through the split. Before publishing, run the os unit suites against the local package feed: it is the first time 72 os files that use TestOS meet the stricter simulator. Ship as digitalbrain release N (NuGet).
  7. 7. os, at the bump to release N (a production deploy): re-pin; 10 one-line edits from Context.State.Save(x, []) to Store; HandleDirectoryNeuron loses its reflection. Run os unit and integration suites, OsHostConformanceFacts, then the live aspire start gate.
  8. 8. digitalbrain, release N+1, only if the owner says yes: KernelState.Save with an empty list throws ArgumentException; one architecture fact pins that no src file calls State.Save(..., []).

What breaks

  • Public API: nothing removed. Added: INeuronState.Store<T>(T) with a default implementation (the 4 other implementers keep compiling: InMemoryNeuronState, RecordingContext, os GmailStateFacts.FaultingContext, os GitHubRepositoryWritesFacts.TestNeuronState) and protected Neuron<TState>.Store(TState). No member with that signature exists in a subclass in either repo (os FilesWorkspaceNeuron.cs:335 is Store(FilesApp, bool), an overload; os AgentNeuron.cs:478 is a local function).
  • Internal types deleted: HostNeuronState (3 references, all in HostNeuronContext.cs, plus a doc comment in Signalling/ClusterListeners.cs:15), HostNeuronContext.HostState (0 uses), ReactionCursor (7 src references in 3 files, 1 test file), the BrainGrainStorage journal overloads (2 src call sites, 23 test call sites in 6 files). Constructors of SynapseEngine and HostNeuronContext gain a journal parameter (1 caller each, NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:54-55).
  • os call sites: 10 one-line edits (AuthNeuron.cs:23, AgentNeuron.cs:715, CircleFactoryNeuron.cs:47, CircleInvitesNeuron.cs:59, InviteCodeNeuron.cs:45 and :95, Connections/ConnectionIndexNeuron.cs:39, HandleDirectoryNeuron.cs:14, DeploymentNeuron.cs:230, TableIndexNeuron.cs:77). They keep compiling and working until step 8. os references no internal type this move deletes (IntoChat.OS.Kernel.Composition is a friend of Runtime.Orleans; grep: 0 hits).
  • Persisted data: format and record keys unchanged (BrainEntry, BrainSnapshot, key = host grain id + state name), so os PlainInstanceRegistration.cs:54-56 and the 3 os grains on the cluster store read what they read today. One content difference in new entries: a signal-only entry is written with StateName "" instead of the class state name; replay ignores the field for such entries.
  • Behaviour on Orleans: the stream reference carries a publisher sequence again (as before commit 4a95825); an ephemeral signal no longer sends a stream reference; a state that cannot be encoded or decoded throws JsonException/NotSupportedException instead of JournalPayloadException (no catch in either repo); a Clear inside a reaction now carries the cursor like a Save; a stale version asks the host to deactivate the activation on every path, not only when the exception escapes a grain call.
  • Behaviour when the cluster store is not BrainLog (the public UseClusterStore(IClusterStoreRegistration) hook; in the repos only the test FakeClusterStore, 9 uses in 6 files): a stateful hosted neuron no longer falls back to plain IGrainStorage for loads and zero-signal saves (HostNeuronState.cs:37-43 is gone); it fails with UnregisteredClusterStorage.Message.
  • Behaviour in the simulator (TestOS, named in 72 os files and run by the os deploy gate): saves are version-checked, a [StatelessNeuron] may no longer save, a conflict drops the activation, SeedStoredState under a live activation makes its next save conflict, and JournaledSignal.At is rounded to milliseconds.
  • Tests: ReactionCursorFacts.cs (2 facts) retargeted; 23 call sites in JournalJsonFacts (2), ChallengerSignalLogStressFacts (3), MultiSubscriberCursorFacts (3), SignalLogAdversarialFacts (6), SignalLogCompactionFacts (4), SignalLogPubSubFacts (5) retargeted; the last five files are also test files of the dead IBrainSignalLog fossil.

Risks

  • Step 2 changes the fold of the durable store. A wrong fold loses data on replay. Mitigation: the aggregate is BrainLog's code moved, not rewritten; golden-bytes facts; the conformance kit runs on BrainLog from the same step; a rehearsal on a copy of real brain storage; Apply now refuses a non-contiguous entry on live writes too.
  • Simulator facts in both repos that relied on blind overwrite or on a stateless save fail at step 5 (digitalbrain) and surface in os only when os runs against the new package. Two candidates are known (step 5); the full count is unknown because nothing was run.
  • The ambient does not disappear. ReactionScope is still an AsyncLocal, because [On] handlers call Save with no handle to their reaction and an interleaved turn must not carry the cursor. It goes from two copies to one class read by one function, and a closed scope can no longer carry.
  • Net source grows by about 285 lines. If the owner measures this move by lines it is a loss; the reduction comes in R06 (streams and the simulator's synapse registry) once the port exists.
  • One Orleans behaviour is unverified: that an InconsistentStateException escaping a grain call deactivates the activation (orleans-facts marks it from memory). The design asks for deactivation explicitly through the existing IHostTicks.Deactivate, so it does not depend on it.
  • StaleVersionException is internal to the core. Only NeuronHostGrainBase.Invoke maps it to InconsistentStateException; on timer, reminder and reaction turns it is what neuron code sees. No code in either repo catches either type today.
  • If R03 is not done first, GateRunner (264 lines) and TestGates (154 lines) move behind an IGatekeeper port instead of disappearing, and this move grows by about 70 lines while the hardest twin pair stays twice.

Proof it worked

1) grep in src: "is not BrainGrainStorage", "ReactionCursor", "_ephemeralSequence" return 0 hits; "GrainState<" returns 0 hits under Hosting/ and Synapses/; "class .* : INeuronState" returns KernelState and InMemoryNeuronState only; "new KernelState(" returns exactly 2 hits (HostNeuronContext, TestNeuronContext). 2) JournalConformance<TTarget> (15 facts) passes unchanged on MemoryJournalTarget and BrainLogJournalTarget, including "a stale state version refuses the whole commit", "a stale cursor version refuses the whole commit", "a refused commit consumes no sequence and no position" and "after Reopen versions, sequences and positions are what they were". 3) KernelStateFacts pass in DigitalBrain.Tests.Unit, which references the core and DigitalBrain.UI and no runtime: nothing is nudged before the append returned; a refused append nudges nothing and calls drop; a save inside a ReactionScope carries the cursor and a save in a flow that outlived the scope does not; Store nudges nobody; an ephemeral publish appends nothing; a stateless neuron may publish and may not save, store or clear. 4) GoldenBytesFacts (7) and the other 24 Storage facts pass with no edit, and a copy of a real brain directory opens, checkpoints and reopens. 5) After step 7, grep "State\.Save\([^,]+, \[\]\)" over os/src and digitalbrain/src returns 0 production hits. 6) HostConformanceFacts pass on both targets, and OsHostConformanceFacts plus the live aspire start pass after the os bump.

Your decision

May INeuronState.Save with an empty signal list become an ArgumentException one release after Store ships (step 8)? It makes NeuronOfTState.cs:6 true at both levels, and it breaks at run time any module outside these two repos that still passes an empty list, including computed lists like the one at OperationNeuron.cs:83-86. The alternative is to keep Save permissive forever and treat Store as the readable name.

Review notes on this proposal

Quote check. All 8 before-snippets (4 pairs) were opened and compared line by line at HEAD a275f82 / os HEAD: HostNeuronState.cs:32-57 and :103-114, TestNeuronContext.cs:96-110, BrainLog.cs:200-222, TestJournal.cs:110-120, BrainGrainStorage.cs:26-43 and :60-68, os HandleDirectoryNeuron.cs:9-16. All are verbatim with correct ranges; nothing had to be fixed. Every other file:line cited in the final text (SynapseEngine.cs:252 and :317-328, BrainJournalWriter.cs:125-127, BrainLog.cs:158, OperationNeuron.cs:83-86, StateNeuron.cs:335-346, TestSynapses.cs:49/:321-326/:352/:362/:400, TestOS.cs:334/:347/:349, UnitScenario.cs:52, BuilderTools.cs:81) was checked against the files.

Corrections the reviewer made

  • Renamed kernel type StoredRecord to JournalRecord: DigitalBrain.State.StoredRecord is a public shipped record (src/DigitalBrain/State/StateTypes.cs:14) and the names would collide.
  • Renamed kernel type PublisherMark to PublisherRange: DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans.PublisherMark is the on-disk snapshot record (Storage/BrainLogModel.cs:28) and would silently win inside BrainLog.
  • Removed IBrainJournals/BrainJournals and the registry-based RecordOwner. The record key is now taken from the activation's own grain id in NeuronHostGrainBase.OnActivateAsync and the journal is built once per activation: recomputing the grain type from the registry at write time can produce another key after a unit reload.
  • KernelState: added Clear and PublishEphemeral (missing from the proposal snippet although INeuronState.Clear is required and both hosts expose PublishEphemeral); renamed the private method Commit to CommitEntry so it does not shadow the Commit type.
  • ReactionScope: Move may return null, Open takes an optional carried callback (replaces the simulator's Committed at the same point in the flow instead of a finally at the end of the reaction), and a closed scope never carries (a detached flow started inside a reaction keeps the scope in its captured ExecutionContext).
  • Decided the designer's open decision 2: the simulator writes no cursor record in this move (it would be write-only with a new failure mode); it waits for R06. Removed the claim that the simulator gets a cursor record in the entry for free.
  • Moved the conformance kit out of the shipped DigitalBrain.Testing.Silo package into tests/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans.Tests.Integration (a public kit over an internal port cannot be used by anyone outside); the new InternalsVisibleTo targets that test project instead.
  • Zero-signal census corrected from 12 to 13: OperationNeuron.cs:83-86 passes a computed empty list. Added it and the StateNeuron.StoreState override (StateNeuron.cs:335-346) to the Store split, otherwise the later 'Save with [] throws' step breaks them at run time.
  • Added missed callers that step 2 must keep working: BrainLog.AppendEntryWithPosition (Log/BrainSignalLog.cs:57), BrainLog.Named (Observation/SynapseObservation.cs:50), BrainLog.Read (2 callers). Raised the BrainLog delegation estimate from 45 to 70 lines.
  • TestJournal facade: kept Publishers() and the Checkpoint signature (callers TestOS.cs:347, :349), which the designer's facade dropped or changed.
  • Reordered steps: BrainLogJournal now lands with the BrainLog fold so BrainLogJournalFacts can exist in that step (the designer added the facts in step 3 and the adapter in step 5); merged the two additive core steps; added a run of the os suites against the local feed before publishing.
  • Qualified 'no stored byte changes': existing bytes and the format are untouched, but new signal-only entries carry StateName "" instead of the class state name (ignored by replay).
  • Bugs: stated that B3 costs journal reads and loses no signal, and that BrainLog.cs:158 already returns the atomic position; made the D1b order drift conditional on R03 not being done; added a verified defect (a conflict inside a reaction is swallowed at SynapseEngine.cs:317-328 and leaves the activation with a stale ETag).
  • Breaks: added the non-BrainLog cluster store case (public UseClusterStore hook), the Clear-carries-cursor change, millisecond rounding of JournaledSignal.At in the simulator, the two changed internal constructors, and that 5 of the 6 retargeted test files belong to the dead IBrainSignalLog fossil; added that fossil as a soft dependency.
  • Problem text: listed the BrainLog ranges correctly (the four ranges named summed to 112, not 147), corrected where the cursor AsyncLocal is read (HostNeuronState.cs:48, :93, not inside the storage call), dropped the unreproducible '66 subclasses' count, fixed 'references only the core' (DigitalBrain.Tests.Unit also references DigitalBrain.UI).
  • Effort raised from M (3-4 days) to L (6-8 days); net LOC restated (src about +285, tests about +665 with the kit counted as tests, os -8).

Doubts that remain

  • Nothing was compiled or run (read-only). The after code was checked against the remaining types by reading; the Journal<TSignal> name next to BrainLog's private string property Journal and TestOS.Journal(...) compiles by the lookup rules but may deserve a rename.
  • The number of simulator facts (digitalbrain and os) that depend on blind overwrite, on a stateless save, or on sub-millisecond JournaledSignal.At is unknown without running the suites.
  • MemoryJournal sits in the production core package as an internal type; it could live in DigitalBrain.Testing instead, at the price of moving KernelStateFacts to DigitalBrain.Testing.Tests.Unit and one more friend assembly for the kit.
  • Orleans deactivating an activation when InconsistentStateException escapes a grain call is from memory in orleans-facts; the design does not depend on it but the double deactivation request (explicit plus Orleans) was not exercised.
  • A protected Store on Neuron<TState> is visible to RevisionedNeuron<TState>, Flow<T>, ConnectionNeuron<TState> and StateNeuron subclasses; whether a silent store may bypass their own save rules was not examined.
  • Whether the 5 SignalLog* test files disappear entirely with the IBrainSignalLog fossil (they also call store.Storage overloads) depends on how the fossil move is cut; the '2 instead of 23 call sites' figure assumes they do.
  • The checkpoint path must avoid copying every record when converting ReadOnlyMemory<byte> back to BrainRecord; the TryGetArray approach is stated but not measured.

R05 · Kernel · wave 1

Write subscriptions and delivery once, in the journal

Replace SynapseEngine, the Orleans stream doorbell and the TestSynapses twin with one kernel Subscriptions aggregate and one Delivery loop, every cursor a brain Position, woken by a [OneWay] grain call that is driven from a subscription index the journal folds from its own records.

Net
src about -65 (about 1,225 deleted: 1,141 in six whole files and 38 measured, about 46 partial / about 1,160 added, est.) and about -155 once SubscriptionUpgrade leaves in the cleanup release / tests about +680 (293 deleted, measured / about 970 added, est.) / os -2 lines and 1 edit. The gain is one implementation instead of two, not line count.
Effort
L (about 2 weeks, the upper end): about 1,160 new source lines and 970 test lines, two adapter switches, a persisted-cursor upgrade that must be rehearsed on a copy of production storage, and 21 existing test files deleted or retargeted; it can only start after R04.
Depends on
R04
R02 (soft: the [On] lookup in the core; otherwise ReactionDispatcher.cs moves in this move)
Review confidence
medium

What is wrong today

The delivery protocol (dedupe a nudge, read from the cursor, Gap, react, move the cursor in the reaction's commit) is written twice: SynapseEngine.cs (520 lines, two near-copy loops Pull :239-297 and PullBrain :472-516) and TestSynapses.cs (418 lines); the protocol-twice re-measurement lists 5 drifts between them (D7, D9-D12) and this review found two more (typed Ensure of an unregistered signal type throws on Orleans, SynapseEngine.cs:107-109, and is accepted by the simulator, TestSynapses.cs:63-66; the simulator guards its static [On] cache against collectible types, TestSynapses.cs:378-380, the Orleans ReactionDispatcher.cs:13,35 does not). The subscriber state is an anemic bag (SynapseState.cs:7-26) whose Cursor is documented as a publisher sequence (:24) and holds a brain position for "*" (SynapseEngine.cs:413-415). The doorbell is an Orleans stream that carries only a pointer and needs SignalPublisher (90), StreamNames (48), SignalReference (16), a PubSubStore, a durability check and an Azure queue; no other code in either repo uses streams. The documented recovery (re-announce, reconcile on silo start: SignalPublisher.cs:38-42,59-63) has no production caller, os skips the matching conformance fact (os IntoChatOsTarget.cs:94-99), and the retention floor never sees a live cursor on Orleans (SynapseRetentionHosting.cs:16,28-32) while the simulator's does (TestSynapses.cs:179-187). Reading the code also shows: a brain-wide subscriber can lose signals with no Gap because the brain floor is "first held position - 1" while trimming is per publisher (BrainLog.cs:294 vs :411-425); a reference from a publisher the subscriber also holds a per-source entry for never pulls the "*" entry (SynapseEngine.cs:215-224); a non-state neuron with overlapping per-source and "*" entries reacts twice (SynapseEngine.cs:422).

Design principle

An aggregate owns its invariants and a domain service is written once over driven ports. Today "Subscription" is not a type: its invariants (no self, same brain, one "*" entry, coverage, monotonic cursor) are procedural code in two engines and its cursor has two units under one name. The target makes Subscriptions an immutable aggregate with one value object (Position), Delivery the single domain service, and Orleans and the simulator adapters of four narrow ports (IJournalView, INudge, ISignalConsumer, IAdmission).

Defects this removes

  • Cursor documented as a publisher sequence but holds a brain position for "*": SynapseState.cs:24 vs SynapseEngine.cs:413-415.
  • Duplicate suppression is dead with more than one publisher (B3; it costs journal reads, never signals): BrainGrainStorage.cs:65-67, SynapseEngine.cs:252, BrainJournalWriter.cs:127-131. R04 patches the main path; one coordinate closes it on every path.
  • Retention floor never honours a live cursor on Orleans (D7/B6): SynapseRetentionHosting.cs:16,28-32 vs TestSynapses.cs:179-187.
  • Documented re-announce and start-up reconcile have no production caller (B7): SignalPublisher.cs:38-42,59-63; HostSynapses.cs:20-22 (ISynapseHost.CatchUp is called by one test only). os skips the matching conformance fact: os IntoChatOsTarget.cs:94-99.
  • A brain-wide subscriber loses signals without a Gap, because the brain floor is 'first held position - 1' while trimming is per publisher: BrainLog.cs:294 vs :411-425 (read, not run).
  • A reference from a publisher with a per-source entry never pulls the "*" entry, delaying that publisher's other aliases: SynapseEngine.cs:215-224.
  • A non-state neuron with overlapping per-source and "*" entries runs its reaction twice: SynapseEngine.cs:422 (same condition at TestSynapses.cs:130).
  • One cursor-only journal entry after every brain-wide page even when reactions carried the cursor (B9): SynapseEngine.cs:512 with :329-330.
  • Install edges additive on Orleans, exact in the simulator (D10): SynapseEngine.cs:418-419 vs TestSynapses.cs:122-126.
  • Nudge range and delivery observation differ between runtimes (D11, D12): SynapseEngine.cs:273,314,362 vs TestSynapses.cs:283,344-347.
  • Causation ids mix publisher sequences and brain positions: BrainSignalHistory.cs:73-75.
  • Local topology loses stream subscriptions on restart while cursors persist (B8, plausible, not run): LocalProfile.cs:129-135,142; LocalMemoryGrainStorage.cs:11 is a process-static dictionary.
  • Brain-wide synapse delivers a non-state neuron its own signals against the public contract: ISynapses.cs:24 vs SynapseEngine.cs:506 and TestSynapses.cs:314.
  • Typed Ensure of an unregistered signal type throws on Orleans and is accepted by the simulator: SynapseEngine.cs:107-109 vs TestSynapses.cs:63-66. One check in Subscribing.
  • The Orleans [On] lookup caches (neuron class, signal type) in a static dictionary with no IsCollectible guard and can pin a collectible unit; the simulator's copy has the guard: ReactionDispatcher.cs:13,35 vs TestSynapses.cs:378-380.

Two pull loops and React on Orleans become one Delivery in the core

The same algorithm, once, with no Orleans type and no storage call: the cursor travels as R04's Commit.Cursor through ReactionScope. PullBrain (:472-516) disappears because a per-source and a brain-wide subscription differ only in the publisher filter of ReadAfter. A passed-over signal no longer costs a write each (today :290 and :512); a nudged catch-up that delivers nothing still writes one cursor entry. Not compiled.

The simulator twin shrinks to how a reaction becomes a turn

The before block is the same loop as the Orleans one with a different range (through the reference, not to the end), a different cursor write (an in-memory Advance, no record) and its own AsyncLocal. After, the simulator runs the kernel's Delivery and keeps only what is honestly simulator: the turn scheduler, the direct enqueue, and failing the run on a throwing reaction. os runs this simulator as its deploy gate, so every drift here was a gate hole.

Two mutable lists plus procedural invariants become the Subscriptions aggregate

Cursor is a Position in every entry, so the comment at SynapseState.cs:24 can no longer lie. The coverage rule stops being is ISynapseReceiver in transport code and applies to every neuron; the same block exists a second time at TestSynapses.cs:129-138 and goes with it. At is what lets retention tell a live cursor from a stale one on Orleans too. Known hole kept: a brain-wide alias of a base type and a per-source alias of a derived type still deliver twice.

Stream, namespace and PubSubStore become a one-way grain call; the null cursor lookup becomes a query

Who to wake, who is due and the lowest live cursor are three queries on one projection that replay rebuilds. The publisher awaits nothing of a subscriber. The sweep asks by wanted alias, not by raw position: a position-only "behind" check would wake every subscriber of an active brain every minute. StreamNames, SignalReference, the namespace concept, IStreamSubscriptionManager, OnSubscribed, PubSubStoreCheck and the Azure queue have no remaining reason to exist; the swallowed send failure at :51-55 has nothing left to swallow because Reconcile has a caller.

Deleted lines

src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Synapses/SynapseEngine.cssrc520
src/DigitalBrain.Testing/TestOS/TestSynapses.cs (TestSynapses + SynapseRegistry)src418
src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Synapses/SignalPublisher.cs (SignalPublisher, AppendedSignals, SignalPublishing)src90
src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Synapses/SynapseState.cs (the v1 DTO moves into SubscriptionUpgrade.cs; SynapseCounters becomes the core's DeliveryCounters)src49
src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Synapses/StreamNames.cssrc48

Steps, each shippable

  1. 0. R04's critique calls this move "R06" in two places; it means this one.
  2. 1. db, additive: Kernel/Subscriptions.cs, SubscriptionIndex.cs, IJournalView; Journal<TSignal> gains After, a floor position per publisher, alias marks and the index fold (built from the final records after replay, live per entry afterwards, never throws); MemoryJournal and a BrainLog-side view implement IJournalView; the on-disk PublisherMark (BrainLogModel.cs:28) gains one member, Id(3) FloorPosition (LastPosition is derived). What breaks

  • Persisted: every subscriber gets a new record "subscriptions" (cursors as brain positions, written once per brain when its log is opened); the v1 record "synapses" is left untouched for one release, then cleared.
  • Persisted: the on-disk PublisherMark (BrainLogModel.cs:28) gains one optional member, Id(3) FloorPosition; older checkpoints load and the value is derived once (0 when Floor is 0). A brain-wide subscriber that is behind at the upgrade can get one Gap it would not have got before.
  • Persisted / infra: the PubSubStore grain store, the PubSubRendezvous blobs and the Azure queue signals-0 are no longer read or written; stream ids brain.signals.* and the wire alias synapse.ref leave ContractManifest.json; the grain method alias nudge enters it.
  • Public semantics: Gap.From / Gap.To become brain positions. 1 os reader (os PlainSubscription.cs:44, logs only); 3 test assertions on gap:1-4 (HostConformanceFacts.cs:235, SynapseCursorFacts.cs:138, ReactionDeliveryFacts.cs:184) hold while the source is the brain's only publisher.
  • Public semantics (simulator): TestOS.Cursor(subscriber, source) returns a brain position, not a publisher sequence: 4 assertions in ReactionDeliveryFacts.cs, 0 callers in os.
  • Public API removed in the release: CloudProfile.SignalQueues and TopologyValidator.ValidatePubSubStore (0 os callers each). Added: RetentionOptions.ReconcileEvery.
  • Public API removed one release later: RuntimeOptions.PubSubStore and .SignalStreams (os sets both at OsRuntimeOptions.cs:28-29 and reads one at CloudStorage.cs:16), IntoChatOSNames.PubSubStore / .SignalStreams, StreamsBinding (4 members) and with it the positional member Bindings.Streams (constructor and Deconstruct change), Need.Streams and NeedKind.Streams in the core package. 0 os callers of the last four; no module in either repo declares Need.Streams.
  • Public API unchanged: ISynapses (os: 6 files / 10 call sites call Ensure or Remove, 7 files call EnsureBrainWide; no source change), ISignalHistory, IRetentionPolicy, SynapseObservation.List / .Deliveries (os Kernel.cs:60,63), FrameworkTarget.SubscriptionCount (overridden in os IntoChatOsTarget.cs:110), TestOS.DuplicatesDropped / SynapsesOf / Synapses, TestOSOptions.
  • Behaviour: a brain-wide subscription no longer delivers a non-state neuron its own signals; coverage trimming applies to every neuron; install edges are exact on Orleans; the Deliveries observation includes brain-wide deliveries, after success; in the simulator a nudge activates an inactive per-source subscriber; a live lagging cursor now pins retention on Orleans for StaleCursorAfter (7 days by default); Context.Caller inside a reaction is empty.
  • Internal grain interface ISynapseHost gains [OneWay] Nudge(long): an old silo does not have the method and the call is lost; the sweep heals it once the old silo is gone.
  • Internal types deleted: SynapseEngine, SynapseState / SynapseSource (Orleans), SynapseCounters, SignalPublisher, AppendedSignals, StreamNames, SignalReference, HostSynapses, NullSynapseCursorLookup, SynapseRegistry, TestSynapses. 12 test files in digitalbrain/tests name them (3 deleted, 9 retargeted); 0 files in os.

Risks

  • The cursor upgrade touches persisted production state: a wrong conversion skips or repeats signals. Mitigated by the 'same due set' assertion on a copy of real storage, golden v1 records, writes that require the record not to exist, and the v1 record left byte-identical.
  • Rollback, or a brain that moves back to an old silo during a mixed-version window, replays every reaction since the upgrade, because the old build reads the frozen v1 cursors. Roll forward only after day one unless the owner pays for dual-write (open decision).
  • Retention changes in production: every converted cursor counts as live for StaleCursorAfter, so journals stop trimming past them for up to 7 days after the upgrade, and from then on a stuck subscriber (a reaction that always throws) pins its publishers' history until it goes stale and then gets a Gap. There is still no dead-letter.
  • Three rules become unconditional (no self-delivery on brain-wide, coverage trimming, exact install edges). The 7 os files that call EnsureBrainWide were grepped: none constructs a signal type it subscribes to. That is not a proof.
  • A nudge is a grain call and activates its target. The alias rule limits that to subscribers that want one of the appended signals; the sweep adds one activation per subscriber per SkipFlush (1,000) positions of covered traffic it does not want. With HistoryWindowSignals below 1,000 a subscriber of rare signals on a busy publisher can still get a Gap for history it did not want, as today.
  • The sweep walks every subscription of every open brain once a minute, in memory, against the alias marks of what it covers; Hearing walks every subscription of the brain on every append. Both need an index by source if a brain holds thousands of subscriptions. A brain no activation has opened since silo start is not reconciled.
  • A stale version on the subscription record (a reentrant host: an interleaved Ensure writes while a reaction is open) refuses the reaction's whole commit and drops the activation; the reaction runs again. Same exposure as today's _etag.
  • In the simulator more subscribers are activated by nudges, including ones whose generated source must be restored first (InstallRestoreDeadlockFacts covers that path today).
  • Target code was not compiled and is written against R04 types that exist only as text. Marked assumptions: BrainLogs.TryOpen / Opened and BrainLog.OwnerOf; TestNeuronHost.Delivery(); the simulator's alias-to-type table; a grain call from a silo background thread (NeuronHosting.Get<T>(services, id) does it today).
  • D9 (catch-up awaited before the first call on Orleans, queued behind it in the simulator) is not closed here; it is a TestNeuronHost scheduling question for the conformance move.

Proof it worked

1) grep -rE "IStreamProvider|StreamId|PubSubStore|SignalReference|StreamNames|Orleans\.Streams" src returns nothing outside the inert RuntimeOptions / IntoChatOSNames members; neither Microsoft.Orleans.Streaming package is referenced. 2) Delivery, Subscriptions, Fanout exist once, in src/DigitalBrain/Kernel; TestSynapses.cs and SynapseEngine.cs are gone; the simulator's delivery file is about 100 lines. 3) New HostConformanceFacts pass on BOTH targets: a live lagging cursor pins the retention floor; a publish that lost its nudge is delivered by Reconcile without any further publish; with two publishers in one brain a duplicate nudge increments DuplicatesDropped and HistoryReads does not move; a re-install with fewer reactions drops the alias; 500 signals a subscriber does not want produce no nudge, no sweep nudge and no journal entry for it. 4) DeliveryFacts: a reaction that saves produces exactly one journal entry carrying state + signals + the subscriptions record; 1,000 passed-over signals in one catch-up produce one cursor-only entry, not 1,000. 5) The rehearsal on a copy of real brain storage reports, per brain, equal due sets before and after the upgrade for every per-source entry, 0 upgrades on the second run, and byte-identical v1 records. 6) 243 simulator facts, 269 silo facts and os OsHostConformanceFacts green, with the skip at os IntoChatOsTarget.cs:96-99 removed; the live aspire start gate shows one SubscriptionsUpgraded line per brain and the sweep's due count at 0 on an idle system.

Your decision

Rollback policy for the cursor upgrade. Recommended: new record name "subscriptions", v1 "synapses" left frozen, roll forward only after the first production day (a rollback re-runs every reaction since the deploy). Alternative: write both records for one release so rollback is exact, at the cost of carrying the publisher-sequence coordinate through the kernel for that release (about +25 lines, deleted next release). Second: confirm that a brain-wide subscription must never deliver a neuron its own signals (the public doc says so, today's code does it for non-state neurons). Third: confirm that a live lagging cursor may hold back retention for StaleCursorAfter in production (it never did on Orleans).

Review notes on this proposal

Quote check. 8 before-blocks in 4 pairs checked against the files at HEAD: SynapseEngine.cs:252-332 (three ranges), TestSynapses.cs:259-326 (three ranges), SynapseState.cs:7-26, SynapseEngine.cs:409-430, SignalPublisher.cs:44-74 (two ranges), SynapseEngine.cs:371-378, SynapseRetentionHosting.cs:28-32. All verbatim with correct line ranges; nothing had to be fixed. Line citations in the prose were also checked (BrainLog.cs:294 and :411-425, SynapseEngine.cs:107-109/215-224/413-415/422/506/512, TestSynapses.cs:63-66/122-130/179-187/378-380, ISynapses.cs:24, BrainSignalHistory.cs:73-75, ReactionDispatcher.cs:13,35); two were corrected (BrainLogs dictionary :37 -> :15; BrainJournalWriter :125-131 -> :127-131).

Corrections the reviewer made

  • Reconcile predicate replaced. The designer's SubscriptionIndex.Behind compared the durable cursor with the last position of what the subscription covers; with the alias prefilter a durable cursor trails whenever a covered publisher writes anything unwanted, so every sweep would wake (and keep activated) every subscriber of an active brain, with a read each and no flush. New SubscriptionIndex.Due: Gap owed, or a wanted alias after the durable cursor (per-publisher alias marks), or a lag of SkipFlush positions. A nudged catch-up that ran no reaction writes its cursor once; a duplicate nudge writes nothing (the designer forced a flush there).
  • GrainNudge no longer resolves the grain type through NeuronTypeRegistry (it returned when the subscriber's unit was not loaded, so such a subscriber was never woken; the stream subscription activates it today). It addresses the record owner key, which is the host grain id.
  • JournalCursorLookup took the journals in its constructor: BrainLogs -> IRetentionPolicy -> ISynapseCursorLookup -> BrainLogs is a DI cycle (BrainLogs.cs:22, RetentionPolicy.cs:6). Now resolves BrainLogs lazily and returns 0 (trim nothing) when the log is not open yet (BrainLog.cs:64 can trigger a checkpoint from the open path).
  • Rewrote the after code against R04 as its critique left it: IBrainJournals no longer exists, so the brain-level reads are a new port IJournalView (not members of the per-activation IBrainJournal); kernel StoredRecord is JournalRecord and kernel PublisherMark is PublisherRange; the upgrade writes through BrainLog.Append with the grain key from log.Named.
  • One wanted-rule by journal alias (Subscription.Wants) for fan-out, sweep and delivery; the encoded write path passes its aliases instead of waking every covering subscriber; PositionedSignal carries the journal alias.
  • Delivery made safe for reentrant hosts and the simulator: cursor moves go through one locked Move, a failed reaction calls Retreat(entry) instead of restoring a whole snapshot (which could drop a subscription made inside the reaction), Flush has one writer, catch-up is single flight.
  • Simulator: reaction failures are wired to os.Fail through DeliveryCounters.failed (the kernel's catch would have swallowed what TestSynapses.cs:267 surfaces); nudges of one subscriber are serialised so the second DuplicateDelivery copy counts as a duplicate; the alias-to-type table is per TestOS instance and must not hold collectible types.
  • On-disk change reduced from two members to one (FloorPosition); LastPosition is derivable. Stated the derivation rule correctly (0 when Floor is 0) and its one visible effect (a possible extra Gap for a brain-wide subscriber that is behind at the upgrade).
  • Open-time reconcile dropped (it would run before the log's open task is cached, where the nudge cannot find the owner); the sweep covers open brains. Said plainly that a brain nobody opened is not reconciled.
  • Deletes: removed RuntimeTestHooks.SubscriptionCount (it backs the public abstract FrameworkTarget.SubscriptionCount, asserted at HostConformanceFacts.cs:116 and overridden in os IntoChatOsTarget.cs:110); it is rewritten over the index. Profile lines corrected from 9 to 7. Partial total 56 -> 46.
  • Breaks added: TopologyValidator.ValidatePubSubStore is public; Bindings.Streams positional member changes the Bindings constructor when StreamsBinding goes; Need.Streams / NeedKind.Streams live in the core package; TestOS.Cursor changes unit (4 assertions); ContractManifest.json needs the nudge alias; RetentionOptions.ReconcileEvery is new public API; retention now honours live cursors in production; Context.Caller inside a reaction is defined as empty.
  • Counts corrected: os files calling Ensure are 6 (10 call sites), not 13; test files naming the deleted internal types are 12, not 13 (DeliveryContractFacts matched a string); BrainLogs dictionary is at :15, not :37.
  • Steps: added SynapseMutationFacts, AzuriteOrleansTarget, JournalJsonFacts, FakeTopology, NeedFacts, the DeliveryContractFacts term list (it pins ResumeAsync and ISynapseRegistryIndex), the manifest entry, the os skip removal (IntoChatOsTarget.cs:96-99), and the rule that steps 3 and 4 merge if a shared HostConformanceFact splits between them. Added the mixed-version warning to the os step.
  • Bugs: added two verified by reading (typed Ensure registration drift; static [On] cache without IsCollectible guard on Orleans). Marked B3 as costing reads only and already patched on the main path by R04. Added os's skipped conformance fact as evidence for B7.
  • Overstatement removed: 'a skipped signal no longer costs an fsync' narrowed; '13 defects' no longer claimed in netLoc; proof item '1,000 unwanted signals produce zero cursor-only entries' replaced by what actually holds; net src corrected from about -235 to about -65.
  • Typed Ensure keeps Orleans' refusal of an unregistered signal type (typeOf(alias) == signal) instead of silently falling back to the type name.
  • Effort kept at L but stated as the upper end (about 2 weeks) with the real file counts.

Doubts that remain

  • Nothing was compiled or run. The after code is written against R04 as its critique describes it; R04's final code exists only as text, so every signature shared with it (ReactionScope.Open, BrainLog.Append, KernelState's nudge call) is a reading, not a fact.
  • What Context.Caller is inside a reaction today is not established (stream pulling agent plus DigitalBrainCallFilter.cs:75-77). With a grain-call nudge it would be the publisher or the publisher's caller; the target clears it in HostConsumer. R07 (access/caller) should own this rule.
  • A grain call from a silo background thread (the sweep) is assumed to be allowed; orleans-facts.md does not state it. NeuronHosting.Get<T>(services, id) does the same from tests and os composition.
  • The brain-wide floor is the highest trimmed position of any publisher, including the subscriber itself, so a subscriber that trims its own history can get a spurious Gap; excluding the reader is one parameter on ReadAfter / FloorOf and was not added.
  • Coverage is by alias set: a brain-wide alias of a base type and a per-source alias of a derived type still deliver the same signal twice.
  • SynapseObservation.List skips the "*" entry (:57) while TestOS.Synapses lists it (TestSynapses.cs:156). With one index behind both, the move must pick one; os renders the Orleans list in its Creator graph (Kernel.cs:59-60), so I left each filter as it is. That is a remaining drift.
  • The alias-mark sweep and the per-append Hearing scan are linear in the brain's subscriptions; no number exists for how many subscriptions a production brain holds.
  • A neuron declared with [Neuron(State = "subscriptions")] would collide with the record name (none exists in either repo; "synapses" has the same exposure today). The registry should refuse both names.
  • Whether the 8 shared HostConformanceFacts stay green between step 3 (simulator on the kernel) and step 4 (Orleans on the kernel) was judged by reading two of them, not all eight.
  • B8 (local topology loses stream subscriptions on restart) and the brain-wide lost-signal defect were verified by reading only.

R06 · Kernel · wave 1

Resolve every call from one catalog built at registration

A public core ICallCatalog (CallCatalog / NeuronCalls / CallDescriptor), written once when a class is registered or a unit is deployed, replaces the 12 places that re-derive contract, method, scheduling, access and host kind from strings; os deletes its AppDomain scans, and a contracts-only client asks the silo once per neuron type instead of twice per call.

Net
src -61 (558 measured out, 497 est. in) / tests +264 (6 measured out, 270 est. in) / os -141 (158 measured out, 17 est. in). The gain is not lines: 12 string resolvers become 1 table, and 13 process-wide static dictionaries (9 db, 4 os) are deleted.
Effort
L (about 6 days): steps 1-4 are 3 to 4 days because two hosts and two filters change together with their test retargets; os bump A is a production deploy with its own tier run and the parity review; release B is 1 day and its os bump is two one-line implementations.
Depends on
R07 (soft, either order): shares OperationAccess; if R07 lands first its AccessGate moves from BoundCall to CallDescriptor in step 3, if R06 lands first R07 adds its declaring-interface step to OperationAccessOf
R03 (none, either order): NeuronHostGrainBase.Committed (:212) reads _class today and _calls.Class after; if R03 landed the line is gone
Review confidence
medium

What is wrong today

"Which method is this call, how is it scheduled, who may call it, which host runs it" is answered from strings in 12 places with 3 arity rules and 2 interleave rules. Contract name to Type is resolved 4 times (NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:321-322, TestNeuronHost.cs:185, PlainNeuronCallFilter.cs:131-141 and its copy os KernelInvocationSink.cs:69-79, the last two by scanning every loaded assembly). Method by name+arity is resolved 3 times: NeuronBinder.cs:146-150 excludes CancellationToken and includes inherited interfaces; PlainNeuronCallFilter.cs:127 includes the token and excludes inherited interfaces; os PlainInstanceRegistration.cs:83-90 reads the contract method only. Interleaving is decided by NeutralCallMapping.cs (68 lines) on Orleans and by CallMarkers (TurnScheduler.cs:25-66, 42 lines) in the simulator, and they disagree on [InterleavesWhen]. Host kind is recomputed per call with up to 2 uncached IsDefined (OrleansInvocationSink.cs:59, SynapseEngine.cs:337) although the registration holds it, and os rebuilds it by scanning every type of every assembly with the grain type names as string literals (KernelInvocationSink.cs:105-121). Per call today: 2 binds in-silo, 3 when the call interleaves (NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:151-155, 233-238), 3 grain round trips from a contracts-only client (OrleansInvocationSink.cs:63, 22, 24), through 12 process-wide static dictionaries in db and 4 in os.

Design principle

One concept, one model, one owner: the call (contract method + scheduling + access) and the hosted class (host kind, state name) are domain objects of the kernel, but today they have no type and each adapter and filter re-derives them from the wire strings, so the rules drifted. The target makes the call an immutable value (CallDescriptor) inside one aggregate per hosted class (NeuronCalls), built once at registration in the Orleans-free core; Orleans, the simulator, the filters and os only read it.

Defects this removes

  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Enforcement/PlainNeuronCallFilter.cs:127: the access lookup counts CancellationToken in the arity and skips inherited interface methods, while NeuronProxy.cs:108 never sends the token; a method-level access attribute on such a method is replaced by the contract's rule (latent: no such method found by grep).
  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Hosting/NeutralCallMapping.cs:32-33, 49 vs src/DigitalBrain.Testing/TestOS/TurnScheduler.cs:38, 45: Orleans and TestOS disagree on [InterleavesWhen] (private static predicate of a base class: found vs registration error; throwing predicate: no vs exception).
  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Hosting/NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:28, 322, NeutralCallMapping.cs:20-21, 30, src/DigitalBrain.Testing/TestOS/TestNeuronHost.cs:16, TurnScheduler.cs:30-31: process-wide static dictionaries keyed by the neuron class keep the closed StateNeuron<TRecord> of a live-deployed (collectible) unit for the life of the process once it takes one IViewReader/IStateReader call, against the rule written at NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:94-96.
  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Hosting/OrleansInvocationSink.cs:59 and Synapses/SynapseEngine.cs:337: PolicyOf runs up to 2 uncached IsDefined per call although the registration holds the policy; NeutralCallMapping.cs:29 a third.
  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Hosting/NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:151-155, 233-238: an interleaved call binds twice on the host and once on the caller.
  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Hosting/OrleansInvocationSink.cs:63, 22: a client without the classes pays 3 grain round trips per call and the host type is never cached (step 6: 1, or 2 for an [InterleavesWhen] class). No db test covers this branch: nothing in db implements INeuronHostCatalog.
  • os PlainInstanceRegistration.cs:83-90 reads [ReadOnlyCall] on the contract method only, while the scheduler reads contract or class (NeutralCallMapping.cs:57-67): two rules for one attribute. Latent: all 19 class-side [ReadOnlyCall] in both repos have a contract-side twin.
  • os KernelInvocationSink.cs:105, 120 and PlainInstanceRegistration.cs:66, 80: a type asked before its assembly is loaded caches null for the life of the process (latent).
  • NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:322 and TestNeuronHost.cs:185: with two interfaces of one wire name on a class, the first silently wins; the catalog refuses the class at registration.
  • src/DigitalBrain.Testing/TestOS/TurnScheduler.cs:63: OneWay is computed per call and discarded by all three callers (TestNeuronHost.cs:49, 56, 63).

Three arity rules and four name-to-contract resolvers become one table built at registration

The filter counted the CancellationToken and skipped inherited interfaces, so a method-level access attribute on such a method was replaced by the contract's rule. After, arity is one property and access is read from the MethodInfo that was bound. CallDescriptor is a sealed class with an internal constructor: nobody outside the core can make one, and a redeployed unit's table dies with its registration instead of sitting in a static dictionary.

The sink reads the descriptor; a process without the classes asks the silo once per type

Decision: the caller picks the [AlwaysInterleave] endpoint from the descriptor and the host checks the same descriptor; the doubled endpoints stay ([MayInterleave] is confirmed only with a static predicate). The probe IsInterleaved is NOT deleted: the one production [InterleavesWhen] class (os LlmNeuron.cs:95-96) runs on neuron.host, one activation, so a client without the class must still ask for it. Every other call from such a client goes from 3 grain round trips to 1.

The host: one lookup per endpoint, and the descriptor runs itself

Today an interleaved call binds three times (caller, RequireInterleaving, Invoke) through two static caches and a static binder. After, the caller and the host each do one dictionary lookup in the table of the registered class, and each filter that needs the method does one more (PlainNeuronCallFilter, os PlainInstanceRegistration): same table, no reflection, no second rule. Orleans and the simulator ask the same NeuronCalls.Interleaves. Side effect: Post now also logs a state conflict.

os deletes its AppDomain scans; the access filter reads the hosted class

os rebuilt the registry by scanning every type of every loaded assembly because NeuronTypeRegistry is internal, and cached null when it found nothing. With ICallCatalog public, KernelInvocationSink.cs disappears (its instance half has 0 constructions) and 4 static caches go. The filter uses TryResolve, not Resolve: a call naming no method is still answered with the access decision first, never with a BindingException that lists what exists.

Host keeps returning a tuple whose first element is Host, so the .Host reads compile unchanged.

  • Behaviour: a class with two interfaces under one wire name fails registration (today the first wins); in TestOS a throwing [InterleavesWhen] predicate means an ordinary turn and a private static predicate of a base is found; an access attribute on a contract method that has a CancellationToken or is inherited is now honoured; os PlainInstanceRegistration treats a class-side [ReadOnlyCall] as read-only; a stream call that names no method fails in the os stream authorizer instead of in the host; Post logs a state conflict.
  • Not changed: the RuntimeInvocation envelope, NeuronProxy (still reads [OneWayCall] and [CallTimeout] on the caller), IInvocationSink, every INeuronHost method including IsInterleaved, the three grain type names, grain ids, persisted state, the [InterleavesWhen] predicate shape static bool P(RuntimeInvocation).

Risks

  • Startup cost: every interface method of every hosted class is described at registration (attributes plus one interface map per interface). Invokers compile on first call, so it is reflection only. Not measured.
  • A class that today starts with two same-named contract interfaces stops the silo at registration. Not measured whether one exists in os; any os tier that starts the composed silo shows it before deploy.
  • Step 4 changes what an access attribute on an inherited or token-bearing contract method means. A grep over both repos' src finds no access attribute directly above a signature with a CancellationToken; inherited methods were not counted. The temporary AccessRuleParityFacts at os bump A is the check.
  • The filter reads the catalog's current class for the target type while an activation may still run the class it was built from until Reload. Only units are replaced live, and their contracts (IViewReader, IStateReader, ISynapseReceiver, IStateInspectable) are the same for every record.
  • Step 6: a client caches routes per neuron type for its lifetime. After a silo deploy that changes a method's scheduling, a call sent to the interleaved endpoint is refused once (BindingException), the type is forgotten and the next call is right; a method that became interleaved keeps taking a turn until the client restarts. A one-way Post gets no refusal back.
  • Step 6/7 order: a release B client against a release A silo fails on Routes. An A client against a B silo works (HostType and IsInterleaved remain).
  • R07, as re-based by its critique, puts Access on BoundCall. If R07 lands first, step 3 of this move re-points its AccessGate from BoundCall to CallDescriptor (same two members: Contract, Access) and step 1 adds nothing under src/DigitalBrain/Access.
  • Not verified in orleans-facts.md: whether an incoming call filter can run for a host whose OnActivateAsync threw. The filter tolerates an unparsable key (TryParse) either way.

Proof it worked

After step 4: grep -rnE "NeutralCallMapping|CallMarkers|NeuronBinder|BoundCall|PolicyOf\(" over digitalbrain/src returns nothing; grep "AppDomain.CurrentDomain" over digitalbrain/src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Enforcement returns nothing. After step 5: os KernelInvocationSink.cs does not exist and PlainInstanceRegistration.cs has no AppDomain.CurrentDomain. Counts: name-to-contract resolvers on the call path 4 -> 1, name+arity rules 3 -> 1, interleave rules 2 -> 1, host-kind derivations 2 -> 1, process-wide static dictionaries on the call path db 12 -> 3 (NeuronProxy.ContractNames, NeuronProxy.Methods, TaskBoxing.Boxes) and os 4 -> 0. Tests: CallCatalogFacts (9 new facts), the 2 new TurnFacts and the 2 new PlainNeuronCallFilterFacts green; TurnFacts, InterleavingFacts, InterleavedAccessFacts (its 7-row policy theory unchanged, probe included), RoutingLifecycleFacts, ProbeCallShapeFacts, PlainNeuronCallFilterFacts and the os Kernel Unit/E2E tiers green; os AccessRuleParityFacts lists every call whose access changed (expected: none). After step 6 the request-count fact shows 1 host request per in-silo call, 1 per call without classes after the first to a type, 2 for an [InterleavesWhen] class. HostCostFacts (Explicit) run before step 1 and after step 3, both tables kept.

Your decision

Does os deploy the silo before its cluster clients? If clients can run ahead of the silo, release B must keep a fallback to HostType + IsInterleaved when Routes is missing (about 10 more lines) or os bump B must stop the clients first. Second: accept that step 4 honours an access attribute written on an inherited or token-bearing contract method (the author's stated intent) once the parity fact has listed the affected calls, or keep the legacy lookup for one release.

Review notes on this proposal

Quote check. 8 before-snippets in 4 code pairs compared line by line with the files at HEAD (digitalbrain a275f82, os 7a28188b3): all 8 are verbatim and their line ranges are right (PlainNeuronCallFilter.cs:124-141; NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:320-330, 131-155, 233-238; OrleansInvocationSink.cs:16-25, 48-67; os KernelInvocationSink.cs:105-121; os PlainInstanceRegistration.cs:83-90). Nothing in the snippets needed fixing. The designer measured os at 5c18b3eb5; the cited os files are unchanged since 6c70e4c50 and were re-read. Fixed outside the snippets: SynapseEngine.cs:338 -> :337, NeuronBinderFacts.cs:166-170 -> :160-172, RoutingLifecycleFacts.cs:62 is a filter condition, not an assertion.

Corrections the reviewer made

  • Kept INeuronHost.IsInterleaved instead of deleting it. The designer's premise was wrong: the one production [InterleavesWhen] class is not hosted stateless (os LlmNeuron.cs:95-96 has [Neuron("ai.llm")] and no [StatelessNeuron]; StatelessAiNeuron is only a base without state), so it has one activation on neuron.host and a contracts-only Generate sent through the ordinary endpoint would queue. NeuronRoutes now carries a Probe flag and the client probes only for such a class (3 round trips become 1, or 2 there). Title line, oneLine, code pair 2, steps 6-7, breaks, risks and the open decision rewritten accordingly; the 5 IsInterleaved test edits, the INeuronHost change and the os edits at PlainInstanceRegistration.cs:34-36 / KernelTestHooks.cs:67,74 are gone.
  • Sink Host no longer resolves the method. RuntimeTestHooks.cs:53 asks for a host with an empty contract and method and 4 test sites read .Host of the tuple; the designer's Host called neuron.Resolve and would throw there, and made Stream bind on the caller. Host now returns (INeuronHost Host, NeuronCalls? Neuron); Invoke/Post resolve.
  • Caller-side interleave decision now uses the stamped invocation, as today (OrleansInvocationSink.cs:20-23); the designer's used the unstamped call while the host sees the stamped one.
  • PlainNeuronCallFilter uses TryResolve, not Resolve. The designer's Resolve at the top of Invoke returned a BindingException naming methods to an unauthenticated caller before the caller check; today that caller gets UnauthorizedAccessException. IsPlumbing stays contract-level (it never needed the method), over the hosted class's contracts. Added a fact for it.
  • CallDescriptor changed from a public positional record to a sealed class with an internal constructor (a with-expression would leave shape/arity of the old method; a public constructor lets any code fabricate Access = Public for the public Authorize). CallCatalog.Register/Unregister made internal; the core already has InternalsVisibleTo for Runtime.Orleans and Testing (DigitalBrain.csproj:18-23).
  • Removed Timeout, OneWay, NeuronType and the Invoker constructor argument from CallDescriptor: no reader in this move (NeuronProxy keeps both attributes caller-side; CallMarkers' OneWay is discarded by all three callers today).
  • OperationAccessOf in this move is 'bound method, then named contract' (today's rule without its two defects). The designer imported R07's extra 'declaring interface' step, which can widen an owner-rule call; that is R07's decision. Also noted that R07's critique re-based R07 on BoundCall, so the dependency text changed.
  • Explicit duplicate-wire-name check with a message naming the class and both interfaces (the designer relied on ToFrozenDictionary throwing a bare ArgumentException); one GetInterfaceMap per interface instead of one per method.
  • Corrected the os read-only claim: all 19 class-side [ReadOnlyCall] (18 os, 1 db) have a same-named contract-side [ReadOnlyCall] (IRoslyn and IChatIndex checked in the file), so the 'stops costing a storage read per call' benefit and the matching risk were removed; the defect is kept as a latent rule mismatch.
  • Re-measured LOC: NeuronHostGrainBase 21 deleted + 33 rewritten as about 24 (the designer's '32 to 10' left out Run); PlainNeuronCallFilter 30 not 33; step 6 deletion of 14 lines dropped; os PlainInstanceRegistration 27 not 30; net src about -61 instead of -99, os -141 instead of -145.
  • Corrected counts and line references: SynapseEngine.cs:337 (not :338); HostPolicy 4 src references (not 5); NeuronRegistration 17 references (not 16); KernelTestHooks.HostOf has 2 callers, both internal (not 3); the cache-miss fact is NeuronBinderFacts.cs:160-172; NeuronBinder/BoundCall are in PublicAPI.Unshipped.txt (14 lines), not Shipped; static dictionaries db 12 -> 3 (the designer wrote 9 -> 3).
  • Added missed breaks: SandboxConformanceFacts.cs:127-137 dispatcher uses NeuronBinder and PlainNeuronCallFilter.ContractOf; HostActivationFacts.cs:69 reads NeuronRegistration.Contracts; the registry's three grain-type constants have 5 src + 13 test uses (kept as aliases so nothing moves); DigitalBrainCallFilter has no constructor and is registered by the public AddDigitalBrainCallFilter, so it takes an optional catalog; TestOS keeps its ArgumentException duplicate checks (TestOS.cs:197, 550) in front of the catalog.
  • Added verified defects the designer missed: static class-keyed caches hold a collectible unit's StateNeuron<TRecord> for the process lifetime (NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:28, NeutralCallMapping.cs:20-21, TestNeuronHost.cs:16, TurnScheduler.cs:30-31); no db type implements INeuronHostCatalog, so the contracts-only sink branch has no db test; CallMarkers computes a OneWay nobody reads.
  • Steps: dropped the cross-runtime SchedulingParityFacts (both runtimes now call one method and db has no two-runtime fixture) in favour of 2 TurnFacts; added a temporary os AccessRuleParityFacts at bump A because step 4 changes an access rule nobody has measured in os; step 6 adds a test INeuronHostCatalog grain; steps 6-7 are now additive on the wire.
  • Closed the designer's ASSUMPTION about the [property: Id(n)] idiom: it is verbatim at Synapses/GateVerdict.cs:11-16. Removed the risk about the instance [MayInterleave] alternative as not relevant to the proposal as written (it stays out, per orleans-facts.md section 7).

Doubts that remain

  • Nothing was built or run. The after-code was checked by reading against the types that remain; CallDescriptor/NeuronCalls bodies marked 'moved' were not re-typed in full.
  • The class-side [ReadOnlyCall] check is a name match (19 of 19 have a same-named contract-side twin); 12 were confirmed in the contract files (IRoslyn 10, IChatIndex 2), 7 were not opened.
  • 'No access attribute on a token-bearing contract method' rests on one multiline grep over both repos' src (attribute directly above or on the signature line). Inherited contract methods with an access attribute were not counted; the temporary os parity fact is what closes this.
  • Whether an Orleans incoming call filter can run for a grain whose OnActivateAsync threw is not in orleans-facts.md; the filter code tolerates it either way.
  • NeuronCalls carries StateName and IsUnit, which are registration facts rather than call facts. It is what lets os drop its scans through one public type, but the name undersells it; a rename to something like HostedNeuron is a judgment call I left to the owner.
  • Whether any os cluster client calls ILLM (the one [InterleavesWhen] class) was not measured; the design no longer depends on the answer.
  • FrozenDictionary.ToFrozenDictionary(keySelector) throwing on duplicate keys is from memory; the final code does not rely on it (explicit check first).
  • Startup cost of describing every interface method of every hosted class is not measured (the designer's figure of 141 neuron classes in os was not re-counted and is no longer quoted).

R07 · Access · wave 1

Make one Access context with an unforgeable Caller

Replace the flat, publicly constructible CapabilityCaller, its seven RequestContext keys and seven "may this caller touch this brain" implementations with a closed Actor hierarchy, a Caller only the kernel and the host's EdgeAuthority can make or re-enter, one carrier (public read port ICallerAmbient, internal write port ICallerCarrier) and one pure decision (AccessPolicy, run by AccessGate) in the host pipeline of both runtimes, shipped first with today's answers and no others.

Net
src -992 (measured) +639 (est.) = about -350 after release 2 (release 1 carries +65 est. of temporary shim code) / tests -394 (measured) +430 (est.) = about +36, plus 9 of the 13 facts of CallFilterAndReminderStressFacts.cs (366 lines) rewritten / os -134 (measured: 39 + 15 + 61 + 19) +55 (est.), -146 more (measured) if the dead stage stack is deleted, and est. -100 across the 51 same-brain lines, 71 stamps and 14 key removals. Not a line-count move: it removes 4 of 5 answers, 6 of 7 identity keys and every way for code to name itself the platform.
Effort
L, at the top of the band (two weeks of work plus calendar time between the releases): digitalbrain steps 1-5 about 5 days, because step 4 turns enforcement on for every silo and rewrites three test files and the kit; os bump 1 about 2 days with the live gate; os migration of 94 non-test files, one persisted field, one wire record and at least 9 contract methods about 5 days; release 2 plus os bump 2 about 2 days; and the counters must be observed in production between bump 1 and release 2.
Depends on
None hard. R06 (one call catalog) is no longer needed: the gate takes today's BoundCall, which gets an internal Access; R06 absorbs that field when it lands
Soft: R03 (delete the two-phase gate) removes Synapses/InstalledGates.cs, one of the two platform StampScope sites; either order works
Soft: the DigitalBrain.Simulation package split (judgment move 9) removes the os production reference to DigitalBrain.Testing, which closes one path to a test EdgeAuthority
Review confidence
medium

What is wrong today

Identity is carried twice and decided many times. Measured now: the five db.cap.* keys are declared in 4 places (RuntimeCallerContext.cs:18,24-27; CallerAdmission.cs:8-12; literals DigitalBrainCallFilter.cs:67-71; literals os RuntimeCallerMirror.cs:17); the Orleans-free core reflects into Orleans.Runtime.RequestContext (CallerAdmission.cs:96-110) while its own port CallerAdmission.Reader (:16) is assigned nowhere; the caller's Kind is derived from three magic AppId strings (CapabilityCaller.cs:17-26). There are 150 ambient reads (db 27, os 123), 186 explicit CapabilityCaller parameters (db 94 in 22 files, os 92 in 40) and 106 stamp sites (db 34 + 1, os 69 + 2 through a global-using alias); 31 of the 34 db stamps exist only so the kernel passes its own filter (29 Stamp(PlatformCaller) + 2 StampScope(Platform(brain, brain, brain))), OrleansInstallHooks.cs:76-83 forges db.caller for the same reason, and os drops its identity 14 times with RequestContext.Remove("db.cap.principal") because "no stamp from a grain" passes for any brain. "May this caller touch this brain" has 7 implementations in digitalbrain giving 5 different answers for a Platform caller, a Scheduler caller and a missing caller (access-identity.md section 3). The one class that reads the access attributes (PlainNeuronCallFilter) is registered only by os, is absent from TestOS, resolves the attribute by name and argument count (:124-129) and exempts every contract-level [Internal] or non-public contract from all checks, stamped or not (:40-44,121-122); and because DigitalBrainCallFilter.cs:47-53 runs for every stamped caller and reads no attribute, [Public] (18 os uses, among them IOrganisation.Rename, ChangeRole, RemoveMember) and [Shared] (2) have no effect today. Anything can call the public static Stamp with a publicly constructed CapabilityCaller, and 10 db and at least 9 os neuron contract methods accept the caller as a call argument (IVault, IDirectory; LocalVaultPort.cs:194-198 fabricates the one it sends), so no check that reads either is a defence against in-process code.

Design principle

A bounded context owns its model and its invariants in one place; here the Access concepts are spread over 8 namespaces in 4 assemblies, the rule is written in two filters, five helper methods and 35 os files, and the central value object is a primitive-obsessed string record whose meaning is re-derived and whose instances anyone may fabricate or pass as an argument. The target puts one value object (Caller, constructible and enterable only by the kernel and the edge authority), one closed set of actors, one driven port for the carrier and one pure domain function for the decision in the core, with Orleans RequestContext and the simulator's AsyncLocal as adapters, so the rule is written once and both runtimes run the same code.

Defects this removes

  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Enforcement/PlainNeuronCallFilter.cs:124-129: the access attribute is looked up by (contract name, method name, argument count including CancellationToken), first match, cached, while the binder excludes the token, sees inherited interfaces and picks overloads by argument type. Latent (0 overloads, 0 tokens on attributed methods today, access-identity.md M10). Removed: BoundCall.Access is read from the bound MethodInfo.
  • PlainNeuronCallFilter.cs:40-44,121-122 and Hosting/DigitalBrainCallFilter.cs:33-38: a contract-level [Internal] (documented "never from an edge caller", AccessAttributes.cs:21) or non-public contract is exempt from both filters, so an unstamped client or a caller of another brain reaches it. Counted in release 1, decided by the one policy in release 2.
  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Hosting/RuntimeSiloBuilderExtensions.cs:97-99: a silo built with AddDigitalBrainRuntime alone never registers PlainNeuronCallFilter, so access attributes are not enforced there.
  • TestOS enforces no access attribute: 0 references to DigitalBrain.Access from src/DigitalBrain.Testing and src/DigitalBrain.Testing.Silo. After: the same AccessGate object.
  • src/DigitalBrain/Brains/CallerAdmission.cs:16: the Reader port is assigned nowhere; :96-110 reflects into Orleans.Runtime.RequestContext from the Orleans-free core instead.
  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Synapses/OrleansInstallHooks.cs:76-83: service code writes db.caller as the installs neuron's id to pass NeuronHostGrainBase.InstallsOnly (:264-275); any in-silo code can do the same. After: the check is on Actor.Kernel, which only the kernel can make.
  • src/DigitalBrain.Identity.Local/Vault/LocalVaultPort.cs:194-198 with Vault/VaultNeuron.cs:118-123: the port fabricates a User caller and a Platform caller out of whoever called and sends it as a JSON argument; the neuron authorizes on that argument's Kind. Release 2: identity travels as data and is trusted only under a Kernel caller.
  • src/DigitalBrain/Capabilities/CapabilityCaller.cs:141 with :20, and os Modules.Apps/Hosting/HostCaller.cs:50 (caller with { AppId = appKey }): an app id "platform", "scheduler" or "assistant" yields that Kind; only CapabilityCaller.App (:71) guards the reserved ids, and Platform is exempt at DigitalBrainCallFilter.cs:48 and CallFilter.cs:35. Whether os validates app keys elsewhere was not checked. After release 2 an app is Actor.App whatever its key.
  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Observation/BrainObserver.cs:11,80-82: IObservedCaller has no implementer in either repo; ClusterBrainObserver.Instances, Brains, Synapses and Deliveries can only throw.
  • src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Capabilities/RuntimeCallerContext.cs:102-108: IsTrusted cannot be false for a caller read from the ambient (Current already returns null on a blank, :39); :65-69 reports "Caller kind User cannot be stamped by AuthenticatedHttp" for a blank field.
  • PlainNeuronCallFilter.cs:58: NeuronId.Parse throws FormatException for a system-scope key that DigitalBrainCallFilter.cs:49 explicitly exempts (latent: no production code creates a system-scope neuron).
  • PlainNeuronCallFilter.cs:131-141: ContractOf does not cache a miss, so an unknown contract name scans every loaded assembly's types on every call; the static cache it does keep holds Type objects of whatever assembly answered first.
  • Hosting/DigitalBrainCallFilter.cs:64-72: the outgoing filter reads the caller from RequestContext and writes the same values to the same keys (a no-op).
  • os Kernel/BrainHosting.cs:43 with IntoChat.OS.Aspire.Server/ServerHostingExtensions.cs:299: the production silo runs the call filter with zero stages and no bindings (GrantCallFilterStage.cs:6 and ConnectionCallBindings.cs:9 implement os interfaces of the same name that nothing resolves), while startup insists that such a stage is registered. R07 removes the dead wiring; whether grant rules run on calls is decision 2.

Who is calling: magic strings and reflection become a closed Actor, a Caller nobody else can make, and one carrier

Kind, StampedBy and "trusted" stop being computed from strings: the actor is a type, and the only public factory is EdgeAuthority (internal constructor, handed once to the composition root). Entering a caller is internal too, so a neuron cannot replay a Caller it once saw. The core no longer knows Orleans exists; the adapter uses only RequestContext.Get/Set/Remove and a [GenerateSerializer] value. For one release RuntimeCallerContext and CapabilityCaller remain as projections of Caller, so none of this is a defence until release 2.

Two filters and a neuron that disagree become one pure decision that gives today's joint answer

Before: a filter that skips the check when nothing is stamped and exempts Platform, and a neuron that sometimes requires a stamp and does not exempt Platform; CanTouchBrain (CapabilityCaller.cs:50-60) gives a third answer and denies Scheduler outright. After: 15 lines that both runtimes execute and that answer exactly as the two filters jointly answer today (step 1 pins it); neurons keep only rules about their own aggregate, written as Actor patterns. The six conjuncts of "a human, directly" (:75-78) become one pattern, and "directly" stays physical because Direct includes not Inside.

The kernel stops impersonating the platform, and callers stop travelling as forgeable arguments

29 Stamp(PlatformCaller) blocks in 4 capability classes, 2 StampScope(Platform(brain, brain, brain)) and one forged db.caller exist because kernel code calls its own neurons under the user's stamp and its own filter would refuse. After, the code that crosses the boundary says Kernel once, in the proxy it uses. The 29 blocks can only go in release 2: os port implementations must first call their neurons as the kernel, and the 10 db and at least 9 os contract methods that take a CapabilityCaller argument must first take plain identity data.

The decision moves from an Orleans filter only os registers into the host pipeline of both runtimes

Before: a contract-level [Internal] returns before any check, a missing stamp means "allowed" or "denied" by Orleans source type, the attribute is found by name and argument count, and the filter exists only on a silo os builds. After: the host that bound the method asks one gate, and TestOS asks the same gate. The exemption is kept for one release, counted, keyed on the contract exactly as IsPlumbing is today: IMarketplace (contract-level [Internal], [Public] List and Read, one neuron at system/main read by every brain) works only through it.

What breaks

  • Release 1, public API removed: namespace DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans.Enforcement (ICallFilter, CallFilter, ICallFilterStage, ICallBindings, PlainNeuronCallFilter): os non-test 4 uses in 1 file (BrainHosting.cs:6,43,46,52-56), os test ProductHostFixture.cs:102-103 plus 6 test files naming PlainNeuronCallFilter; db tests 2 files. SiloNeurons.AuthorizeStreams: os 1 use (BrainHosting.cs:44). AddDigitalBrainCallFilter: 0 uses outside its own file.
  • Release 1, constant RuntimeCallerContext.RequestContextKey removed on purpose (a surviving Remove of the old key would compile and silently stop dropping the caller): os non-test 22 sites in 9 files (KernelGrains 2, SpaceNeuron 3, AppNeuron.Installation 7, FeatureBoot 1, FilesHost 2, FilesWorkspaceNeuron 2, WorkspaceFilesService 1, AppPresentationSink 1, ConnectionBindings 3), os test 3; db src 3 in 2 files, db tests 1 (the earlier count of 13 in db src included two trace-key constants of the same name).
  • Release 1, constant RuntimeCallerContext.ModuleCallKey removed: os non-test 4 sites (CloudHost.cs:156-170), os test 1; db src 7 in 3 files, db tests 4.
  • Release 1, CallerContextStamper removed: os 0 real uses (AppsModule.cs:3 is a global-using alias of that name for RuntimeCallerContext; the test hits are method names and strings); db tests 11 in 1 file. PerAccountAttribute removed: 1 use (PlainNeuronCallFilter.cs:94, deleted with it) and 1 test InlineData (LegacyPureTypeFacts.cs:16). IHostNeuronContext.Caller (internal string) removed: db tests 4 in 2 files.
  • Release 1, behaviour on a silo without os's registrations (templates, examples, any third-party host): AddDigitalBrainRuntime now enforces access; an edge call with no caller is refused unless the host mints one through its EdgeAuthority or sets AccessOptions.UnstampedEdgeIsKernel. The db HTTP edge (DigitalBrain.Runtime.Http) stamps nothing today (0 stamp sites).
  • Release 1, behaviour that changes for os (the four marked matrix rows): neuron code under an unstamped grain call sees Actor.Kernel instead of no caller, so ConfirmationsNeuron.Read answers it (today: "A trusted caller is required") and BrainNeuron.Announce accepts a Platform stamp (today: any stamp is refused); a Platform caller of another brain passes ConfirmationsNeuron and ConnectionNeuron.RequireMember; os tests that set db.caller to "" (5 sites) no longer affect anything.
  • Release 1, RequestContext wire: one key db.access.caller replaces db.cap.principal/account/brain/app/conversation, db.caller and intochatos.trusted-module-call; the value is a type of DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans, so a separate Orleans client process must reference that package and be deployed together with the silo. One silo replica per topology (judgment.md 2.3, not re-measured).
  • Release 1, source-compatible but re-pointed: RuntimeCallerContext (os non-test 232 occurrences in 94 files, os test 100 in 45), CallerAdmission (os 7 in 6 files), BrainScope.CurrentId (os 63 in 31 files), CapabilityCaller parameters (db 94 in 22 files, os 92 in 40) keep compiling over the new carrier. Not marked [Obsolete]: os builds with TreatWarningsAsErrors and does not suppress CS0618. A stamp whose BrainId is not a valid BrainId now throws at Stamp (today: any non-blank string).
