Principal, alias apps.state2), written at AppNeuron.cs:111 and AppNeuron.Installation.cs:40 and replayed at :174,200. The type is deleted; os must keep a record with the same five property names (PrincipalId, AccountId, BrainId, AppId, ConversationId) and re-mint through its EdgeAuthority. digitalbrain itself persists no caller.
  • Release 2, wire records and call arguments: os Framework/Callers/CallRequest.cs:20 ([Id(0)] CapabilityCaller Caller, alias enforcement.call-request) crosses a grain call through CapabilityCallerSurrogate; neuron contracts take the caller as an argument: db IVault 4 methods and IDirectory 6 methods, os VaultNeuron and DirectoryNeuron contracts, typed grains IBrainEntries.Release (:42) and IIntegration.Release (:25). None of these can take a Caller.

  • Release 2, behaviour: a call with no caller from a grain is refused (today: allowed for any brain); contract-level [Internal] and non-public contracts lose their exemption. At least 8 contract-level [Internal] interfaces exist (os IMarketplace, ICircleChat.cs:26,36, InviteCodeNeuron.cs:14,62; db IConnectionIndex.cs:10,26, IBrainAnnouncer.cs:10) plus the internal interfaces; IMarketplace is read cross-brain by design and needs decision 1 before this release.
  • Kept, not broken: CallRequest / CallDecision / CallDenial in DigitalBrain.Capabilities.Identity and their os namesakes in IntoChat.OS.Framework (the grants@1 path, 11 os non-test files), the access attributes [Public] [Shared] [Internal] [Shareable], DigitalBrainCallFilter as a public class (trace half only), INeuronHost [Version(1)], the constructors of the four capability classes (the carrier is an optional last argument).

Risks

  • os behaviour at bump 1. The policy is written to give today's joint answer and step 1 pins it on the Orleans side, but os's 51 neuron-level checks and 71 stamps are covered only by os's own tests and the live aspire start gate; the owner's notes say the test tiers already missed live defects.
  • Release 1 is still forgeable: RuntimeCallerContext.Stamp(CapabilityCaller) is public until release 2, and a stamp whose AppId is "platform" still reads as the kernel through the shim. If the os migration (step 7: 94 non-test files, one persisted field, at least 9 contract methods) stalls, the project has one more model beside the old one. Do not start R07 without committing to step 8.
  • What "unforgeable" means: no public constructor, no init accessor, not serializable, not enterable without the authority. It does not hold against reflection, against an assembly named like a friend assembly (InternalsVisibleTo is by name, no strong names), against IntoChat.OS.Kernel.Composition (it has InternalsVisibleTo on DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans and can write the wire value), or against a TestOS EdgeAuthority in a process that references DigitalBrain.Testing (os Modules.Apps does, in production).
  • The Orleans gateway stays a trusted edge: a process that can connect as a client can send the one key, exactly as it can send the five db.cap.* keys today. CallerEdgeFilter only guarantees that a client never arrives as Inside or with a Via.
  • Stream calls: whether RequestContext is present when the body of an IAsyncEnumerable grain method starts is not confirmed in orleans-facts.md (ASSUMPTION), and no incoming filter runs for a host grain's stream, so Inside is never raised there and an unstamped stream from a grain is refused where an unstamped Invoke is the implicit kernel. A spike before step 4; fallback: gate in OrleansInvocationSink.Stream as today (:42).
  • Direct = no neuron hop and not sent by a grain. This reproduces today only if IOutgoingGrainCallContext.SourceId is null on a non-grain thread of the silo (the HTTP edge), which the repo relies on at DigitalBrainCallFilter.cs:75 but orleans-facts.md does not state. Step 1 pins it; if it is false, approvals from the os edge are already non-direct today.
  • LegacyCallers.Project(Read(stamp)) is not the identity for Platform and Scheduler stamps: Platform("identity.vault", "system", "system") reads back with principal and account "system". Code that logs or compares a platform principal sees the change; SessionSynapseNeuron.cs:81 compares two projections and is unaffected.
  • TestOS runs a call as the kernel when the fact entered nobody, so existing facts pass; that is a deliberate difference from Orleans. The os deploy gate (digitalbrain UnitScenario.cs:52, called from os BuilderTools.cs:81) exercises the access rules only once UnitScenario enters a person.
  • The kernel is exempt everywhere, and in release 1 every unstamped grain call is the kernel (counted). Code that wants less than the kernel must mint Module or Scheduler.
  • The 29 Stamp(PlatformCaller) blocks cannot be deleted before os port implementations call their neurons as the kernel: doing it earlier compiles and then fails at run time with OutsideBrain. That is why they are in release 2.

Proof it worked

AccessPolicyFacts (core unit, no runtime): one row per cell of the decision table, plus "an unknown Actor subtype is denied". CallerFacts: typeof(Caller).GetConstructors() is empty, no property has a public setter or init, JsonSerializer.Deserialize<Caller> throws, EdgeAuthority has no public constructor, ICallerAmbient has no Enter. AccessMatrixFacts from step 1 passes unchanged after step 3 and after step 4 except the four marked rows; it contains the [Public]-from-another-brain, method-[Internal]-from-another-brain, unstamped-client-to-exempt-contract and service-proxy-is-not-direct rows. AccessConformance (kit in DigitalBrain.Testing.Silo): the same matrix passes on the Orleans fixture and on TestOS; it is the first test that fails when the two runtimes answer an access question differently. OrleansCallerAmbientFacts: the six actors round-trip CallerWire; a client that sends Via and Inside arrives with neither. An os fact reads a stored apps.state2 document with a Principal through the os-owned record before bump 2. Grep facts after release 2, both repos, non-test: "db\.cap\." 0 (today 15 + 5); RuntimeCallerContext\. 0 (74 + 232); CallerAdmission\. 0 (4 + 7); Stamp\(PlatformCaller\) 0 (29); \bCapabilityCaller\b 0 (136 + 136); identity RequestContext.Get/Set/Remove in digitalbrain only in OrleansCallerAmbient. Production: the counters access.implicit_kernel, access.would_deny and access.legacy_stamp read zero before release 2 is cut.

Your decision

Four, in order of weight. (1) [Public] (18 os uses, among them IOrganisation.Rename, ChangeRole, RemoveMember and IOrganisationInvites.InviteMembers) and [Shared] (2: ICircleChat.PublishPost, ITable) have no effect today, because DigitalBrainCallFilter.cs:47-53 refuses a caller of another brain before any attribute is read. R07 keeps "same brain" for both in 0.19. Before 0.20: give [Public] its documented meaning (any authenticated caller, any brain) after reading those 18 methods one by one, or delete it; give [Shared] a meaning or delete it. IMarketplace needs the answer, because the contract-level exemption it lives on ends in 0.20. (2) The os grant stage and bound-connection rule have not run on grain calls since they were registered against os interfaces nothing resolves. Delete that stack (146 lines, 9 test files, and the startup check at ServerHostingExtensions.cs:299), which is what production does today and my recommendation, or revive it as a stage of the gate, which is new enforcement in production and needs its own audit release. (3) Who in os may hold the EdgeAuthority: one internal holder for the real edges and the kernel composition, or a service any compiled module can resolve (then every module can mint the kernel). (4) Ticks and reminders run as Kernel in R07 (today's "no stamp is trusted"); narrow them to Scheduler once access.implicit_kernel shows which ticks cross brains?

Corrections the reviewer made

  • AccessPolicy rewritten to give today's JOINT answer of the two filters. The designer's policy claimed to reproduce today's answers but widened three cells: [Public] from another brain (refused today by DigitalBrainCallFilter.cs:47-53, which reads no attribute; 18 os uses including IOrganisation.Rename/ChangeRole/RemoveMember), method-level [Internal] from a grain of another brain, and Module + [Internal] across brains. New finding stated: [Public] has no effect today, like [Shared]; its meaning is now open decision 1.
  • Audit exemption re-keyed on the CONTRACT (today's IsPlumbing predicate on the bound contract Type) and extended to the no-caller case. The designer's gate threw 'no caller' for an unstamped client call to a contract-level [Internal] contract before reaching the audit branch (a production break at os bump 1 with no counter), and its predicate would have refused [Public]/unmarked methods of IMarketplace under any policy that keeps the brain rule.
  • Caller.Direct changed to 'Via is null && !Inside'. DigitalBrainCallFilter.cs:74-78 fills db.caller from the source grain and CallFilterAndReminderStressFacts.cs:158 pins it, so 'direct' is already physical today; the designer's logical Via would have let a grain using SiloNeurons.Get under a person's caller approve a confirmation as 'a human, directly'. Designer's bugsFixed item 14 (service-level proxy clears db.caller and looks direct) removed as false.
  • Added the two db.caller writers the designer missed: SynapseEngine.cs:338-345 and OrleansInstallHooks.cs:76-83 (the latter forges the installs neuron's id; added to bugsFixed). Deleting the key without converting them breaks every subscription and install. 23 measured lines added to deletes.
  • Found and added: a Caller cannot replace CapabilityCaller where it is a neuron call ARGUMENT (db IVault 4 + IDirectory 6 methods; os VaultNeuron, DirectoryNeuron, IBrainEntries.Release, IIntegration.Release). Release-2 steps now give these identity data (Actor.Person) trusted only under a Kernel caller. Added the related defect LocalVaultPort.cs:194-198 / VaultNeuron.cs:118-123 (fabricated caller argument) to bugsFixed and to code pair 3.
  • 'Nothing persisted changes' removed: os AppState.cs:24 persists a CapabilityCaller ([Id(12)], apps.state2) and os CallRequest.cs:20 serializes one through CapabilityCallerSurrogate. Added to breaks, steps 7 and 9, proof and adds.
  • os Framework/Callers/ is no longer deleted as a folder: CallRequest/CallDecision/CallDenial there are live on the grants@1 path (11 non-test files). Only the dead stage stack (146 measured lines incl. GrantCallFilterStage.cs and ConnectionCallBindings.cs) is deletable, and only by owner decision; found the startup check ServerHostingExtensions.cs:299 that requires an os ICallFilterStage. os bump 1 no longer registers any stage (the designer's re-registration was new enforcement in a production deploy).
  • IAccessStage, AccessRequest and IAccessPolicy removed from the core design (zero implementers and one implementer). AccessGate is now synchronous, takes today's BoundCall (new internal BoundCall.Access), and returns the caller the host enters; the designer's async gate could not change the ambient for its caller and depended on R06's CallDescriptor from step 2 on. R06 dropped from dependsOn.
  • ICallerAmbient split into a public read port and an internal ICallerCarrier with Enter: a public Enter let any code with a service provider replay a captured Caller, and Enter(null) from a grain is the kernel in release 1.
  • LegacyCallers.Read no longer maps 'AppId == PrincipalId' to Actor.Module (an app keyed like its user's principal would have become a trusted module); the 4 ModuleCallKey sites mint Module explicitly. Noted that the projection is lossy for Platform principals and that an 'app:' principal must map to Actor.App.
  • Steps re-ordered so each compiles: PerAccountAttribute deletion moved from step 2 to step 4 (PlainNeuronCallFilter.cs:94 and LegacyPureTypeFacts.cs:16 use it); ModuleCallKey/RequestContextKey removal moved from step 3 to step 4 (PlainNeuronCallFilter.cs:60-84); Prepare kept for IsInterleaved and RequireInterleaving; CallerAdmission.Reader is assigned instead of inventing a CallerAmbients static.
  • LocalOwner option (person 'owner') replaced by AccessOptions.UnstampedEdgeIsKernel, which is what a runtime-only silo does today; the person variant would have refused method-level [Internal] and BrainNeuron.Announce in the existing db integration suite. Noted that the db HTTP edge has 0 stamp sites.
  • Orleans sink hop no longer claims inside: true for every call; no incoming filter runs for a host grain's stream, so a client's stream would have arrived 'inside'. TestOS CallerSink made async so the AsyncLocal does not leak.
  • Census corrections: CallerContextStamper has 0 real uses in os (a global-using alias to RuntimeCallerContext), which also raises os stamp sites from 69 to 71; RequestContextKey in db src is 3 in 2 files, not 13 in 4 (the regex matched two trace-key constants); os KernelInvocationSink is never constructed, so 'honour its db.caller for one release' was removed; RuntimeCallerMirrorFacts does not exist; CallerContextStamper.cs is 37 lines; BrainHosting change is 16 lines to 1; contract-level [Internal] interfaces are at least 8, not 5; 'os UnitScenario.cs:52' is a digitalbrain file called from os BuilderTools.cs:81.

  • Strengthened bug 9: os HostCaller.cs:50 ('caller with { AppId = appKey }') and CapabilityCallers.Of accept the reserved ids, so an app key 'platform' reads as Platform and is exempt from the brain rule.

Doubts that remain

  • Orleans: IOutgoingGrainCallContext.SourceId being null on a non-grain thread of the silo, and RequestContext being present when the body of an IAsyncEnumerable grain method starts, are not stated in orleans-facts.md. The first decides whether Direct = 'Via is null && !Inside' reproduces today; the second decides where Stream is gated. Both are marked ASSUMPTION and need the step-1 matrix and a spike.
  • How os persists AppState (journal JSON through RuntimeProtocol, or Orleans [Id] fields) was not read. The stored shape is the five strings of CapabilityCaller either way, but the compatibility of an os-owned replacement record must be proven by reading a real stored document before bump 2.
  • I did not read the 51 os same-brain lines, the 71 os stamp sites or the 18 [Public] method implementations one by one; whether any os path relies on a behaviour outside the matrix (for example a neuron in a brain literally named 'system' reached by module stamps) is covered only by os tests and the live gate.
  • Whether os validates app keys against the reserved ids 'platform', 'scheduler', 'assistant' before HostCaller.AsApp was not checked; the defect is certain in the two functions cited, its reachability is not.
  • Counts taken from verify/access-identity.md and not re-measured: 51 same-brain lines in 35 os files, 40 identity RequestContext accesses in digitalbrain, 84 attributed methods with 0 overloads, one silo replica per topology.
  • The after-code was not compiled. Points most likely to need adjustment: the 'Color Color' binding of Actor inside Caller (property and type of the same name), [Immutable] on a class with public fields, an internal interface (ICallerCarrier) implemented from a friend assembly, and whether the Orleans source generator accepts an internal sealed class as a RequestContext value type (CapabilityCallerSurrogate, an internal struct in the same project, suggests yes).
  • Step 4 is one large step (gate on, three old mechanisms off, four test files replaced). It cannot be split without running the old filters and the new gate side by side for a step; I judged a double decision worse than a large step, but it is where the 5-day estimate is most at risk.
  • In release 1 the implicit kernel makes every unstamped grain call Kernel for neuron code that reads Context.Caller; I listed the two digitalbrain neurons whose answers change (ConfirmationsNeuron.Read, BrainNeuron.Announce) but did not audit os neurons for 'a stamp must be absent' checks of the same kind.

R08 · Units · wave 1

Split StateNeuron into pure machine, one entry per turn

Part A: split StateNeuron<TState> into a pure UnitMachine, functions bound per catalog and a thin internal UnitNeuron over StoredUnit<TState> that commits one journal entry per turn (a press goes from 3 appends + 1 journal read-back to 1 append). Part B, only after an owner decision that R09 contradicts: rename the unit meaning of "Synapse" to Unit / [Reaction] / [View].

Net
Part A src: -439 measured at HEAD (StateNeuron.cs 435 + NeuronOfTState.cs 4) + est. 348 = about -90 vs HEAD, about +45 vs the tree after R03 (which has already taken about 130 gate lines out of StateNeuron.cs) / tests: -73 measured, + est. 450 / os 0. Part B src: -40 measured + est. 191, then -90 when SynapseAttribute.cs goes; the rename itself is net 0 over about 1,170 occurrences in digitalbrain (430 src in 53 files, 344 tests in 45 files, 392 in 3 PublicAPI files, whole-word, measured) and in os 60 type occurrences in 18 files + 49 attribute lines. Part A does not shrink the code much: it removes 4 flags, 2 base-class seams, 2 of 3 appends per press, 1 of 2 appends per reaction on a unit with a view, and 1 journal read-back.
Effort
Part A: M (3 days) - about 350 new src lines with their spec in existing tests, three hosting sites, 9 retargeted and 5 new facts on two runtimes. Part B: another M (3-4 days) - two attributes with a legacy read path through the shape reader, the catalog and four analyzers, 135 + 49 attribute lines, a symbol rename of about 1,170 occurrences, and an os PR that is a production deploy. Both together are L; the designer's M (3-4 days) for both was too low.

Review confidence
medium

What is wrong today

src/DigitalBrain/Synapses/StateNeuron.cs is 435 lines (about 305 once R03 removes the gate) and does five jobs: function selection, reflection invoke with timeouts and slots (:377-420, parameter plan rebuilt on every call; the catalog is scanned through :62-66 from :118, :254, :258 and :360), view generation, press handling and storage. It declares its state as TState but stores StoredState<TState>, so Neuron<TState> carries two internal virtuals for this one subclass (Neurons/NeuronOfTState.cs:25-28; 1 overrider in both repos) and the neuron coordinates the flags _viewsPending, _journalingRefusal, _savingView and _stored (:39-43) through the OnCommitted hook (:231-234). Because Save demands a signal, the view is stored by saving the unchanged state again with ViewChanged (:271): a reaction on a unit with a view is 2 appends, and a press is 3 appends plus a History.Read to learn the sequence the neuron itself just wrote (:315-317). By reading, not run: if the second append fails the stored view stays older than the stored state while Orleans already counts the signal as delivered (SynapseEngine.cs:321-325); a second reaction to the same signal that throws after the first one saved is not retried; an activation whose record type was replaced in the catalog reacts to nothing while its cursor moves on (:62-66, :86). Separately, "Synapse" names both the durable edge and the unit function, package and host; os has 10 shipped units with 32 [Synapse] functions (16 alias reactions, 6 Tick reactions, 10 views) and os v0.16.1 (production) has none and no unit host.

Design principle

A domain object holds the rules and nothing else. Here the unit's rules (what a reaction result means, when a view is a new generation, what a press emits) sit inside an infrastructure subclass that works around its base class's persistence contract with mode flags. The target makes the rules a pure object (UnitMachine: results in, UnitStep out) and makes the aggregate's stored record its declared state (StoredUnit), so one turn is one atomic commit. Part B is ubiquitous language: one word, one meaning per context.

Defects this removes

  • All by reading; none was run.
  • A failed or interrupted second append leaves the stored view older than the stored state: the state entry (StateNeuron.cs:94/98) and the view entry (:271) are separate appends, and on Orleans the first already carried the cursor so the signal is not retried (SynapseEngine.cs:321-325). After: one entry. A view function that throws still leaves the previous view, as today.
  • Partial delivery with two reactions to one signal: the first reaction's save carries the cursor, a throw in the second is logged and never retried (StateNeuron.cs:86-104; SynapseEngine.cs:321-325). After: one commit per signal. 0 of 10 shipped units have two reactions to one signal.
  • An activation whose record type was replaced goes deaf and lets its cursor move on (StateNeuron.cs:62-66, :86) in the window between catalog.Replace and Reload (OrleansSynapseRegistrar.cs:35-39). After: it keeps the functions it has.
  • Neuron<TState>.InspectState serialises a unit record through a static JsonSerializerOptions built from Web defaults (NeuronOfTState.cs:11,19), which RuntimeProtocol.cs:17 forbids for collectible types; reached for units through NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:180-184 (BrainObserver.cs:65) and TestOS.cs:294-296. Effect if System.Text.Json keeps the metadata: one load context that cannot unload per inspected-then-redeployed unit. After: UnitNeuron re-implements IStateInspectable with RuntimeProtocol.OptionsFor.
  • Cost, not a defect: a press costs 3 appends and reads the journal back to learn the sequence it just wrote (StateNeuron.cs:315-317). After: 1 append, 0 reads.
  • Minor: the static WarnedViews dictionary keyed by brain + view name only grows (StateNeuron.cs:44, :279). After: a per-activation set.

One turn, one entry: the flags and the base-class seam go

The neuron's declared state is what it stores, so Neuron<TState>.LoadState/StoreState (NeuronOfTState.cs:25-28) are deleted; State.State == null replaces _stored and State.View replaces _view. There is exactly one Save in the class. Not shown: Run (today's :357-375 with a UnitCall), Receive, the two readers and an IStateInspectable re-implementation that serialises State.State through RuntimeProtocol.OptionsFor. The bare-record read (:330-332) is dropped: see openDecision.

Views and presses become pure rules; a press is one entry

Today: append 1 (:315 emit), a journal read to learn its own sequence (:316), append 2 (state + StateChanged), append 3 (:271, the same state again + view + ViewChanged). After: one entry [state+view; emit, StateChanged, ViewChanged] with the same signal order, and the emit alone when nothing changes or the reaction throws. Corrected against the designer: a Reaction with a null Next is refused (today Save throws; the draft would have stored {state:null}), and Activate stays async so a refusal still arrives through the returned task.

Functions bound per catalog, not rebuilt per call

The gain is not speed (a turn does journal I/O): the parameter plan, budget and await type are decided once per catalog instead of per call, and the neuron holds no reflection. The designer's "bound once per activation" would break LiveCatalogFacts.ANeuronActivatedBeforeARegisterSeesTheNewSynapses (LiveCatalogFacts.cs:52-68: catalog replaced for the same record type, no reload, the live activation must run the new function), so binding follows the catalog instance. The designer's typed-delegate binder (Adapt1..6, about 45 lines) is removed: by its own note it bought no speed, and its collectible-unload behaviour was never run.

Part B only: the unit vocabulary (needs the owner's decision)

Mechanical by constructor arity in os: 6 Tick + 16 alias + 10 view = 32 lines in 10 files. The kind is declared instead of inferred from the return type (SynapseShape.cs:196-199) and the state alias is no longer repeated as a string. Corrected: OfAlias(string Alias) hid Trigger.Alias (CS0108, an error here), and Trigger.Alias now calls the existing SignalAliases.Of(Type) (Signals/SignalAliases.cs:22); it replaces the copies in os DebuggerReadsNeuron.cs:92,114 and BrainEventsEndpoints.cs:139. This pair lands only if the owner picks this direction over R09 section 6, which keeps [Synapse] and renames the edge.

  • Part A. Two reactions to one signal commit together or not at all (0 of 10 shipped units have this)
  • Part A. Unit state written as the bare record (every build before 698ac29, 2026-11-08, in no tag) reads as empty: 0 production brains; dev brains last written before that date lose their unit state on the next save
  • Part A. SynapseFinished outcome "saved" is published when the function returned a new state, before the turn's single commit (0 consumers in os; 10 digitalbrain test files name the two signals)
  • Part B. [Synapse] -> [Reaction]/[View]: os 32 lines in 10 shipped units and 17 lines in 6 test files; digitalbrain 135 occurrences in 31 test files. Both repos set TreatWarningsAsErrors, so [Obsolete] gives compiled code no grace period; it only keeps runtime-compiled unit source and stored builds working (the unit compiler blocks on errors only, Compile/SynapseCompiler.cs:167)

Type renames in os: 60 occurrences in 18 files (38 in 9 src files, 22 in 9 test files) - SynapseSource 14, SynapseCompiler 13, ISynapseCatalogSource 12, SynapseKind 6, ISynapseHost 5, SynapseCatalog 4, and 1 each of SynapseShape, SynapseDiagnostic, SynapseDescriptor, SynapseCatalogSource, SynapseAttribute, ISynapseRestorer
  • Part B. SynapseDescriptor.Signal/SignalAlias/Key/TakesCancellation -> UnitFunction.On: os DebuggerReadsNeuron.cs:92,114 and BrainEventsEndpoints.cs:139; digitalbrain SynapseShapeFacts.cs:99,138
  • Part B. Not compile-checked in digitalbrain: 17 type-name strings in AllowedApis.cs:58-62,:101-104; Rules.cs:88; and outside both repos intochat.docs (81 lines in 20 files)
  • Not broken on purpose: signal aliases synapse.view-changed/state-changed/started/finished, contract aliases synapse.view/state/host, neuron types synapse.installs/sources, the package id DigitalBrain.Runtime.Synapses, IViewReader/IStateReader/PublishedView/ActivationRefusedException (33 os uses keep compiling), the stored JSON shape {state, view}

Risks

  • Nothing was compiled or run. The after-code was checked by reading against the types that remain after R03.
  • StoredUnit has a parameterless and a positional constructor; System.Text.Json then uses the parameterless one and the init setters, where StoredState used the positional one. Same JSON shape; the guard is StateViewFacts.TheViewSurvivesDeactivationAndRestart and the retargeted schema-evolution fact.
  • A pressed reaction can no longer name the emit's entry as its cause. Keeping it would need Publish to return the sequence (a port change) or a second append.
  • Functions follow the catalog instance. An activation whose record type left the catalog keeps running the functions it has until INeuronHost.Reload replaces it; this relies on the registrars reloading on every swap (OrleansSynapseRegistrar.cs:39,62; TestOS.cs:472-478), read not run.
  • Part B is a rename of about 1,170 occurrences with two homonym pairs and 17 names held as strings; a regex rename is wrong. It must be one symbol-based commit.
  • Part B conflicts with R09 section 6, which keeps [Synapse] for the function and renames the edge record to Subscription. Landing both produces two incompatible vocabularies.
  • Part B timing: if an os release with units ships before the rename, [Synapse] is in stored builds and generated source in production and step B8 can never run.
  • Part B: [View] beside DigitalBrain.UI.View and [Reaction] beside Reaction<TState> are legal C# (attribute lookup only considers attribute classes) but are two meanings one token apart.

Proof it worked

UnitMachineFacts run with no context, no catalog and no compiled assembly. On both runtimes (TestOS and silo): APressIsOneEntry asserts one journal entry holding [emit, StateChanged, ViewChanged] with the new state and view; TheViewIsStoredInTheEntryThatChangedTheState; TwoReactionsToOneSignalCommitOnce; AFailingSecondReactionWritesNothing; AnActivationWhoseRecordWasReplacedStillReacts. Unchanged and green: LiveCatalogFacts.ANeuronActivatedBeforeARegisterSeesTheNewSynapses, StateViewFacts.AFailingReactionLeavesTheEmitJournaled, StateViewFacts.TheViewSurvivesDeactivationAndRestart, SynapseHostSiloFacts.ANeedNeutralUpdateSwapsAndUnloads, SynapseLoaderFacts.UnloadFreesTheContextWhenNothingHoldsIt. Greps that must return 0 in src after A3: "_viewsPending|_savingView|_stored\b|_journalingRefusal", "LoadState\(|StoreState\(", "History\.Read" inside Units/, "TargetInvocationException" inside Units/, "static.*ConcurrentDictionary" inside Units/; exactly one "await Save(" in UnitNeuron.cs. After the os pin bump: XWatchEndToEndFacts.cs:225 still sees ["time.tick","x.polled","synapse.view-changed"]. Part B: AnObsoleteSynapseUnitReadsTheSame (the same unit written both ways yields equal UnitFunction lists); grep "\[\s*Synapse\b" in os src returns 0 outside that fixture; the AllowedApis architecture fact is green.

Your decision

1. Vocabulary direction, to be decided once for R08 and R09. R08 Part B keeps "Synapse" for the edge and renames the functions to [Reaction]/[View] (cost: every authored unit line, the prompts, four analyzers, intochat.docs). R09 section 6 keeps [Synapse] for the function and renames the edge record to Subscription (cost: the edge API, nothing authored). The architecture judgment asks only for the pure machine. Recommendation: ship Part A now, it needs no decision and no os source change; decide the word once; if Part B is chosen it must land before the first os release that hosts units, because os v0.16.1 (production) has none today. 2. Part A drops the read of the bare-record state format (written before 2026-11-08, in no tag): 0 production brains, but dev brains last written before that date lose their unit state. Confirm this is acceptable. 3. If Part B: should the package id DigitalBrain.Runtime.Synapses (10 references in os) change too? Recommendation: no.

Review notes on this proposal

Quote check. 4 of 4 before-snippets verified verbatim against the files at HEAD (digitalbrain a275f82, os 7a28188b3): StateNeuron.cs 83-105 / 231-234 / 320-333; 252-259 / 268-278 / 284-287 / 304-317; 377-420; os WebWatch.cs 18-42 (elided) and SynapseShape.cs 30-46. Elisions are lines containing exactly "// ...". No snippet text needed fixing; the errors were in citations outside the snippets (listed in changesMade).

Corrections the reviewer made

  • Split the proposal into Part A (pure machine, one entry per turn; digitalbrain only) and Part B (vocabulary), because R09 section 6 renames the same word in the opposite direction (keeps [Synapse], renames the edge to Subscription) and the architecture judgment asks only for the machine. Retitled accordingly; the vocabulary direction is now the first open decision.
  • Replaced 'functions bound once per activation' with 'bound per catalog instance': the designer's version breaks the existing green test LiveCatalogFacts.ANeuronActivatedBeforeARegisterSeesTheNewSynapses (LiveCatalogFacts.cs:52-68), which replaces the catalog for the same record type without a reload.
  • Fixed step ordering: a public StateNeuron<TState> cannot derive from Neuron<StoredUnit<TState>> with an internal StoredUnit (CS0060), so the class becomes internal UnitNeuron in step A3, not in the later rename step; added LiveRegistryFacts.cs:28 (test assembly without InternalsVisibleTo), AllowedApis.cs:62 and 14 PublicAPI.Unshipped.txt lines to that step.
  • UnitMachine.Reacted now refuses a Reaction with a null Next; the draft would have saved {state:null}, which reads back as never stored (today Save throws).
  • UnitNeuron.Activate is async again so an ActivationRefusedException arrives through the returned task, as today.
  • Removed UnitBinder (typed delegates, Adapt1..6, about 45 lines): no speed gain by the designer's own note, unload behaviour never run, motivated by an unverified recollection. BoundFunction calls MethodBase.Invoke with BindingFlags.DoNotWrapExceptions.
  • Part A snippets now use the names that exist after step A3 (SynapseDescriptor, SynapseCatalog, ISynapseCatalogSource, ISynapseReceiver, SynapseKind); the draft mixed in Part B names that do not exist yet.
  • Trigger.OfAlias(string Alias) hid Trigger.Alias (CS0108, an error under TreatWarningsAsErrors): renamed the positional property to Name; Trigger.Alias now uses the existing SignalAliases.Of(Type) instead of a new hand-written copy.
  • UnitLimits made internal (0 uses in os; a public type would need an AllowedApis entry).
  • Corrected citations: NeuronTypeRegistry.cs:50 and TestOS.cs:184 are doc comments (code sites 62,114,174 and 195,216,472); TestOS.cs:472-474 is 472-478; SynapseCatalog.cs:77 is a gate branch R03 deletes, the remaining alias copies are os DebuggerReadsNeuron.cs:92,114 and BrainEventsEndpoints.cs:139; BuilderTools.cs:87 is a comment, the prompt line is :56 and the schedule regex is :103; SynapseCompiler.cs is under Compile/.
  • Corrected counts: os type renames are 60 occurrences in 18 files, not 86 in 21; digitalbrain census 430 src / 344 tests / 392 PublicAPI with the table's identifiers; prompt lines are 8, not 9; added digitalbrain test attribute uses (135 in 31 files) and intochat.docs (81 lines in 20 files), both missing.
  • Added missed breaks: TreatWarningsAsErrors in both repos removes the [Obsolete] grace period for compiled code; two homonym pairs (public vs internal SynapseSource and ISynapseHost) make a regex rename wrong; 17 type names are strings in AllowedApis.cs; os BuilderTools.cs:103 and NoLegacyWorkMechanismsFacts.cs:32 are source regexes; SynapseCatalog.cs:144,147 and three analyzers key on SynapseAttribute; SynapseShapeFacts.cs:99,138 read TakesCancellation.
  • Removed overstatement: 'CausationId has two meanings' is no longer listed as a bug (the press value is asserted by two tests; it is a deliberate loss, listed under breaks); 'stale view' narrowed to a failed or interrupted second append; the 560 test lines are no longer counted as an R08 deletion (23 of 28 facts are R03's; R08's share is 73 lines); the five remaining facts become UnitNeuronFacts, not UnitMachineFacts.
  • Net LOC restated against both baselines (about -90 vs HEAD, about +45 vs the tree after R03) and effort raised from M for both halves to M for Part A plus M for Part B.
  • Dropping the bare-record state fallback is now an explicit owner decision, not a silent note.

Doubts that remain

  • Nothing was compiled or run; the after-code is checked by reading only. The await inside a switch-expression arm and the mixed deconstruction inside a lambda are legal C# 13 but were not compiled here.
  • R03's exact leftover in StateNeuron.cs and ISynapseReceiver is assumed from its design (about 130 lines removed, two Receive members left); if R03 lands differently the Part A baseline shifts.
  • The InspectState item rests on System.Text.Json sharing its metadata cache between equivalent options instances; that is from knowledge of the library and RuntimeProtocol.cs:17, not from a run.
  • os XWatchEndToEndFacts.cs:225 and CreatorAppFacts.cs:224 are expected to stay green because they assert signal order; CreatorAppFacts labels carry numbers that look like sequences and were not traced to their source.
  • TestOS behaviour for a reaction that fails before any write (retry or drop) was not read; AFailingSecondReactionWritesNothing may expose one of the known runtime drifts.
  • Whether SynapseRestorer.RebuildReason really flags a stored build that references a deleted SynapseAttribute depends on AllowedReferences.FirstDisallowed, which was not opened.
  • The designer's brief was not visible to me; if it mandated the [Reaction]/[View] direction, Part B stands as written and only the R09 conflict needs resolving.

R09 · Context map · wave 2

Re-cut the core by context and split INeuronContext

Move every type that is not published language out of the open namespaces (namespace-only, same assemblies and package ids) so the unit sandbox rule is "the namespace is open" with no type lists, dissolve DigitalBrain.Spi by direction, and shrink INeuronContext from 12 properties + 5 methods to 7 + 4, with Operations, History and Confirmations injected through the neuron's constructor and the save path made internal.

Net
src: -110 measured (56 + 17 + 15 + 16 + 6), +113 est., so about 0, then -15 when LegacySeat goes in Release 2; +300 est. for the checked-in mapping table and codemod. tests: -53 measured, +253 est. os: about +320 using lines (est., upper bound), 90 using DigitalBrain.Spi; lines replaced, about 990 qualified names rewritten in place. This move is churn, not deletion: 86 of 178 core files change their namespace line and PublicAPI.Unshipped.txt (3,322 lines, measured) is regenerated.
Effort
L (1-2 weeks): no hard design left but it is wide: 86 core files, a 3,322-line public API file, 42 analyzer strings, 355 other db files and 446 os files through a codemod, an unmeasured list of capability-package types to re-home when the closure fact first runs, and the port split across 5 context implementers, 51 db call sites and 110 os call sites over two releases.
Depends on
R01 (hard: RevisionedNeuron, NeuronState, IBrainSignalLog, the IAppHost family and State fossils are deleted, not moved)
R03 (hard: IGateIndex, InstalledGateIndex, GateChanged, ProvisionalGateQueue and the Check record leave before the move)
R07 (hard: the Caller type on INeuronContext; CallerAdmission and the CapabilityCaller records are replaced, not re-homed)
R04 (order only: its KernelState implements INeuronSeat instead of INeuronState)
R05 (order only: ISynapses moves to DigitalBrain.Kernel with whatever name R05 gives it)
R10 (order only: if DigitalBrain.Apps leaves the package, its os rewrites disappear)
R14 (order only: IInProcessHostFactory and InProcessHostOptions follow DigitalBrain.Simulation)
Review confidence
medium

What is wrong today

Measured at digitalbrain a275f82 / os 7a28188b3: the core has 178 files, 10,323 lines, 325 public type declarations, and 81 files declare exactly namespace DigitalBrain;, which holds unit vocabulary, neuron base classes, kernel neurons, policies, installs, the app model and the module manifest side by side. The sandbox therefore keeps two hand-written arrays, 57 refused + 94 allowed names (src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Synapses/Host/AllowedApis.cs:56-73, :79-106), and the fact that polices them checks one of the twelve open namespaces (tests/DigitalBrain.Architecture.Tests.Unit/UnitSurfaceFacts.cs:27), so DigitalBrain.Time.TimeNeuron (Time/TimeNeuron.cs:12), DigitalBrain.Capabilities.Connections.ConnectionNeuron<TState> (ConnectionNeuron.cs:10), CapabilityCallers and ICapabilityBroker are open to units unruled, while the 10 shipped units use 8 of the 94 names. DigitalBrain.Spi (13 files) mixes ports a runtime implements, interfaces the core implements, host listeners, unit plumbing and test infrastructure, and its own fact is already bent (CoreVocabularyFacts.cs:47-48). INeuronContext (Spi/Spi.cs:6-62) has 12 properties + 5 methods and points upward at Operations and Confirmation (:27, :30, :57, :60). os reads Context.Services 64 times as a service locator and Context.State 25 times, 10 of them Context.State.Save(next, []), which bypasses the invariant NeuronOfTState.cs:43 enforces.

Design principle

Bounded contexts with an explicit context map and a Published Language: today the boundary between what a sandboxed unit may see and everything else is a list of names kept by hand, and the lowest port (INeuronContext) depends on a higher context (Work). The target makes the boundary structural (a type is published language because of the namespace it is declared in; one fact proves that namespace hands out no closed type, another checks a one-way dependency table) and applies Interface Segregation to the port.

Defects this removes

  • Sandbox rule covers 1 of 12 open namespaces (verified by reading, not run): AllowedApis.cs:328-336 opens every type of the 12 namespaces less a refused list whose 57 entries are all in the exact DigitalBrain namespace, and UnitSurfaceFacts.cs:27 checks only that namespace. A unit may therefore reference DigitalBrain.Time.TimeNeuron (Time/TimeNeuron.cs:12), TimeState, DigitalBrain.Capabilities.Connections.ConnectionNeuron<TState> (Capabilities.Connections/Connections/ConnectionNeuron.cs:10, a neuron base class, while DigitalBrain.Neuron is refused at AllowedApis.cs:69), DigitalBrain.Capabilities.CapabilityCallers (Capabilities/CapabilityCaller.cs:112) and ICapabilityBroker (Capabilities/ICapabilityBroker.cs:4). No exploit found (CallerAdmission.Stamp is internal, Brains/CallerAdmission.cs:20), but docs/neurons-and-synapses.md:24 claims the surface is ruled.
  • The stated invariant of Neuron<TState> ("There is no save without a signal", NeuronOfTState.cs:6, enforced at :43) is bypassed through the public port at 13 sites: Context.State.Save(next, []) at 10 os sites (AuthNeuron.cs:23, AgentNeuron.cs:715, CircleFactoryNeuron.cs:47, CircleInvitesNeuron.cs:59, InviteCodeNeuron.cs:45 and :95, Identity ConnectionIndexNeuron.cs:39, HandleDirectoryNeuron.cs:14, DeploymentNeuron.cs:230, TableIndexNeuron.cs:77) and 3 framework sites (DigitalBrain.Identity.Local/Connections/ConnectionIndexNeuron.cs:46, DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans/Operations/OperationNeuron.cs:121, and :83-86 where the list is computed empty). The internal seat removes the door.
  • Upward dependency of the lowest port: Spi/Spi.cs:27, :30, :57, :60 make INeuronContext depend on DigitalBrain.Operations and DigitalBrain.Confirmation, while DigitalBrain.Flows is built on Neuron<T>, which is built on that port.
  • Behaviour keyed on a namespace string: DigitalBrain.Testing/ModuleOnTestOSFacts.cs:46 decides what is a neuron contract by !ns.StartsWith("DigitalBrain.Spi", StringComparison.Ordinal).

The sandbox boundary: 151 names kept by hand become one namespace rule

The 12 namespaces are the array that exists today; what goes is the 57 + 94 names and the clause that reads them. The open namespaces keep their names (no DigitalBrain.Authoring): unit sources are persisted text and name them (Runtime.Synapses/Compile/SynapseCompiler.cs:21-28 prelude; all 10 shipped units write using DigitalBrain;, 6 using DigitalBrain.Time;). The fact fails today for TimeNeuron and ConnectionNeuron<TState> (their base is a neuron class), which the old fact never looked at. By using-lines, 9 files in the open namespaces of Capabilities.Storage and Capabilities.Connections import closed namespaces, so Bridges will not be one entry.

INeuronContext: 12 properties + 5 methods become 7 + 4; the save path and the locator go internal

Seven members leave: Services, State, Operations, OperationsQuery, History, Confirmations, Confirm. TraceId stays: Flow.cs:105 and StateNeuron.cs:88, :314 read it and it is a fact about the current turn. Seat is private protected, so it is reachable only from the core and its InternalsVisibleTo assemblies; Flow<T>, StateNeuron<TState> and ConnectionNeuron<TState> read Seat.Services where they read Context.Services, Context.History and Context.Confirm today, so the 6 os Flow subclasses and 9 os connection subclasses do not change their constructors. PublishEphemeral keeps its default body.

Bound services through the constructor, one resolver for both runtimes, and what an os neuron pays

Bindings are a constructor argument, not a lookup in the host container: TestOS's container is whatever the test supplied, and its Operations and Confirmations fakes publish through the context (TestNeuronContext.cs:22-25), so TestOS binds over a context it builds first. The isolation of today is kept (history of the neuron's own brain, operations with the neuron as origin) because the runtime binds to the id it parsed before constructing (NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:43). Nothing is cached statically, so a collectible unit class is not pinned. The os file shows both costs: two qualified names rewritten by the codemod (DigitalBrain.Neuron is written in full 197 times in os) and one constructor parameter; its unit test becomes new TraceReaderNeuron(fake.History).

The namespace move for two files, and the context map as a fact

FOLDER MAP (86 of 178 core files get a new namespace line before R01/R03 delete some; 13 files and 18 public types stay in DigitalBrain). Stay in root: Signal, Causation, Gap, BrainId, NeuronId, SynapseAttribute, StateAttribute, SlotAttribute, Reaction, SignalEnvelope, the 4 records of SynapseSignals.cs, SignalAttribute, EphemeralSignalAttribute, CapabilityAttribute, CallsAttribute. To .Neurons: Neurons/ (Neuron, Neuron<T>, DeactivationCause), NeuronAttribute, OnAttribute, the 3 class-level attributes (InterleavesWhen, Reentrant, StatelessNeuron), INeuronContext, ITimers, IReminders, INeuronProxies, IStateInspectable, NeuronAttachment. To .Contracts (exists, os already imports it): the 4 method-level call attributes ReadOnlyCall, OneWayCall, CallTimeout, Interleaves, because they sit on contract interfaces of every context (Time/ITime.cs:28). To .Kernel: Synapse, ISynapses, SynapseRequest, SignalPage, ISignalHistory, SignalAliases, BrainView, IClusterListeners, IBrainObserver. To .Scheduling (new, Work): TimeNeuron, TimeState; a neuron cannot live in Kernel, which may use only Authoring. To .Units: the other 10 files of Synapses/, Synapses/Host, Synapses/Install, IInstallHooks, ISlotResolver, ISynapseRegistrar, ISynapseRestorer, ISynapseReceiver, NoSynapseRestorer. To .Limits: Policies/ (8 files). To .Modules: Modules/ root files (9), ModuleAttribute. To .Apps: Apps/ root files, AppAttribute. To .Operations: IOperations, IOperationsQuery, IOperation. To .Connections: ICapabilityBroker, CapabilityConnection. To .Access: CallerAdmission and the caller records until R07 replaces them. To .Brains: NeuronAddress, INeuronResolver. To .Hosting: InProcessHostOptions, IInProcessHostFactory. Every other existing namespace keeps its name.

Steps, each shippable

  1. db, tests and analyzers only, no release, green today: move the 28 loose metadata-name literals (8 in the two sets of Rules.cs:109-114, 15 in 9 analyzer files, 5 in 3 code-fix files) into Rules.cs constants; add MetadataNameFacts over all 42 with a ProjectReference to the core. Add the two TimeNeuron / ConnectionNeuron probe rows to HostRefusesBannedApisFacts as Skip with the defect text. Owner task in os: list the DigitalBrain types named by the stored unit sources of the production brains (needs read access to brain storage); the move is safe for them if the set is inside the 18 root types plus Rules, Time, UI and capability contracts.
  2. db, additive, no release needed on its own: NeuronBinding, NeuronServices, INeuronSeat and LegacySeat in the core; four bindings in Runtime.Orleans; TestNeuronHost builds the context first and constructs from its NeuronServices; the three kit/runtime contexts implement the seat; save-fault hook on InMemoryNeuronState. Mark the seven leaving members and INeuronState [Obsolete] and move the db callers in the same commit: src code sites Services 12, State 11, Confirm/Confirmations 4, History 1 (Flow, StateNeuron and ConnectionNeuron read the seat; PoliciesNeuron, InstallsNeuron, BrainGraphNeuron, ConnectionRequestsNeuron, OperationNeuron take constructor parameters or the seat); test sites Services 9, History 7, Operations 5, Confirm/Confirmations 2. Identity.Local/Connections/ConnectionIndexNeuron.cs:46 is outside the core's InternalsVisibleTo: it becomes a Neuron<TState>.Save with a signal (decision 2). New dual-tier BoundServiceFacts. Gate: whole db suite.
  3. db, Release 1, last step after R01, R03 and R07 so that fossils, gate types and caller records are deleted instead of moved: run the namespace move from eng/namespace-moves.tsv over the repo (86 core files; 169 other src files, 176 test files, 10 example and template files touched by the designer's census), regenerate PublicAPI.Unshipped.txt, update the Rules.cs constants. Delete the two arrays in AllowedApis.cs, UnitSurfaceFacts.cs and Spi_is_interfaces_only. Add AuthoringSurfaceFacts and ContextMapFacts with their exception sets filled from the first run on the moved tree (this run is the measurement nobody has yet; types in open capability namespaces whose base or signature is closed either move to a closed sub-namespace or are written into Bridges). Retarget CoreVocabularyFacts.cs:15-32 (7 rows name DigitalBrain.Spi.*), MovedTypesFacts.cs and LegacyPureTypeFacts.cs if R01 left them, HostRefusesBannedApisFacts.cs:36 (the probe names ISynapseHost through the prelude; it must write DigitalBrain.Units.ISynapseHost), DigitalBrain.Testing/ModuleOnTestOSFacts.cs:46, docs/neurons-and-synapses.md:24. Un-skip the two probe rows. Gate: whole db suite.

  5. os, ordinary commits, no package change: replace the 64 Context.Services sites (8 of them are properties that re-export the locator: AuthNeuron.cs:17, AiNeuron.cs:12 and :20, UiNeuron.cs:13, AppNeuron.cs:30, CircleFactoryNeuron.cs:40, CircleNeuron.cs:60, CandidateNeuron.cs:17) by constructor parameters; move the 25 Context.State sites to Neuron<TState>, deciding per site what the 10 empty-signal saves publish; rewrite GmailStateFacts.cs:37 (FaultingContext) and GitHubRepositoryWritesFacts.cs:274, :311 over FakeNeuronContext.
  6. db Release 2, then os bump 2 (can ride any later bump): delete the seven obsolete members, INeuronState, LegacySeat and ConnectionNeuron.ServiceProvider; the five INeuronContext entries leave ContextMapFacts.Known; remove CS0618 from WarningsNotAsErrors in os. Making internal the public core types nothing outside mentions (30 by the designer's word match, not re-measured) is a separate commit of this release.
  7. Glossary: adopt the rename table of the designer's report (section 6) as docs/glossary.md in step 3, with one change (INeuronState becomes INeuronSeat, not INeuronJournal). Each type rename lands with the move that owns the type (R03 gate words, R05 Subscription and Delivery, R07 Caller, R10 App and Screen), old name [Obsolete] for one release; no rename is done for its own sake here.

What breaks

  • digitalbrain public API: 86 of 178 core files change namespace, so most of the 321 public type names get a new full name (about 250 by the designer's script); PublicAPI.Unshipped.txt (3,322 lines) is regenerated. No type forwarders are possible or needed: assembly and package ids do not change, source compatibility is restored by the codemod.
  • os source (designer's r09_census.py re-run over 1,746 .cs files): about 990 fully qualified references in 298 files (the script prints 1,329 because DigitalBrain.Neuron(, .Module(, .App( are counted as class and attribute); by word match DigitalBrain.Neuron 197, .ReadOnlyCall 125, .On 109, .Module 94, .Interleaves 60, .ModuleManifest 53, .App 49, .InstallStatus 46, .AppDefinition 30, .CallTimeout 27, .Setting 25; 90 files with using DigitalBrain.Spi;; about 320 new using lines; 446 files touched (355 non-test).
  • os, INeuronContext split: 110 member call sites change (Services 64 in 31 files, State 25 in 11, Operations 9, History 6, OperationsQuery 3, Confirm 2, Confirmations 1); Synapses 17, Time 30, Reminders 15, Timers 9 are untouched. They compile with CS0618 warnings in Release 1 (os has TreatWarningsAsErrors, os/Directory.Build.props:8) and break in Release 2.
  • os tests: GmailStateFacts.cs:37 (FaultingContext : INeuronContext, INeuronState) and GitHubRepositoryWritesFacts.cs:274, :311 (TestNeuronContext, TestNeuronState : INeuronState) cannot implement the state port once it is internal; a neuron attached to a hand-written context cannot load or save state from Release 2. The 12 os test files that call NeuronAttachment.Attach(neuron, context) with a FakeNeuronContext keep working (signature unchanged). Unit tests of every neuron that gains a constructor parameter change their new XNeuron().
  • digitalbrain outside the core: 169 src files, 176 test files and 10 example/template files touched (designer's census); 5 implementers of INeuronContext (HostNeuronContext, TestNeuronContext, FakeNeuronContext, CoreVocabularyFacts.NullContext, RecordingContext); ConnectionNeuron.cs:26 protected IServiceProvider ServiceProvider => Context.Services (9 os subclasses) reads the seat in Release 1 and is deleted in Release 2.
  • Strings that name moved types and fail silently: 37 in DigitalBrain.Analyzers (22 in Rules.cs, 15 in 9 analyzer files), 5 in DigitalBrain.Analyzers.CodeFixes, 6 in os architecture facts, 7 InlineData rows in CoreVocabularyFacts.cs:26-32, the probe source at HostRefusesBannedApisFacts.cs:36, and the behaviour check DigitalBrain.Testing/ModuleOnTestOSFacts.cs:46 !ns.StartsWith("DigitalBrain.Spi", ...).
  • Unit sandbox surface narrows: of the 94 names on the allowed list, 76 move to closed namespaces (PoliciesState, PolicyGrammar, InstallRecord, Installed, Deployed, BddState, ScenarioAttribute, AppKey, NeuronAddress, JournaledSignal, ...), and public types in open capability namespaces that are not contracts (ConnectionNeuron<TState>, StorageWriteRunner, CrossConnectionMove, ...) close as they are moved. The 10 shipped units use none of them (grep: Synapse, SignalEnvelope, Gap, Reaction, Tick, Signal, State, Slot only); installed units in production brains and probe units in db tests are not measured.
  • Persisted data: none found. Journaled signals are resolved by [Signal] alias (Runtime.Orleans/Storage/SignalTypeMap.cs:8), neuron types by [Neuron] / [State] alias, and stored unit sources name only open namespaces, whose names are kept.

Risks

  • An installed unit in a production brain names a type that closes (for example reacts to Installed by type). It would fail to restore after the os bump. Step 1 measures this before anything ships; a hit is fixed by leaving that one signal record in the root namespace, which the closure fact allows for pure records.
  • The two exception sets (AuthoringSurfaceFacts.Bridges, ContextMapFacts.Known) are unmeasured because nothing was built. If the first run in step 3 shows many edges, the map is the target and the sets are the work list; if it shows a cycle the map cannot express (for example Kernel types that need a Neurons type), the map changes, not the code.
  • Both facts read base types and signatures, not method bodies or attributes. A body in Kernel that calls into Limits is not caught. That is a known limit, not a guarantee.
  • ActivatorUtilities over the NeuronServices wrapper is an assumption from memory of the .NET source. The wrapper forwards IServiceProviderIsService to the host container, which does not know the bindings: a class with several public constructors can silently get the constructor without the bound parameter. Two os neuron classes have two public constructors today (LocalCloudNeuron, ModuleScaffolderNeuron; regex census). BoundServiceFacts must cover the single-constructor case on a real silo before step 4.
  • The two runtimes still choose constructors by different rules (ActivatorUtilities in Orleans, widest public constructor with default values in TestOS, TestNeuronHost.cs:224-228). This move makes them resolve from the same NeuronServices but does not unify the choice.
  • The os diff is large (446 files) inside a bump that is a production deploy. It is compile-checked and behaviour-neutral, but it conflicts with every open os branch; do it as one scripted commit and re-run the script instead of merging by hand.
  • A missed metadata-name string turns an analyzer rule off without any error. MetadataNameFacts covers all 42 only if step 1 really moves the 28 loose literals into Rules.cs.
  • Orleans wire naming of surrogated core types (17 [RegisterConverter]) across the bump was not verified. Both shipped topologies run one replica (ResourceSpecs MinReplicas = MaxReplicas = 1), so there is no mixed-version traffic; a multi-silo rolling upgrade would need a check.
  • Closing the State back door forces a decision at 13 sites that save without a signal (10 in os, 3 in the framework). If that decision is postponed, Release 2 cannot delete INeuronContext.State.

Proof it worked

1) grep -rn 'RefusedDigitalBrainTypes\|UnitFacingDigitalBrainTypes' src tests goes from 9 lines to 0; grep -o '"DigitalBrain[.A-Za-z0-9_]*"' AllowedApis.cs | wc -l goes from 164 to 12. 2) The two probe rows (a unit naming TimeNeuron, a unit naming ConnectionNeuron) are skipped-red before step 3 and green after. 3) AuthoringSurfaceFacts green with a written Bridges set that only shrinks. 4) ContextMapFacts: every DigitalBrain namespace of the core is placed; the five INeuronContext edges are gone from Known at Release 2. 5) grep -rl '^namespace DigitalBrain;' src/DigitalBrain goes from 81 files to 13; grep -rn 'namespace DigitalBrain.Spi' src returns nothing. 6) At Release 2 INeuronContext has 7 properties and 4 methods; in os Context\.Services goes from 64 to 0 and Context\.State\b from 25 to 0. 7) MetadataNameFacts green for all 42 analyzer strings and the analyzer test project reports every DB diagnostic it reported before. 8) BoundServiceFacts green on both runtimes, including a collectible StateNeuron. 9) All 10 shipped unit sources deploy unchanged in the os Apps integration suite, the stored-source census of step 1 is clean, and the live aspire start gate passes on the os bump.

Your decision

Three decisions only the owner can make. (1) Accept that the published language keeps the namespace DigitalBrain instead of a new DigitalBrain.Authoring: the alternative costs os about 569 more qualified rewrites of Signal / NeuronId / BrainId (434 + 97 + 33 + attributes, measured) and puts every stored unit source that writes using DigitalBrain; at risk; a separate DigitalBrain.Authoring.dll with type forwarders costs os nothing but adds a package to a cascade where each publish needs your token, and can still be done later on top of this move. (2) Is "no save without a signal" a real invariant? If yes, the 13 empty-signal saves each need a named signal (and each then grows the journal by one signal per save, AgentNeuron.cs:715 included); if no, delete the sentence at NeuronOfTState.cs:6 and give Neuron<TState> a silent save instead of closing the port. (3) Is removing Context.Services worth 64 os edits and a second os production bump? The sandbox rule, the Spi dissolution and the Operations / History / Confirmations injection are complete after bump 1; Release 2 only deletes.

Review notes on this proposal

Quote check. All 4 designer before-snippets (6 files) were opened and compared: AllowedApis.cs:34-106 + :328-336, Spi/Spi.cs:5-62, os TraceReaderNeuron.cs:1-18, ISlotResolver.cs:1-13, ISynapses.cs:1-13, ActionDescriptor.cs:1-7 are verbatim with correct ranges; nothing invented. In the final proposal: pair 1 is unchanged; pair 2 is Spi.cs:5-62 with three "// ..." elisions (lines 13-16, 19-25, 37-55) to stay near 45 lines; pair 3 is new and copied from NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:94-102 and os TraceReaderNeuron.cs:9-18; pair 4 is new and copied from Time/TimeNeuron.cs:1-12 and Spi/ISlotResolver.cs:1-4. One cited path was wrong (SynapseCompiler.cs is under Compile/, not Host/).

Corrections the reviewer made

  • TraceId stays on INeuronContext: three core call sites read it (Flows/Flow.cs:105, Synapses/StateNeuron.cs:88, :314) and the designer gave no replacement. The port is 7 properties + 4 methods (6 of today's + R07's Caller), 7 members leave, not 8; title text, deletes and proof corrected.
  • The designer's NeuronAttachment.Attach taking an internal journal does not compile (CS0051) and os calls the public method in 12 test files. Replaced by an internal INeuronSeat that the three kit/runtime contexts implement beside INeuronContext; Neuron.Attach reads it with context as INeuronSeat; the public signature is unchanged. A Release 1 LegacySeat adapter keeps hand-written contexts working until Release 2.
  • Added INeuronSeat.Services (internal): the core's own base classes Flow<T> (Flow.cs:190, :338, :438, :459), StateNeuron<TState> (:316, :350) and ConnectionNeuron<TState> (ConnectionNeuron.cs:26, 9 os subclasses) cannot get bound services by constructor injection without forcing every subclass to forward parameters.
  • NeuronServices takes its bindings as a constructor argument and builds each bound service once per activation. The designer's lookup in the host container cannot work in TestOS (the container is the test's own, and the Operations/Confirmations fakes need the context's Publish, TestNeuronContext.cs:22-25); TestNeuronHost.cs:199-200 is reordered so the context exists first. The SynapseCatalog special case of TestNeuronHost.cs:226 becomes a fifth binding.
  • Corrected the ActivatorUtilities assumption and risk: the wrapper forwards IServiceProviderIsService to the host, so the danger is a silently wrong constructor on multi-constructor classes, not a missing interface. Measured with a new script (r09_ctors.py): 2 os neuron classes have two public constructors, 9 have default-valued parameters.
  • Removed the deletion of Neuron.cs:61 and its bugsFixed entry: the same-brain check has three copies (Neuron.cs:61, HostNeuronContext.cs:107, TestNeuronContext.cs:82) and none in FakeNeuronContext.Get (Fakes.cs:591), so deleting the core copy would remove the check for every unit test over the kit's fake.
  • Fixed the context map where it contradicted itself: TimeNeuron : Neuron<TimeState> was placed in DigitalBrain.Kernel, which may use only Authoring; it goes to a new DigitalBrain.Scheduling in the Work context. The four method-level call attributes (ReadOnlyCall, OneWayCall, CallTimeout, Interleaves) go to DigitalBrain.Contracts instead of .Neurons, because they are applied on contract interfaces of the published language and the kernel (Time/ITime.cs:28).

They land in the move step; step 1 keeps only what is green today.

Doubts that remain

  • Whether os persists a CLR full name of a moved type anywhere outside the six string literals found in its architecture facts.
  • private protected access to Neuron.Seat from derived classes in InternalsVisibleTo assemblies (OperationNeuron in Runtime.Orleans, ConnectionNeuron in Capabilities.Connections) is standard C# but was not compiled.

R10 · Presentation · wave 2

Make the app a neuron: Screen<TState>, delete the host

Replace App/App<TState> and its os-only host with Screen<TState> : Neuron<TState>, IScreen (about 220 lines in the core, hosted by the existing host grain on both runtimes), migrate the 30 os app classes behind unchanged public view names, then delete the App model, 6 analyzers and about 800 lines of os host machinery; do not move App to os.

Net

Effort
L, the top of it (2 weeks, 3 if the pilot finds a long handler or the live view path is not a small mapping): the framework part is two days (about 220 lines, deletions, facts on both runtimes), but os has 16 app files with 30 classes, 415 lines of desktop window logic to move, an edge mapping and a live path, more than 4,000 test lines to retarget (3,205 in the app tests), and two production deploys with a live gate each.
Depends on
R02 (DigitalBrain.Kernel.ReactionTable.React: the one [On] lookup in the core, which Screen calls)
Soft: the one-call-catalog / typed-caller move (removes the single app-caller stamp that ShellScreen keeps for signal-triggered reactions, and lets os HostCaller.cs go)
Soft: the dead-code deletion of release R1 (step 1 rides it)
Review confidence
medium

What is wrong today

App is a programming model whose base class is in the framework and whose only production host is in os. The app object is a field under a lock (src\DigitalBrain\Apps\AppOfTState.cs:11-35) run by a hand-made mailbox (Apps\Hosting\AppTurns.cs:49-75); the os host then adds a best-effort listener, one Task.Run per request, a SemaphoreSlim per app, a substitute caller, a reflection argument binder and render-by-member-name (CompiledAppsHost.cs:87-104, 631-674, 713-754; InProcessAppHost.cs:106-115). Measured: 6 Task.Run and 4 SemaphoreSlim lines in CompiledAppsHost.cs, 13 HostCaller lines in CompiledAppsHost.cs and AppPresentationSink.cs. The defects that follow are verified by reading: two Ino [On("source")] reactions are never subscribed in production while Watches() are served only in production; a journaled request can be dropped; two quick requests are not ordered; state is silo memory; an operation the manifest declares and the class lacks is dropped and reported as done; an app's Get<T> takes its brain from the ambient caller and falls back to the literal "owner". The cost sits in os: 82 commits and 25 fixes on APPS\Hosting and APPS\Runtimes against 14 commits and 0 fixes on src\DigitalBrain\Apps. Five apps keep 15 mutable cache fields beside State, and RequestLedger, AppKey, IAppHost, App.Present and App.GetCapability have 0 os callers.

Design principle

One concept, "a stateful thing that reacts to signals one turn at a time and shows a view", has two models (the unit's StateNeuron inside the actor system, App outside it), and the App aggregate is split over an in-memory object, private fields, a records neuron, two view neurons and a host singleton that holds its invariants. The target makes the screen one aggregate with one consistency boundary: state and view are saved as one journal entry in one grain turn, [On] means one thing, and the model is written once in the core. The os edge stays the anti-corruption layer: the public view name does not change.

Defects this removes

  • os\src\IntoChat.OS.Apps\Ino\InoApp.cs:159 and :580: [On("grants/default")] and [On("settings")] are never subscribed in production; only the test host subscribes SignalSubscriptions (src\DigitalBrain.Testing\TestOS\Apps\InstalledApp.cs:98-114) and os reads them only to draw graph edges (APPS\Brain\AppGraphSource.cs:94-99).
  • The reverse split: App.Watches() are served only by the os host (CompiledAppsHost.cs:607, HostedAppWatches.cs:31) and never by TestOS; os tests call the handler by hand (TasksAppFacts.cs:303,312; InoAppFacts.cs:345; CreatorAppFacts.cs:347).
  • A journaled request can be lost before it runs: src\DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans\Signalling\ClusterListeners.cs:23 (bounded channel, DropOldest) and :18 (not replayed after a silo stop); the retained copy written at AppNeuron.cs:339-343 has no caller in os\src (RetainedRequests, AppNeuron.Installation.cs:301).
  • Request order is not guaranteed: CompiledAppsHost.cs:102 starts one Task.Run per AppRequested and the per-app semaphore is taken inside the task (:637-639).
  • App state is silo memory: src\DigitalBrain\Apps\AppOfTState.cs:12 (a field), held in InProcessAppHost.cs:15; lost on restart, one copy per silo.
  • An operation the manifest declares and the class has no [On] for is dropped silently and reported as done: CompiledAppsHost.cs:645-648 then :660. The two operation-name normalizers disagree (AppDefinition.cs:89 strips '-'; SpecComment.cs:129-139 strips '-', '_' and whitespace).
  • An app's Get<T>(key) takes the brain from the ambient caller stamp and falls back to the literal "owner" (APPS\AppsModule.cs:77-84); a turn that has no stamp gets a substitute caller, the brain owner or a platform caller built from three copies of the brain id (APPS\Hosting\HostCaller.cs:16-35). A screen addresses its own brain (Id.Brain) and runs under the caller of the call.
  • At-least-once watch delivery with no dedupe is patched by hand in app state: InoApp.cs:323-325 (SeenProgress, 256 ids); RequestLedger, the framework's answer, has 0 os callers.
  • Hard-coded tenant in the host: CompiledAppsHost.cs:106 TriggerEnsureAll("owner", ...) (the same line remains in FilesHost.cs:29, which this move does not touch).
  • Found while re-reading, fixed in os step 4 when HostedAppTypes is rewritten: AppNeuron.cs:58 calls IsHostLoaded(Name()) with the app name, but HostedAppTypes.cs:11-12 looks the argument up as an app key (InProcessAppHost.cs:50, keyed from CompiledAppsHost.cs:563), so the branch that keeps a shipped app's pre-0.18 record usable can never be taken.

A field under a lock plus a hand-made mailbox becomes a neuron turn that saves state and view together

No lock, no mailbox, no resolver delegate, no ledger: the host grain already gives one turn at a time, and StoredState<TState>, PublishedView, ViewChanged and the two internal storage hooks of Neuron<TState> already exist for the unit's StateNeuron. The class calls no Orleans API and is in the core, so both runtimes host it with no adapter code. The core gains a project reference to DigitalBrain.UI (724 lines, no dependency).

Listener, Task.Run, substitute caller, semaphore and reflection binder become one call on the screen

A click is ordered by the grain mailbox, runs under the caller the edge stamped, and a signal with no reaction throws to the clicker. On the one-way Request path the same throw reaches nobody; AppNeuron's OperationIsDeclared check stays the guard there. The edge does need a change: the Dart shell and OsWorkspaceRoot build the name {brain}/apps/{app}/view themselves, so ViewAlias replaces UnitAlias at AppUiEndpoints.cs:52 and :155 and the name stays as it is.

The same app: Calculator as a screen

Handler bodies and the view are the same; Set becomes Show and the history survives a restart. The [Signal] records the app already declares stop being DTOs for a reflection binder: the view emits one and the reaction receives it. Render is an abstract member of the base, not a method found by its name. Emits is an instance method; the 59 emit lines that sit in static renderer classes keep AppSignals.Emit, whose body is rerouted through ScreenCatalog.

'Render cannot await' caches and watch pumps become state and durable synapses

The no-op Set whose only job was to make the host render again is gone. Small read models go into the state; a large one (Ino's transcript) stays a field loaded in OnActivate. Follow is Context.Synapses.Ensure, which os production code already calls in 15 places: a durable synapse on both runtimes, replacing 116 lines of pump-with-backoff that only production ran. The os snippets carry no ConfigureAwait because os\src\IntoChat.OS.Apps has none; the core snippets keep ConfigureAwait(true).

Steps, each shippable

Risks

  • Stored format for UI state: 14 state records plus their views are written on every interaction; a shape that does not round-trip now breaks an activation after a deploy, where a restart used to heal everything. Guard: ScreenBaseline round-trips every state; additive changes only.
  • A handler holds a real grain turn and the edge call waits for it. 17 ArtCraft-family classes await engine.Execute and Markdraw/DataStudio await VFS I/O; durations are not measured. A long handler must start the work one-way on its worker neuron and react to the completion signal. A neuron the handler awaits that calls back into the screen now deadlocks until the call timeout; today's host is outside the actor system and cannot.
  • The live view-change path is not traced. The client watches a view through IView and the os ViewChanged (os\ui\core\lib\src\node_contracts.dart:16-19); a screen publishes the core ViewChanged from another neuron. If mapping it costs more than estimated, the fallback is that ShellScreen also publishes each view to the os IView neuron (one more write per change, no edge or client change).
  • Caller in signal-triggered reactions: a synapse delivery has no person stamp, and os ownership checks accept only User, Platform or App callers. Until the typed-caller move lands, ShellScreen stamps the app caller around reactions in one place; that is why it lives in the Apps module, where HostCaller is internal.
  • IScreen.Request accepts any signal the class has an [On] for, including signals meant to arrive only through a synapse (FlowsChanged, FieldChanged). Any caller allowed to call the screen can trigger a refresh. ScreenCatalog limits the os path to declared operations; the core does not.
  • A Follow that throws in OnActivate fails the activation, where a failed watch retried with backoff. Optional sources are wrapped in try/Swallow.
  • Two hosts coexist between os deploys 1 and 2 (AppNeuron.Request routes by ScreenCatalog). Keep the window short.
  • Journal volume: each Show writes the state and the whole view JSON. Writes per click drop from at least 3 to 1, but entry sizes are not measured; the step adds the StateNeuron.MaxStateBytes cap to StoreState.
  • The core gains a package dependency on DigitalBrain.UI, reversing today's rule (ViewNodes.cs:9-10 says the core does not reference it). The alternative is the class in DigitalBrain.UI with InternalsVisibleTo from the core. Pack, PublicAPI files and the unit sandbox reference set must be checked either way.
  • Nothing was compiled. One point is marked ASSUMPTION in the core code (System.Text.Json and declared default parameters) and one in the os code (IHostedAppTypes.IsScreen). Calculator was read in full and Tasks in the quoted parts; the other 14 app files were measured by grep.
  • The [Scenario] attribute and the BDD report neuron go with BddRunner; if an in-product scenario report is wanted it has to be rebuilt on TestOS.

Proof it worked

ScreenFacts pass on TestOS and, unchanged, on the silo through BothRuntimesFixture (the class is in the core; neither runtime gains code). New facts that cannot be written against today's host: 50 presses sent without waiting give the expected expression (order); os.Restart() then Read returns the same generation and JSON (durability); on Testing.Silo a grant change reaches the Ino screen (today only on TestOS). Greps after os deploy 2: Task.Run( in CompiledAppsHost.cs 6 -> 0, SemaphoreSlim 4 -> 0; 'AppTurns|InProcessAppHost|HostedAppWatches|IPresentationSink' in os\src -> 0; classes deriving DigitalBrain.App 30 -> 0; Listen<AppRequested> 2 -> 1 (FilesHost); 'apps/' view names in os\ui unchanged (git diff empty for live_desktop_shell.dart and workspace_app.dart). After digitalbrain R2: src\DigitalBrain\Apps holds SpecComment.cs only and typeof(App) has no hit in src. Live on aspire start: one Calculator press is one IViewReader.Activate and one journal entry from app.calculator carrying synapse.state-changed and synapse.view-changed, where today it is IApp.Emit, IApp.Read twice, IView.Publish, IApp.Present and IApp.Progress; the open window shows the new view without a reload.

Your decision

Is it acceptable that first-party screen state becomes stored state: 14 state records and their views written to the journal on every interaction, with the additive-fields discipline that implies? If yes, do this move as designed. If no, the only alternative is to move about 3,250 src lines of App, analyzers and test kit to os, which removes nothing, fixes none of the listed defects, and needs TestOS.Track (internal, TestOS.cs:520) made public.
