R11 · Work · wave 2

Give Operation, Approval and Flow their lifecycles in core

Operation, confirmation and flow state get pure transitions in the core; the operation and confirmations neurons are hosted once for both runtimes; storage plugs in through IOperationHandler<TPlan>; the approval wait becomes a saved state; moving flow bodies out of the turn is a separate, optional release.

Net
src about -370 after release D1 (2,163 deleted measured + 30 est. / 1,824 added est.), about -570 if D2 ships; 181 lines moved unchanged / tests -100 measured, +500 est., 2,455 lines in 7 files retargeted (measured) / os about -25 est., about 11 files touched before D2
Effort
L (about 2 weeks for A, B, C, D1; D2 adds about a week): four digitalbrain releases and two required os production bumps, two runtimes to host the moved neurons on, 2,455 lines of tests in 7 files to retarget, and for D2 a change to how all 7 os flows treat their inner state.
Depends on
R07 (Context.Caller in the core: the person rule, the requester principal and Caller.Via for RaisedBy)
R14 (the kit that runs the confirmations and operation facts on both runtimes)
R05 (soft: with a one-way nudge, delivery provably never runs in the publisher's turn, which the Outcome read-back needs)
Review confidence
medium

What is wrong today

Three aggregates of one context have state records with no behaviour, and their lifecycles sit in per-runtime plumbing. OperationState has Running/Done only (Runtime.Orleans/Operations/OperationState.cs:6-13); the up-to-5-minute approval wait is a Task.Run closure (RuntimeOperations.cs:46,61-85) plus a TaskCompletionSource in KernelConfirmations._pending (:18), so nothing durable links an operation to its question. RuntimeOperations type-switches on WritePlan (:44, :130), OperationNeuron names WritePlan (:83-85) and publishes storage.operation.* for every plan type (:95,:112); 6 of the 12 files in Runtime.Orleans/Operations (945 lines) name storage types. Operations and confirmations are written again for the simulator (FakeOperations runner mode in Fakes.cs:290-449, TestConfirmations 177 lines, TestOSOperations 40), and TestConfirmations lacks four limits the silo enforces. Flow.cs (617 lines) has 13 inline "State with" on a FlowState<T> with zero methods, 8 volatile instance fields, a detached Ask relayed back through proof-guarded self-calls (:410-454, :228-244), an [Interleaves] Cancel that may not save (:148-196), and it names Orleans.Runtime.SiloUnavailableException by string (:611). PoliciesNeuron (:61-77) and InstallsNeuron (:104-135) act on any ConfirmationResolved with a matching plan id without checking the answer.

Design principle

An aggregate owns its lifecycle and its invariants; here the lifecycles (awaiting approval, answered, cancel requested, step interrupted) live in closures, in-memory flags and adapters, once per runtime. The target puts each lifecycle on its state record as pure transitions returning (Next, Signals), hosts the two kernel neurons from the core on both runtimes, and inverts the storage dependency (the kernel declares IOperationHandler<TPlan>, the storage package implements it).

Defects this removes

  • An operation waiting for approval holds its wait in a Task.Run closure and is saved as Running, Fraction 0 with no plan id (RuntimeOperations.cs:46,61-67; OperationState.cs:35-51). After a silo restart OperationNeuron.Reconcile (OperationNeuron.cs:54-67) ends it 'interrupted' while the question stays pending in the confirmations neuron until it expires; approving it changes nothing.
  • PoliciesNeuron.OnResolved adopts a rule (PoliciesNeuron.cs:61-77) and InstallsNeuron.Judge completes an install (InstallsNeuron.cs:104-108,127-135) on any ConfirmationResolved with a matching plan id, from any publisher (both subscribe brain-wide: PoliciesNeuron.cs:126, InstallsNeuron.cs:209), without reading the answer from the confirmations neuron; the plan id is itself journaled (PoliciesNeuron.cs:52).
  • A write the policy does not ask about leaves no audit record: StorageOperationGate.cs:77 skips Confirm when ConfirmationRules.Asks is false, against IConfirmations.cs:6 ('Every plan ends with one ConfirmationResolved, skipped ones included').
  • Every operation, whatever its plan, publishes storage.operation.progress and storage.operation.done, and only WritePlan operations publish a started signal (OperationNeuron.cs:83-85,95,112).
  • KernelConfirmations._brains keeps one entry per plan answered at once or cancelled: added at KernelConfirmations.cs:109, removed only in CompletePending (:88); the paths at :113-117 and :134-143 remove from _pending only.
  • The simulator's confirmations lack four limits of the silo's: id length, 20 pending, expiry cap, ending the plan when the token fires (TestConfirmations.cs:95-138 vs ConfirmationsNeuron.cs:37,46-49 and KernelConfirmations.cs:134-139).
  • Two question builders with different wording (StorageOperationGate.cs:20-52 on Orleans operations; StorageConfirmation.For through StorageWriteRunner.cs:77-88 on the simulator and the direct door), and the stale-item check exists on Orleans only (StorageOperationGate.cs:89-96 vs TestNeuronContext.cs:25).
  • StorageOperationGate.Run, Execute, CopyAcross, Same and VerifyFailed (StorageOperationGate.cs:10,55-61,100-144) have no production caller (ConfirmingOperationGate.cs:26 calls Ask only); 2 facts keep a second copy-verify-delete algorithm alive.
  • The Orleans-free core names an Orleans type by string and finds IHostApplicationLifetime by reflection (Flow.cs:600,611). Fixed in D1.
  • D2 only: Flow.Cancel returns before the cancel is saved: _cancelRequested is a field (Flow.cs:153), CancelRequested is a one-way self-call (:160, :560-561) and the Cancelled save happens in that later turn (:185-186); an activation that ends in between loses a cancel the caller was told succeeded.
  • D2 only: Flow.cs:482 keeps a static ConcurrentDictionary<Type, bool> keyed by GetType(); for a flow class in a collectible assembly whose T is not collectible, that static pins the assembly.

The approval wait becomes a saved state of the operation

Line 67 of the before block is where an operation sits for up to five minutes as Running, Fraction 0, inside a thread-pool closure. After, the wait is a phase with the plan id in it: a reactivated operation without inline bytes reads the answer and goes on, and OperationView can say 'waiting for approval'. The enum and the property keep their names (OperationPhase, Phase), so the saved JSON is unchanged. IOperationGate, ConfirmingOperationGate and StorageOperationGate (251 lines) have no successor.

The kernel stops naming WritePlan: a handler port, implemented by storage

Describe is async and takes a scope: the storage question needs Stat calls and an owner per brain and origin (StorageOperationGate.cs:68-76). The simulator line at the bottom of the before block is the second implementation of the same lifecycle; after, both contexts use the one core NeuronOperations, and one question builder and one stale check run on both runtimes. OperationWait.For (core, est. 12) polls IOperationsQuery.Read every 100 ms, as RuntimeOperations.WaitFor does today (:109-120).

Flow: the approval relay goes (D1); cancel becomes one saved transition (D2, optional)

The before block exists because the body is awaited inside the turn (Flow.cs:334), so Cancel must interleave and may not save. D1 removes the approval relay without touching that. D2 removes it, and costs a contract change: os step bodies mutate the flow's inner state in place (AgentStep.cs:63-66) and reactions do too (:77, :86), which is safe today only because a body holds the neuron.

Confirmation rules once, on the state; the neuron and the simulator are shells

The before block is a request/response rebuilt from a cluster listener, a TCS map and a 50 ms poll; its _brains map (line 109) is not cleaned on the immediate-answer path. The simulator copy below it passes the uncapped plan.Expiry and has no id or pending limit. After, the rules are one record with no host in it, so FakeNeuronContext (no neuron host), TestOS and the silo all run them. ConfirmationPlan and ConfirmationsNeuron keep their names; Principal(caller) maps R07's Actor to ConfirmationPrincipal as R07's own rewrite of this neuron does.

Deleted lines

Risks

  • Release D2 lets reactions, reminders and Resume run while a step body is in flight. os bodies and reactions mutate State.Inner in place (AgentStep.cs:63-66,77,86): a reaction can save a half-written Inner, and TurnCompleted can arrive before inner.RunId is assigned and be dropped on a wait with no expiry. D2 must not ship before the 7 os flows are changed and their tests run on both runtimes.
  • Context.Detach on Orleans (needed by the operation neuron in C) relies on a task started in a grain turn running on the activation's scheduler and interleaving at awaits. Flow.Ask, PoliciesNeuron.Ask and InstallsNeuron.Ask rely on the same today, but it is not in orleans-facts.md; the code carries an ASSUMPTION comment. The keep-alive works through this repo's own DelayDeactivation call (NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:291).
  • Reactions that read the answer back (Outcome) call the confirmations neuron from inside a reaction. This is safe only if signal delivery never runs inside the publisher's turn; not verified for either runtime.
  • The core ConfirmationsNeuron needs a runtime-neutral caller (R07). Without R07 release B cannot ship; the mapping from Actor to ConfirmationPrincipal follows R07's design text, not final code.
  • Inline bytes of a plan now cross one grain call as base64 to the operation neuron (today they stay in the starter's closure). An operation with inline bytes cannot resume after a reactivation during the approval wait; it ends 'interrupted' and its question is withdrawn.
  • Every handled write the policy does not ask about journals three more entries than today (operation.awaiting, the skipped confirmation.resolved, a progress 0). If that is too much, the neuron reads the policy and skips the phase for plans it does not ask about.
  • Two implementations of IOperationDriverLifetime will exist (os LocalOperationDriverLifetime, registered with TryAddSingleton at OrleansHostWiring.cs:29, and the runtime's SiloDriverLifetime). States saved before C have Driver and possibly no DriverLifetime: the neuron must keep treating Driver as the fallback token or old running operations never reconcile.
  • A tracked (adapter-driven) operation learns of a cancel at its next Report instead of at once on the same silo (the local OperationRegistry is gone).
  • AwaitedConfirmations sees an answer given on another silo up to 1 second late (today the cluster listener delivers it at once). The three blocking os adapters are its only production users.
  • Simulator tests that read a WritePlan's result right after Start must await the operation; how many do is not counted. How many of the 33 FlowFacts depend on a body holding the turn is not counted either.
  • The write-grant protocol (StorageWriteAuthorizer, 142 lines) and the operation-inside-operation in the four os adapters remain. R11 moves the file but not the cause (IStorage.Write is both app command and adapter primitive).

Proof it worked

Facts that must hold afterwards: (1) grep of src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans for 'WritePlan' returns only files under Capabilities/, and the Operations and Confirmations folders do not exist; (2) after D1, src/DigitalBrain/Flows/Flow.cs contains no 'State with', no 'Orleans', no 'Context.Confirm' and no '_asking'; after D2 also no '[Interleaves]', no 'Proof' and one private mutable field; (3) grep for 'TaskCompletionSource' in confirmation code finds ApprovalWaiters and the TestConfirmations shell only; (4) os builds with FilesWorkspaceNeuron.cs:319 unchanged. Tests: FlowStateFacts, OperationStateFacts and ConfirmationStateFacts exercise every transition without a host; the confirmations conformance set and the retargeted OperationEntityFacts run the same facts on TestOS and on the silo; new facts 'approval wait survives reactivation', 'skipped plan is audited', 'item changed after approval is plan-invalid', 'a question that cannot be raised ends the operation', 'a forged ConfirmationResolved adopts no policy and completes no install', 'a plan raised again by the nudge under another actor is a no-op'. After release A, FlowFacts and FlowRuntimeFacts pass unmodified. In os after C, a delete approved after a silo restart completes and the Files app can show 'waiting for approval' from OperationAwaitingApproval.

Your decision

Ship D2 or stop at D1. D1 already removes the approval relay and the Orleans name from Flow.cs with no behaviour change. D2 makes Cancel durable and takes Flow.cs from about 450 to about 235 lines, but reactions and Resume then run while a step body is in flight, and the 7 os flows, which mutate their inner state in place, must be changed first. Second, smaller: move the tracker vocabulary (FlowStatus.Blocked, RelationKind, Relate) to os now (16 os files) or leave it.

Review notes on this proposal

Quote check. 4 of 4 before-snippets (7 file ranges: RuntimeOperations.cs:44-85 and :130-142, OperationNeuron.cs:83-86, TestNeuronContext.cs:24-25, Flow.cs:148-186, KernelConfirmations.cs:101-133, TestConfirmations.cs:95-110 with lines 101-109 elided) were compared line by line with the files at HEAD a275f82. All are verbatim and the line ranges are right; nothing had to be fixed in the quotes. The errors were in counts, the caller census and the after code.

Corrections the reviewer made

  • Renamed nothing in the core that collides with os: the designer's DigitalBrain.Confirmation.IApprovals / ApprovalsNeuron would be ambiguous (CS0104) in 7 os files that import both DigitalBrain.Confirmation and IntoChat.OS.Modules.Apps.Approvals, where os already has IApprovals and ApprovalsNeuron. The core neuron stays ConfirmationsNeuron; the new contract is IConfirmationRequests.
  • Added the missing os break: FilesWorkspaceNeuron.cs:319 calls Context.Operations.Reconcile (OperationRecovery.cs:10), a type the designer deleted with 'no os users', and :9 imports the namespace being removed. OperationRecovery now moves to the core.
  • Removed [Obsolete] from IConfirmations.Confirm / Context.Confirm: os has TreatWarningsAsErrors and does not suppress CS0618 (os/Directory.Build.props), so BuildFlow.cs:257 would stop compiling, not 'get a warning'.
  • FakeOperations and TestConfirmations are not deleted: FakeNeuronContext exposes both by type (Fakes.cs:551, :571) and has no neuron host; 11 os and 2 digitalbrain test files use it. Only FakeOperations' runner mode goes (est. 30 lines); TestConfirmations becomes a shell over the same pure rules.
  • Moved the confirmation rules onto ConfirmationState as pure transitions (Request, Answer, Withdraw). The designer applied 'lifecycle on the state' to Operation and Flow but left Approval's rules inline in the neuron, which is also what made a host-free simulator copy impossible. Code pair 4 rewritten accordingly.
  • Fixed a compile error: FlowState<T> already has a member named Ended (DateTimeOffset?, FlowState.cs:125); the designer's bool Ended is now IsOver, in FlowState and every use in Flow.
  • Fixed INeuronContext.Detach's default: the designer forwarded it to Defer, which Orleans drains and awaits inside the same grain request (NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:148,343; protocol-twice D8), so the default would have held the turn. Removed two invented members (host.OS.Stopping, HostNeuronContext._activationEnds) and shortened the keep-alive from 1 hour to a 1-minute period.
  • Fixed StorageWriteHandler: the designer's NeuronOperations.WaitFor(provider, id, ct) had no brain in scope. Inner operations are now read through OperationScope.Operations (new member) with a core OperationWait helper.
  • Fixed OperationNeuron.Prepared: it is one-way, so an exception from Request (20 pending, invalid plan) left the operation AwaitingApproval forever with no question. It now completes the operation as Failed('denied').
  • Kept OperationPhase/Phase instead of the designer's OperationStatus + [JsonPropertyName("Phase")]: RuntimeProtocol.Json is camelCase Web defaults, so the attribute was noise; keeping the names keeps the saved JSON by construction.
  • Kept the persisted signal aliases storage.operation.{started,progress,done} instead of renaming them to operation.*: aliases are stored in journal entries and subscription rows, and SynapseEngine.cs:259,486 silently drops aliases that no longer resolve. StorageContractFacts.cs:216-218 pins them.
  • Made plan re-raising robust: the designer's rule compared the requester principal derived from the ambient actor, so a nudge re-raise (kernel) of a plan first raised under a person would throw and Flow.Ask would fail the flow. Idempotence is now keyed on the raising neuron (ConfirmationEntry.RaisedBy, additive).
  • Aligned the confirmations neuron with R07's actual design text: Context.Caller is non-null, Actor.Person has Principal (not Id), the host refuses other brains before the method runs, so OwnBrain and the null check are gone.
  • Split release D into D1 (approval relay and HostShutdown removed, bodies stay in the turn, no behaviour change) and D2 (bodies leave the turn, owner's decision). Reason verified in os: step bodies and reactions mutate State.Inner in place (AgentStep.cs:63-66,77,86); 'AgentStep.cs: 1 line' was not true.
  • Reworked release B so it is shippable alone: KernelConfirmations is cut to a small poller and deleted in C with its last user (ConfirmingOperationGate.cs:21), instead of deleting IConfirmationContexts while the Operations folder still names it. Moved INeuronContext.Detach from D to C, where the operation neuron first needs it.
  • Corrected counts: _cancelRequested has 6 'is not null' and 3 'is null' tests (not ten); FlowFacts has 33 facts and 4 lines naming callbacks/proof/shutdown (not 33 lines); 5 digitalbrain test files name KernelConfirmations/IConfirmationContexts, including FlowRuntimeFacts.cs:24,31, which therefore does not 'run unchanged'; 1 os file names ConfirmationsNeuron (not 2); 16 os files name the tracker vocabulary (not 15); 2 of 7 StorageOperationGateFacts (37 lines) are deleted, not 'about 110 lines'.
  • Added breaks the designer missed: BrainHosting.cs:41 names StorageWriteAuthorizer by full namespace; Runtime.Http/Storage/ContentUploadLog.cs uses OperationDone; RuntimeServicesHosting.cs:27-29 and RuntimeSiloBuilderExtensions.cs:96 register deleted types; PublicAPI.Unshipped.txt files must change with every public add/remove; the storage question wording changes for users on Orleans.
  • Added verified defects: InstallsNeuron.cs:104-135 trusts the signal exactly as PoliciesNeuron does (the designer had not re-read it); StorageOperationGate.Run/Execute/CopyAcross have no production caller; Flow.cs:482 is a static Type-keyed cache. Marked the two Flow.Cancel/Type-cache fixes as D2-only.
  • Recomputed LOC: net src about -370 after D1 and about -570 with D2 (designer: -820, which assumed FakeOperations and TestConfirmations vanish and D2 ships). Retargeted tests are 2,455 lines in 7 files (FlowRuntimeFacts added).
  • Stated costs the designer left out: inline bytes cross a grain call as base64; three extra journal entries per unasked write; two IOperationDriverLifetime implementations and the Driver fallback for old states; cancel reaches a tracked operation at its next Report.

Doubts that remain

  • Nothing was compiled or run. The after code was checked by reading against the types that remain; R07's Caller/Actor shape is taken from R07's design text, not from code.
  • Whether a task started in a grain turn and not awaited runs on the activation's scheduler is not in orleans-facts.md. Release C's operation neuron depends on it (Context.Detach); the repo depends on it today in three places.
  • Whether signal delivery can run inside the publisher's turn on either runtime was not verified. If it can, the Outcome read-back from a reaction deadlocks against a non-reentrant confirmations neuron.
  • How TestOS dispatches a call on an internal contract of the core assembly (IOperationNeuron, IFlowTurns) was not checked; only proxy creation is covered by the Orleans precedent.
  • The claim that a nudge re-raise runs under a different actor than the first raise follows from R07's text ('a reminder runs as the kernel'); whether today's code already fails flows this way was not tested.
  • The state store key is (state name, grain id); the payload format was not read, so the namespace move of ConfirmationState and OperationState is unverified beyond the key.
  • Counts of tests that depend on a body holding the turn (FlowFacts, os flow tests) and on a WritePlan finishing inside Start are unknown.
  • Additions are estimates. The Flow.cs figure after D1 (about 450 lines) is the least certain.

R12 · Identity & Connections · wave 2

Model identity and connections as right-sized aggregates

Write the identity rules once as a pure model (BrainDirectory, AccountBook, SessionBook) run by the simulator's reference identity and by Identity.Local behind one Orleans-free bound wrapper, make ConnectionState an immutable record saved as Save(next, fact), and replace the hand-written connection index and catalog with one projection fed by the facts a connection already publishes.

Net
src: 2,563 measured deleted or rewritten, about 2,027 est. written = about -540 (est.), about -660 with the four surrogates (120 measured, last step); 72 of the deleted lines are stamp lines R07's ledger also counts / tests: -140 measured, +570 est. = about +430 (on purpose: the rules become testable without a runtime and four unpinned rules get pinned) / os: -156 measured, +70 est., plus mechanical edits (9 state classes to records, 35 + 26 assignment lines, 4 overrides; 157 Snapshot renames one release later)
Effort
L (1-2 weeks): about 5 days for the four framework steps, half a day for step 5 once R07 exists, 2-3 days for the os bump with its live gate; nine connectors change in one production deploy.
Depends on
R07 (explicit Caller, one access decision): only for step 5, deleting the three forwarder classes; R07's own ledger removes their 23 stamp blocks
R04 (one commit path): not required; it decides whether connector-private saves (10 os SaveQuietly sites, 21 more through Gmail's) keep the filler signal ConnectionUpdated. This move keeps SaveQuietly as it is
Kernel subscription and nudge move (judgment move 6): not required; it closes the lost-doorbell window and can remove the remaining Subscribed() call if it offers a subscription that starts at the journal's first position
Review confidence
medium

What is wrong today

Identity has no model, only three implementations of one: the rule methods of DirectoryState in Identity.Local (DirectoryNeuron.cs:188-366, file 658 lines), InMemoryIdentity in Testing (479 lines; it is the identity under TestOS, AppBaseline.cs:38) and os's DirectoryNeuron plus DirectoryPort (518 + 215 lines). They have drifted on access rules: the in-memory copy has no owner check on EnsureMember / RemoveMember / RemovePrincipalMember (InMemoryIdentity.cs:236-280; the local neuron checks at DirectoryNeuron.cs:509,529,548 and os at DirectoryPort.cs:80,86,92) and never spends a Once grant (:302 against DirectoryNeuron.cs:284-300). No conformance fact pins either rule, and os runs the identity kit only against the framework's in-memory directory (os IdentityCapabilityKits.cs:57-63), so its own directory is under no kit at all. Sessions sit in the same state: every Issue rewrites and journals all seven arrays, with raw session ids in inspectable state (DirectoryNeuron.cs:118-128,627), under a signal DirectoryChanged(scope) that has 0 subscribers. ConnectionNeuron mutates the live state before the write (ConnectionNeuron.cs:22,108-109,315-324), so it carries Restore by reflection, CopyBaseFrom, a fault seam with 0 implementers and, in os, GmailState.Copy() to compensate. A connection already publishes connection.connected / expired / failing / disconnected, which have 0 subscribers in either repo, and then maintains an index neuron and a catalog neuron by hand from six operations (three journal writes sequenced in one turn): a crash after the first leaves a connected connection unlisted and unbindable, a listing costs 2 + N neuron calls, and Identity.Local's copy lists an unreadable connection as 'connected' (ConnectionRequestsNeuron.cs:64-81).

Design principle

An aggregate is the consistency boundary of its rules, a bounded context has one model, and a read model is derived from facts, not maintained by commands issued from inside the aggregate. Today the identity rules live in three adapters and the connection aggregate writes its own read models. The target puts the invariants in pure records that return the next state plus typed facts, keeps every host a thin store around them, and derives the catalog from the connection's facts through the kernel's subscription and cursor.

Defects this removes

  • ConnectionNeuron.cs:108-109,315-316,324: state mutated before Save; a failed write leaves an unsaved credential and status in memory (the kernel swaps at NeuronOfTState.cs:45-46; only os GmailNeuron.cs:47-65 compensates).
  • ConnectionNeuron.cs:109-111 with LocalConnections.cs:32-36 and os ConnectionRequestsNeuron.cs:49: three writes sequenced by hand; a crash after the first leaves a connected connection untracked, unlisted and unbindable until it is reconnected.
  • Identity.Local only: ConnectionRequestsNeuron.cs:64,67,72,79-81 with LocalConnections.cs:85: an unhosted, throwing or unconfigured connection is listed as connected and treated as bindable (os's copy reads without a default, os ConnectionRequestsNeuron.cs:52-56).
  • ConnectionRequestsNeuron.cs:133-139 with LocalConnections.cs:66-70 (and os ConnectionRequestsNeuron.cs:99): pending requests are never removed, so the first request of a kind suppresses every later one.
  • ConnectionNeuron.cs:237 -> :325 with ConnectionRequestsNeuron.cs:35-45 (os :33-38): every OAuth token refresh bumps the catalog revision and publishes ConnectionsChanged although nothing a consumer can see changed; os BuildFlow re-checks on each (BuildFlow.cs:105,373).
  • InMemoryIdentity.cs:236-280: no owner check on EnsureMember, RemoveMember, RemovePrincipalMember in the identity every TestOS app test runs (the local directory checks at DirectoryNeuron.cs:509,529,548, os at DirectoryPort.cs:80,86,92); no conformance fact pins the rule.
  • InMemoryIdentity.cs:302: a Once grant is never spent (spent at DirectoryNeuron.cs:284-300).
  • DirectoryNeuron.cs:403 and InMemoryIdentity.cs:113: CreateBrain idempotency keyed by operation id alone; another account that reuses the id is handed the first account's owner Member (os keys by account and operation, os DirectoryNeuron.cs:120).
  • DirectoryNeuron.cs:118-128,627,635 with NeuronOfTState.cs:16-20: raw session ids in inspectable state, sessions removed only by End, all seven arrays journaled per Issue.
  • ConnectionIndexNeuron.cs:44-48: saves under the base class with an empty signal list, then mutates the 'immutable' state in place (removed with the index).
  • os IdentityCapabilityKits.cs:57-63: os's only identity conformance run is the framework's capability over the framework's in-memory directory; os's own DirectoryPort (215 lines) and DirectoryNeuron (518) are under no kit. Step 6 adds the kit.
  • Doc defects, not behaviour: IIdentityDirectoryPort.cs:52 and IIdentity.cs:55 promise null where both real directories throw (DirectoryNeuron.cs:509, os DirectoryPort.cs:80,158); IGrants.cs:17 promises revoked grants are listed and both implementations filter them (InMemoryIdentity.cs:315, DirectoryNeuron.cs:576). The comments are corrected.

The membership rule written three times becomes one pure aggregate method

The second 'before' is the copy every TestOS app test runs: it has no owner check at all. os holds the third copy and checks in its port (os DirectoryPort.cs:77-81,152-159). After, the rule exists once and takes time as an argument, so it is tested with no runtime; a non-owner gets the exception production already throws. The stored shape does not change: no migration.

Two hand-written forwarding adapters become one bound wrapper shared by both runtimes

The test adapter is the same forwarder written a second time because the first lives in the Orleans package. BoundIdentity serves the tests from step 3; the Orleans registration waits for R07, because the Stamp(PlatformCaller) blocks are load-bearing until the kernel carries the caller. LocalDirectoryPort and IDirectory stay: they are the port-to-neuron adapter, not a copy of a rule.

Mutate-then-restore becomes compute next, Save(next, fact)

Deleted with this: Restore, ConnectionState.CopyBaseFrom and its eight void mutators, IConnectionWriteFaults (0 implementers), Track / NotifyCatalog, and in os GmailState.Copy() and Gmail's Restore call. One call to the catalog remains (Subscribed, once per activation, no data); it goes when the kernel offers a subscription that starts at the journal's first position. Read-only interfaces instead of immutable collections keep the persisted JSON types and most os code unchanged.

Index and catalog written by hand become a projection of connection facts

The connection facts had 0 subscribers in either repo; now they are the mechanism. The listing drops from 2 + N neuron calls to 1, the fail-open 'connected' default is gone, and the Context.State.Save(next, []) bypass disappears with the index. os deletes its own copies of both neurons and hosts this one. The seed runs in OnActivate, not through Defer: deferred work is drained inside the same grain call (NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:137-148), where a reaction could interleave with it.

Steps, each shippable

  1. 1. digitalbrain, framework release N. Immutable ConnectionState: a record with init members in the same order (same JSON), IReadOnlyDictionary / IReadOnlyList, a sealed redacting ToString; ConnectionTransitions; ConnectionNeuron computes next and calls Save; AcceptGrantAsync becomes (grant, state) -> (grant, state); add ConnectionAdopted / ConnectionShared / ConnectionUnshared and the optional ProbedAt. Delete Restore, CopyBaseFrom, the eight void mutators, IConnectionWriteFaults and the Faults hooks. Keep Snapshot, PublishAsync and Commit as plain one-line forwards for one release (not [Obsolete]: both repos build with TreatWarningsAsErrors). The base still calls the index and the catalog in this step. Tests: ConnectionNeuronFacts (DemoConnectionState becomes a record) and ConnectionKeysFacts.cs:27 (assignment becomes an initializer) retargeted; add ConnectionStateFacts and AFailedSaveLeavesTheConnectionUntouched (a throwing INeuronState); PersistedStateFacts unchanged and green.
  2. 2. digitalbrain, release N. Catalog projection: add ConnectionCatalog, ConnectionCatalogNeuron, IConnectionCatalog, IConnectorOffers (Declared, Offered, ProvidesOf) with a default over IReadOnlyList<ConnectorDefinition> + ConnectionNeuronTypes, and the public one-release LegacyConnectionIndexNeuron; move the rule IntegrationIsAvailable into Capabilities.Connections. ConnectionNeuron drops Track / Untrack / NotifyCatalog and gains Record. LocalConnections reads the catalog in ListInOrder and Bind. Delete IConnectionIndex, IConnectionCatalogUpdates, Identity.Local's ConnectionIndexNeuron and ConnectionRequestsNeuron. Tests: the ConnectionsConformance kit loses ConnectionsSubject.Index and gains an AddConnection seam (its two index-backed facts, :74-90, are rewritten); LocalConnectionsConformance and ConnectionNeuronFacts register the catalog and the legacy reader instead of the two stubs (ConnectionNeuronFacts.cs:78-102 deleted); add ConnectionCatalogFacts: listed after Idle, removed on disconnect, adopt listed disconnected, a fact that changes nothing writes no revision, a re-activated catalog catches up from its cursor, a 0.18 fixture ({Pending,Revision,Order} + index {Keys}) seeds rows in the stored order, an unreadable connection seeds as failing, a pending request is cleared by connect, ListInOrder makes one neuron call.
  3. 3. digitalbrain, release N. Identity rules once, no persisted change: add Model/* (Outcome, Refusal, 9 facts, BrainDirectory, AccountBook, Session, SessionBook), BoundIdentity, BoundSessions. InMemoryIdentityDirectory becomes the in-memory store over the model; InMemoryIdentity keeps its public surface (constructor, Declarations, Descriptor, SignIn, AddSignInHandler, OnCall; FakeIdentity derives from it) and delegates to BoundIdentity / BoundSessions. DirectoryNeuron's bodies call the model through DirectoryState.Brain / With and emit typed facts; DirectoryChanged stays declared (old journals name its alias) and is no longer emitted; PrincipalRow and BrainOperation are replaced by the model's PrincipalRecord and BrainCreation with the same JSON members. Correct the comments at IIdentityDirectoryPort.cs:52, IIdentity.cs:55 and IGrants.cs:17. Tests: add BrainDirectoryFacts, AccountBookFacts, SessionBookFacts (no runtime); add four IdentityConformance facts written like the existing Refused helper (a non-owner's add does not happen, a non-owner's removal does not happen, a Once grant allows once, a creation operation id is scoped to its account); LocalIdentityConformance and LocalOwnerFacts.cs:17,37 use BoundIdentity / BoundSessions; delete PortIdentity / PortGrants / PortSessions (:94-208). The kit runs on InMemory, Fake, Local over TestOS and the Orleans capability.
  4. 4. digitalbrain, release N. Sessions leave the directory: add ISessionStore + SessionsNeuron at system/identity.sessions/main holding SessionBook (hash, brain, principal, expiry; expired entries dropped on every Start). LocalDirectoryPort.Issue / End / Validate hash the id, use the store, and read membership from the directory. Delete DirectoryState.Sessions, SessionRow and IDirectory.Issue / End / Validate. Sessions issued before the upgrade stop validating: one sign-in on a local host. Tests: Issue appends one entry at system/identity.sessions/main and none at system/identity.directory/main; the inspected state holds no session id; an expired session is gone after the next Issue; the kit's five session facts stay green (LocalIdentityConformance registers SessionsNeuron and its ResolveNeuron learns ISessionStore).
  5. 5. digitalbrain, the release that carries R07. CapabilityHosting.cs:18-22,32-41 and CapabilityRegistration.cs:37-38,55-57 construct BoundIdentity / BoundSessions; delete IdentityCapability, GrantsCapability, SessionsCapability. Tests: retarget SecretsIdentityCapabilityConformance.cs:60-66, SecretsIdentityCapabilityFacts.cs:65-66,86-87,112-113 and SessionsCapabilityFacts.cs:15,36. os at that bump: IdentityCapabilityKits.cs:57,62-63 (3 lines). If R07 slips, steps 1-4 and 6 still ship; the forwarders stay one release longer.
  6. 6. os, one bump to release N (a production deploy). It cannot be partial for the catalog: with os's own two neurons still present the host fails at startup on a duplicate neuron type. Nine connector states become records and keep [GenerateSerializer] / [Id] (35 Snapshot.X = lines; Gmail's SaveGmailStateAsync takes Func<GmailState, GmailState> and the 26 assignment lines in its 21 call sites become with-expressions; 4 AcceptGrantAsync overrides; Gmail's AcceptGrant override loses Copy() and Restore; 2 test initializers of Secrets). Delete os ConnectionIndexNeuron (45), ConnectionRequestsNeuron (111) and the fact at IdentityRulesFacts.cs:71-75; register an IConnectorOffers over ConnectorCatalog + CapabilityRegistry; move GmailWebhook.cs:29 and AuthorityNeurons.cs:32 to the catalog; remove three rows from MovedTypeAliasFacts.cs (:73,75,76) and MovedTypes.json (:95,105,110). Add one persisted-state fixture fact per connector state with own fields, and an identity kit over os's own DirectoryPort; what it fails is the real drift list and is recorded per fact. Gate: live aspire start, connect, restart, list, resolve storage through the broker, check that an existing brain's catalog seeded from its index. The owner may split it into two framework releases and two bumps (states first, catalog second).
  7. 7. digitalbrain release N+1, then os. Remove the one-release aids: the Snapshot / PublishAsync / Commit forwards (os: 157 Snapshot renames, 6 PublishAsync, 1 Commit, mechanical), LegacyConnectionIndexNeuron and the seed, DirectoryChanged. Delete the four surrogates (120 lines) only after an os integration run without them; os updates ContractManifest.json:47,49,50,165 and MovedTypes.json:61,76,131,716 in that bump and SurrogateRoundTripFacts loses four cases.

What breaks

  • Runtime.Orleans public classes deleted (step 5): IdentityCapability, GrantsCapability, SessionsCapability. digitalbrain src: CapabilityHosting.cs:18-41 and CapabilityRegistration.cs:37-38,55-57; digitalbrain tests: 3 files incl. two Assert.IsType lines (SecretsIdentityCapabilityFacts.cs:112-113); os: 1 test file (IdentityCapabilityKits.cs:57,62-63) and 1 comment. AddIdentityCapability<TPort>() keeps its signature.
  • Identity.Local public types deleted: SessionRow, PrincipalRow, BrainOperation, IDirectory.Issue / End / Validate, the DirectoryState.Sessions member. Callers outside DirectoryNeuron.cs and LocalDirectoryPort.cs: 0 in both repos. IDirectory, DirectoryNeuron, DirectoryState, LocalDirectoryPort and all four AddLocalIdentity overloads stay (7 digitalbrain callers, 0 in os).
  • Unchanged on purpose: IIdentityDirectoryPort (os implements it 3 times), IIdentitySessionPort (os once), IIdentity / IGrants / ISessions, the persisted DirectoryState JSON apart from Sessions.
  • InMemoryIdentity / InMemoryIdentityDirectory keep their public surface and change behaviour: a non-owner's EnsureMember, RemoveMember, RemovePrincipalMember throw UnauthorizedAccessException (succeeded before); a Once grant is spent; a brain-creation operation id is scoped to its account. os runs 13 app-fact files on InMemoryIdentity through AppBaseline; any that changes membership as a non-owner (the default caller is LocalOwner) must fix its set-up. Not measured which.
  • IConnectionIndex deleted: os 6 files (GmailWebhook.cs:29, AuthorityNeurons.cs:32, its own index and requests neurons, IdentityRulesFacts.cs:75, MovedTypeAliasFacts.cs:75); digitalbrain src 6 files, tests 2. IConnectionCatalogUpdates deleted: os 2 files, digitalbrain src 3, tests 2. IConnectionWriteFaults deleted: 0 implementers, 1 os alias row. os also removes MovedTypes.json rows :95,105,110.
  • Testing kit: ConnectionsSubject.Index (ConnectionsConformance.cs:20) is replaced by an AddConnection seam; 1 user (LocalConnectionsConformance), 0 in os.
  • os cannot take any framework version that contains ConnectionCatalogNeuron without deleting its own ConnectionIndexNeuron and ConnectionRequestsNeuron in the same bump: CapabilityHosting.cs:128-132 hosts the Capabilities.Connections assembly wherever an ICapabilityConnections is registered, and NeuronTypeRegistry.cs:83-86 fails startup on a duplicate type name.
  • ConnectionState becomes a record with init members; Secrets is IReadOnlyDictionary, SharedInto is IReadOnlyList: 9 os subclasses, 35 Snapshot.X = lines in 7 connectors, 26 next.X = lines inside Gmail's 21 SaveGmailStateAsync lambdas, 2 os tests that assign Secrets = new(...) (GmailPolicyFacts.cs:47, SalesforcePolicyFacts.cs:38); in digitalbrain 2 test subclasses and ConnectionKeysFacts.cs:27. os ContractManifest.json:48 pins the member order: keep it.
  • ConnectionNeuron<T>: Restore deleted (1 os call, GmailNeuron.cs:57); AcceptGrantAsync takes and returns the state (4 os overrides: Drive, Gmail, GitHub, Salesforce); AcceptGrant stays virtual; Snapshot (157 os references), PublishAsync (6 os calls) and Commit (1 os call, SalesforceNeuron.cs:107) keep compiling for one release and go in step 7.
  • Signals: new connection.adopted / shared / unshared and 9 identity.* facts; connection.connected / expired / failing gain an optional ProbedAt; Adopt and Import-as-disconnected emit connection.adopted instead of connection.disconnected (0 subscribers of either); Identity.Local stops emitting identity.directory-changed (0 subscribers).
  • Persisted: {brain}/integrations.accounts/main gains Rows and Seeded (additive; seeded once from {brain}/connection.index/main, os's index state included); a new state at system/identity.sessions/main; DirectoryState.Sessions is dropped at the next directory save, so sessions issued before the upgrade stop validating; connection states keep their JSON.
  • Behaviour: the catalog is eventually consistent with a connection (one delivery behind); an unreadable connection seeds as failing, not connected; a pending request disappears when a connection of its kind connects; a token refresh no longer publishes ConnectionsChanged.
  • Step 7 only: surrogate aliases connection.view, connection.oauth-grant, connection.probe-result, brain.state removed; os baselines ContractManifest.json:47,49,50,165 and MovedTypes.json:61,76,131,716 record them and must be updated.

Risks

  • The projection is only as reliable as the kernel's delivery, and the production runtime has no reconcile on silo start and no re-announce (synapse-engine map 5.2). A lost doorbell heals when the catalog re-activates or the next subscribed fact arrives: after a process crash that is the next read; after a swallowed send failure on a live silo the row stays stale until the next fact. The window is narrower than today's three hand-sequenced writes, not gone, until the kernel nudge move lands.
  • One direct call remains (IConnectionCatalog.Subscribed, once per connection activation) because a brain-wide synapse reads from where it was created (ISynapses.cs:25). If a code path ever saves a connection fact without it, the catalog silently lacks that row.
  • Not verified: whether an activating catalog pulls pending facts before it serves its first read. If it does not, the first listing after a restart can be one fact behind.
  • The os bump touches all nine connectors in one production deploy. The edits are mechanical, but each connector's persisted state must be proven to read back (one fixture fact per state with own fields).
  • Nothing was compiled. Relied on: 'with' on a type parameter constrained to a record; a sealed ToString on a non-sealed record; System.Text.Json reading IReadOnlyList<string> and the retyped secrets converter under RuntimeProtocol.Json. The existing fixture test shows the last two.
  • Clearing pending requests by connector kind is a behaviour choice. If the UI ties a request to a connection id, clear by id.
  • os connector tests that drive a connection under TestOS must register ConnectionCatalogNeuron and, for one release, LegacyConnectionIndexNeuron. Count not measured.
  • The seed makes one Read per indexed connection inside the catalog's activation, which activates every connection neuron of that brain once per brain at upgrade.
  • Step 5 depends on R07. If R07's Caller differs from the four CapabilityCaller members the model reads (principal, brain, app, conversation), OwnedBy and Authorize need a small rewrite.
  • The four surrogates are 'dead' on the evidence of two independent text searches, not a proof; an Orleans codec failure shows only at run time. That is why they go last, behind an os integration run.

Proof it worked

Counts that must read 0 in digitalbrain src: the text 'Context.State.Save(next, [])' (1 today), assignments through Snapshot or State in Capabilities.Connections, 'IConnectionIndex', 'IConnectionCatalogUpdates', 'IConnectionWriteFaults', 'RequireOwner' and 'consumesOnce' outside Model/, 'new DirectoryChanged', and after step 5 'Stamp(PlatformCaller)' under Capabilities/Identity (23 today). Tests: the IdentityConformance kit with four added facts green on InMemoryIdentity, FakeIdentity, Local over TestOS and the Orleans capability; BrainDirectoryFacts / AccountBookFacts / SessionBookFacts run with no runtime; PersistedStateFacts unchanged and green; AFailedSaveLeavesTheConnectionUntouched; ConnectionCatalogFacts including the 0.18 seed fixture, catch-up after re-activation and 'an unchanged fact writes no revision'. Measurements: a counting resolver shows ListInOrder makes 1 neuron call for N connections (2 + N today); a journal assertion shows Issue appends one entry at system/identity.sessions/main and none at system/identity.directory/main, and the inspected session state contains no string starting with 'session-'; a Connect followed by a token refresh publishes ConnectionsChanged once, not twice. os gate: live aspire start, connect, restart, list, resolve storage through the broker, an existing brain's catalog seeded from its index; the new kit over os's DirectoryPort reports its result per fact.

Your decision

Three. (1) Per-brain storage for Identity.Local: one BrainDirectory neuron per brain in that brain's journal, as the designer proposed. It needs a one-way persisted migration, turns CreateBrain / EnsureBrain / EnsureMember from one atomic entry into 2-3 writes in two journals, and maintains principal -> brains by hand because synapses are same-brain only. I cut it; take it only if the local kit is meant to become os's directory. (2) Does os run its rules through BrainDirectory / AccountBook, or keep its own 518-line directory? Converging needs os's Grant (it has an id) aligned with the framework record; the new kit over os's DirectoryPort gives the drift list first. (3) Pending requests are cleared by connector kind when a connection of that kind connects; say so, or clear by connection id.

Review notes on this proposal

Quote check. All 4 designer before-snippets (9 line ranges in 6 files) were re-read against HEAD and are verbatim at the stated lines; nothing invented. Two were replaced because the corrected design no longer changes what they showed: pair 1 now quotes DirectoryNeuron.cs:505-523 and InMemoryIdentity.cs:236-250, pair 2 quotes IdentityCapability.cs:23,133-150 and tests/DigitalBrain.Identity.Local.Tests.Unit/LocalIdentityConformance.cs:132-136, all copied from the files. Pairs 3 and 4 keep the designer's text unchanged. Elisions are '// ...' lines only.

Corrections the reviewer made

  • Cut the per-brain split of the local directory (designer step 4: BrainDirectoryNeuron, AccountsNeuron, per-session neurons, LocalIdentity, DirectorySplit) from the committed steps and made it an owner decision. It re-created the defect the move removes from connections (a principal -> brains read model maintained by hand with a crash window, because synapses are same-brain only), turned one atomic entry into 2-3 writes in two journals, needed a one-way migration and a neuron plus reminder per session, for a kit os does not use (0 AddLocalIdentity calls). Identity now ships with no persisted change except sessions.
  • Reversed the EnsureMember non-owner behaviour: the model refuses and both hosts throw UnauthorizedAccessException, as production does (os DirectoryPort.cs:77-81,152-159) and as the local neuron does today. The designer returned null to match a doc comment and then listed a possible os failure as a risk. The comments are corrected instead; the 'bug' B9 became a doc defect.
  • Found that os does not run the identity kit against its own directory (os IdentityCapabilityKits.cs:57-63 uses the framework's InMemoryIdentityDirectory). Removed the designer's risk 'new facts may fail against os's DirectoryPort and block the bump', added the gap to bugsFixed and an os kit to step 6.
  • Removed every [Obsolete] shim: both repos set TreatWarningsAsErrors (Directory.Build.props:8), so an obsolete member fails its callers' build. Snapshot, PublishAsync and Commit stay as plain forwards for one release.
  • Dropped the hosting change to INeuronProxies and the deletion of LocalDirectoryPort / LocalIdentityHosting: TestOS has no INeuronProxies (Fakes.cs:452 is a hand-filled dictionary) and the vault needs the resolver too. LocalConnections keeps its constructor.
  • Fixed the catalog seed: it ran through Defer with a comment saying 'after the turn', but deferred work is drained inside the same grain call (NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:137-148) and Subscribed() is fully interleaved, so a reaction could race the seed. The seed now runs inside OnActivate; it does not deadlock because IConnection.Read is [ReadOnlyCall].
  • Fixed no-op detection in the catalog: Put always produced a new array, so every fact wrote a revision. Put / Without / Resolved now return this when nothing changes and Apply uses ReferenceEquals. Added the resulting fix to bugsFixed (a token refresh publishes ConnectionsChanged today).
  • Fixed Bind: the designer's check on view.AuthMethodId admitted a disconnected connection; it now checks the catalog row, as it checks the index today.
  • Reshaped IConnectorOffers (Declared, Offered, ProvidesOf) so os keeps listing declared-but-not-offered connectors with Available = false and keeps skipping connections of uncomposed connectors.
  • Replaced ImmutableDictionary / ImmutableArray on persisted records with IReadOnlyDictionary / IReadOnlyList and arrays; added a sealed redacting ToString to the ConnectionState record; kept AcceptGrant virtual; kept [GenerateSerializer] / [Id] on the nine os state types and the member order os ContractManifest.json:48 pins.
  • Fixed the Orleans registration snippet: one factory typed Func<..., BoundIdentity> broke IsDeniedIdentityFallback (CapabilityHosting.cs:68-89); the two typed factory fields stay. Added the missed caller CapabilityRegistration.cs:37-38,55-57.
  • Softened 'the crash window is gone': the production runtime has no reconcile on silo start (synapse-engine map 5.2); the projection heals on catalog re-activation or the next fact. Stated as the first risk.
  • Added the hard release coupling: os cannot take a framework version with the catalog neuron without deleting its own two neurons in the same bump (CapabilityHosting.cs:128-132, NeuronTypeRegistry.cs:83-86).
  • Added what the os census missed: 26 'next.X =' lines in Gmail's 21 SaveGmailStateAsync lambdas, 2 test initializers of Secrets, 13 app-fact files on InMemoryIdentity, MovedTypes.json rows, the ContractManifest.json and MovedTypes.json baselines that pin the four surrogate aliases. Corrected Snapshot references from 155 to 157.
  • Moved the deletion of the four surrogates from step 1 to the last step behind an os integration run; the evidence is two text searches, not a proof, and os baselines record the aliases.
  • Corrected counts: 16 forwarders in IdentityCapability (not 17); six caller-reading members in BoundIdentity (not seven); the test adapters are lines 94-208, 115 lines (not 79-209, 131); 9 typed identity facts (not 13). Noted that 72 of the deleted capability lines are stamp lines R07 already counts.
  • The ConnectionsConformance kit does not 'keep passing': two of its facts call IConnectionIndex.Track. Step 2 now rewrites them behind an AddConnection seam; the legacy index reader is public for one release because TestOS registers neurons one by one.
  • Re-cut code pairs 1 and 2 (their 'before' showed DirectoryState's arrays and LocalDirectoryPort, which the corrected design keeps); pair 1 now shows the drift itself: the local rule beside the in-memory copy with no owner check.
  • Recomputed the ledger: about -540 src lines (est.), about -660 with the surrogates, instead of -850. Dropped the JSON-codec decision and the state-only-save decision from this move (the second is R04's).

Doubts that remain

  • Nothing was compiled or run. The after-code leans on 'with' over a type parameter constrained to a record and on a sealed ToString in a non-sealed record.
  • Whether an activating catalog pulls pending facts before it serves its first read was not verified.
  • The DirectoryNeuron.cs rewrite size (420 lines) and the other additions are estimates; the net of about -540 could be off by 100 lines either way.
  • Which of the 13 os app-fact files on InMemoryIdentity change membership as a non-owner was not measured, nor which os connector tests must register the catalog neuron.
  • DirectoryState.With moves a brain's members and grants to the end of the flat arrays. I found no order dependence across brains, but did not prove it.
  • The claim that the four surrogates have no Orleans-serialized user rests on text searches in both repos.
  • Clearing pending requests by connector kind may not match how the UI uses ConnectionRef.Id.
  • R07's final Caller shape is not fixed; step 5 and the model's OwnedBy follow it.
  • Dropping pre-upgrade local sessions is my call in place of the designer's migration; the owner may want them carried over.

R13 · Units · wave 2

Make unit deployment a durable aggregate with a tenant-free loader

Replace the 882-line SynapseHost singleton (loader refcounts + in-memory per-brain saga + semaphore/channel actor) with a brain-free UnitLoader, a durable UnitDeployment neuron per (brain, sourceKey) that stores the staged build and reacts to approval facts over the durable brain-wide edge, and a (brain, alias) -> BuildRef resolve on every activation, so the process-wide alias registrar and every restore-on-miss site are deleted.

Net
src -1,805 (measured: 1,455 in 11 whole files + 36 of ISourceStore.cs + 275 in replaced ranges + 39 inside SynapseCatalogSource.cs) +1,045 (est.) = about -760 / tests -943 (measured: 575 deleted + 368 rewritten) +865 (est.) = about -80 / os -26 (measured) +45 (est.) = about +20
Effort
L (1-2 weeks, nearer two) - step 3 is one PR that moves the saga and both runtimes' activation paths together, about 20 test files are deleted, rewritten or retargeted, there is a lazy data migration to rehearse against production state, and it ends in an os bump that is a production deploy.
Depends on
R03 (removes the gate-path restore site and its TestOS twin, InstalledGates and InstalledGateIndex; without it one extra site is rewritten in step 3)
R05 (soft: UnitDeployment relies on the durable brain-wide edge exactly as InstallsNeuron does today; it works before R05 and gets simpler after)
R14 (soft: removes the remaining sync-over-async resolve in TestOS.Host)
Review confidence
medium

What is wrong today

src/DigitalBrain.Runtime.Synapses/Host/SynapseHost.cs is 882 lines (wc -l) doing three jobs: a process-level ALC refcount (5 NoInlining helpers, 3 more in SynapseLoader.cs), a per-brain deployment saga whose whole state (Entry {Live, Staged, Rebind}, Pending.Waiting, lines 859-881) is memory only, and a hand-written actor (one SemaphoreSlim with 7 WaitAsync + 7 Release sites, a Channel, a Drain loop, listeners registered only from Deploy, line 140). The only durable trace of a stage is SourcesNeuron.cs:59 Save(State, new Staged(...)) - state unchanged - so the staged bytes, plan ids and proposed bindings die with the process while the PendingInstall survives. The alias registry is process-wide (ISynapseRegistrar.cs:3-6; OrleansSynapseRegistrar.cs:35-37 replaces catalog entry and state type by alias for every brain) while the workflow is per brain; that costs SourceUsedElsewhere (5 call sites), ActivateOnce, UndoActivation and Unwind, and still lets brain A's deploy take the functions away from brain B's running StateNeuron (StateNeuron.cs:60-66 returns []). Because the catalog is a cache nobody hydrates, restore-on-miss exists in NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:44-45, GateRunner.cs:109-124, InstallsNeuron.cs:139-149 (detached, with a proof dictionary and a one-way callback to dodge the semaphore), sync-over-async in TestOS.cs:419, TestGates.cs:117 and a poke in os SynapseStudioNeuron.cs:144-150. Bindings are stored twice (SourceRecord.Bindings, InstallRecord.Bindings) and synchronised by ResolveRebind (SynapseHost.cs:792-833). The two runtimes also disagree on a swap: TestSynapseRegistrar.cs:19 drops the hosts of the alias for every brain and key, Orleans reloads only {brain}/{alias}/default (OrleansSynapseRegistrar.cs:66-71).

Design principle

An aggregate owns its invariants and its state durably, and a tenant boundary is not a parameter threaded through process singletons: today the deployment aggregate is smeared over one singleton's memory, two neurons and six in-memory tables, and a tenant-level workflow shares a class and a lock with a process-level resource manager. The target gives each fact one owner - loaded code by content (UnitLoader, no BrainId), one source's live/staged build and asks (UnitDeployment neuron), alias ownership per brain (the sources directory), consent and bindings (InstallsNeuron) - and lets them talk through ordinary neuron calls and durable signals.

Defects this removes

  • All by reading, not executed; confidence in brackets.
  • D1 [0.8] Cross-brain bleed: brain A deploying a new hash replaces the alias's catalog entry and state type for every brain (OrleansSynapseRegistrar.cs:35-37); brain B's active StateNeuron no longer finds its type and Synapses() returns [] (StateNeuron.cs:60-66), so its gates, reactions and views stop silently; after reactivation B runs code it never approved. Two different sources of two brains that declare the same alias collide the same way. Fixed for code and state; signal types wait for step 5.
  • D2 [0.65] A staged update is lost on restart: SourcesNeuron.cs:59 saves the unchanged state and the staged build lives in entry.Staged (SynapseHost.cs:527). The surviving PendingInstall is then completed against the old live catalog: InstallsNeuron.cs:151-160 with InstallsState.cs:32-39 records the NEW needs hash with the OLD build's schedules and publishes Installed, while the approved code is gone.
  • D3 [0.7] The handlers that apply Installed/InstallDenied to a stage or a rebind are registered only from Deploy (SynapseHost.cs:140, 706-730); Adopt (99-118) does not register them, and a waiting Rebind (859-872) is memory only.
  • D4 [0.7] Alias ownership is checked against memory only (SynapseHost.cs:413-429): after a restart entries is empty until Adopt, so a second source can claim an alias of a not-yet-restored source; SynapseRestorer.cs:45 then restores whichever record comes first.
  • D5 [0.6] Only key 'default' of the deploying brain is reloaded on a swap (OrleansSynapseRegistrar.cs:66-71) although descriptors carry a Key (SynapseShape.cs:36); a keyed state neuron keeps a replaced TState and silently has no functions until idle. TestOS drops every host of the alias instead (TestSynapseRegistrar.cs:19), so no simulator test can see it.
  • D6 [0.6] Another silo never learns of an update: the registrar mutates the local process only and restore runs only on an alias miss (SynapseRestorer.cs:39-42). After R13 every activation reads the brain's durable deployment; an already active grain on another silo converges through the LiveBindings hint or when idle.
  • D7 [0.9, cost] List holds the process-wide gate, reads every stored record with its bytes and re-walks each PE on every call (SynapseHost.cs:256, 271-276).
  • D8 [0.7] os DebuggerReadsNeuron.cs:84-86 builds a brain's topology from catalog.Current.States.Keys, which is every alias loaded in the process, across brains.
  • D9 [0.7] After a restart, an update that changes the needs of an installed alias skips staging: a source not yet adopted has no entry, changed requires entry is not null (SynapseHost.cs:178), so Deploy takes GoLive (194), the new code is live before the person answers, and Store (455) overwrites the old record, so a denial has nothing to fall back to.

The deployment aggregate: from a private class in a singleton's memory to a durable, pure state record

Today 'staged' is a property on a private class in a singleton, and the only durable trace saves the unchanged state. After, the staged build with its bytes and plan ids is the neuron's state, and the seven transitions are pure functions testable without a runtime. Rebind disappears: a waiting rebind's bindings already live in PendingInstall.Bindings. The record + parameterless constructor shape is the one SourcesState uses today (SourcesNeuron.cs:39-43).

The workflow: semaphore + channel + silo-local listener becomes one neuron reacting to durable facts

The turn replaces the semaphore; the durable brain-wide edge (what InstallsNeuron.cs:209 already uses for ConfirmationResolved) replaces the channel, the Drain loop and the listener only Deploy registers. Save-before-ask is the whole trick: the staged build is durable before the person is asked, and the installs neuron's read-only calls interleave with this turn instead of deadlocking on a lock. Three holes of the first draft are closed here: the null stage after the installs, the ready stage left by a crash, and dropped aliases retired too late for their hooks.

Restore-on-miss sites and the proof callback become one awaited resolve

The detached restore existed only because the host's lock is a SemaphoreSlim, which nothing can interleave with. With the deployment as a neuron the same question is two [ReadOnlyCall] reads the framework already interleaves. Drain() (NeuronHostGrainBase.cs:341-344) already runs in the finally of every turn, so the leave check needs no new hook. The gate-path site is deleted by R03; the allow-list re-check on restore moves into loader.Acquire, the only door from bytes to an Assembly.

Process-wide alias registrar plus cross-brain compensation becomes a content-addressed loader with no BrainId

Refcounting by a handle held per activation replaces Shared.Brains, Shared.Activated, ActivateOnce, SourceUsedElsewhere, UndoActivation and Unwind: a build is loaded while something runs it and for no other reason. Looking the catalog up by the state record's own Type is what makes two brains on two hashes of one alias independent. The per-turn read is kept (not bind-once) so the static catalog TestOS mutates still reaches active neurons and LiveCatalogFacts stays green; the other three AliasIn(_catalog.Current) reads (StateNeuron.cs:258, 295, 360) use _alias.

Steps, each shippable

  1. 0. Prerequisite R03 (delete the two-phase gate): it removes restore site GateRunner.cs:109-124, its TestOS twin TestGates.cs:117, InstalledGates, InstalledGateIndex and GateRunner.cs:237. If R03 has not landed, step 3 rewrites the gate-path site as one UnitResolver.Resolve per installed alias; nothing else changes.
  2. 1. digitalbrain, additive, no behaviour change, patch release: add Deployment.cs with DeploymentStateFacts (propose, approve all, approve one of two, deny by plan id, deny of a superseded plan ignored, promote, refuse, retire); add UnitHandle, IUnitLoader, UnitRefusedException and UnitLoader beside SynapseLoader with UnitLoaderFacts (two acquires share one context; release at zero raises Unloading and unloads; a disallowed image is refused before any load; Lingering empties after a forced GC in the test). Add the new type names to AllowedApis.RefusedDigitalBrainTypes (AllowedApis.cs:58) and extend tests/DigitalBrain.Architecture.Tests.Unit/UnitSurfaceFacts.cs. Nothing is wired; no public removal; PublicAPI.Unshipped.txt updated.
  3. 2. digitalbrain, additive, patch release, removes the silent half of D1 and D5: add ISynapseCatalogSource.Of(Type) (SynapseCatalogSource is its only implementer in both repos), implemented in today's SynapseCatalogSource with a ConditionalWeakTable<Assembly, SynapseCatalog> filled in Merge/Replace for collectible assemblies; StateNeuron fixes its alias at construction and reads Of(typeof(TState)) per turn (4 sites). LiveCatalogFacts stays green unchanged. New fact in DigitalBrain.Runtime.Orleans.Tests.Integration: AnActivationKeepsItsOwnFunctionsWhenItsAliasIsReplaced (brain A deploys a new hash; brain B's active neuron and a keyed neuron of A still gate and react). It must be a silo fact: TestOS drops every host of the alias (TestSynapseRegistrar.cs:19) and cannot show the defect. os may bump or skip.

Commit A: UnitDeployment, the ISources directory with legacy drain, UnitResolver, LiveBindings, UnitDeployer : ISynapseHost (+Source), IInstalls.Rewire; InstallsNeuron resolves through UnitResolver.
